A Change of Luck

by fleurjaune (fleur_jaune)

Summary

Shadow Moth is thrown to be confronted by a woman in Chat Noir's place. It shouldn't really make a difference in his quest but he finds himself strangely drawn to her.

Meanwhile Nathalie Sancoeur is having a well deserved breakdown.

Chapter 1: Gabriel I

A flash of black catches the corner of his eye and Shadow Moth swiftly follows after. Perhaps leaving the lair today, leaving Nathalie safely at home while he ventures out to better control his sentimonster might yet pay dividends.

Yet the slight figure in the alley below him, well for a start isn’t quite so slight. Taller than Chat Noir by a least a head and though her figure is neat it’s undeniably feminine, with that black leather showcasing the curve of her behind despite her slim legs, and an even slimmer waist.

The hair is still just as wild as usual, if not wilder, but it’s a dark mass hanging just below her chin just on the threshold between wavy and curly.

He’s always preferred a sleeker look himself yet he can’t deny that despite what he’s fighting for a flurry of excitement stirs in his belly. This might be a chance for a little bit of fun. The little bit of amusement he needs in this long slog in pursuit of the Miraculous.

It changes things, having an adult opponent.

Careful that his descent is silent he creeps behind in pursuit of her, as she frantically seems to be looking for someone else. Ladybug he supposes. It’s odd that she’s not gone to fight his champions by herself but perhaps this Black Cat is a little more reticent than her predecessor.

Finally he’s close enough that he’s able to catch her with an arm around her waist, pulling her back against him. The warmth of her body can be felt despite the layers of clothes between them. Though hers admittedly do little to obscure the shape of her body.

Her hand immediately closes over his arm, and with a sharp press that doesn’t quite tear through his sleeve shows her claws. “Let go of me.”

He grabs her other arm to stop anything else she might do and inclines his head to speak directly into one of the green rimmed black cat ears extending up from her mask, “but my dear, we’ve only started to get acquainted.”

From her startle he’s right that they’re not just for decoration.

Unfortunately enough he can’t reach her ring without releasing one of his hands and chancing her fleeing, the way that every stiff muscle pressed up against him betrays that she wants to.

“Shadow Moth,” she swallows, “I didn’t think friendship was something you were particularly interested in.”

He pulls her a little closer. “A man can get lonely.”

Despite a little hitch in her breath that he treasures there’s acid in her sneer as she says, “What, without Mayura?”

He allows himself a theatrical sigh, and ignores the very real emotion below it. Terrifying as seeing Nathalie put herself in harm’s way was he can’t deny that she’d felt even more like a bulwark of support preventing him from giving up once she’d made this her fight too by donning that Miraculous.

“I do seem to have a vacancy for a partner,” he nuzzles in behind her ear and is rewarded with a shiver against him, “interested in filling it?”

She inhales sharply and then suddenly his foot distracts him a burst of agony as she drives her heel into it, and the shock apparently loosens his hold enough she manages to jab her elbow back between his ribs.

Swearing he releases her, apparently just in time as her heel swings up between his legs.

“And I thought we were getting on so well,” he complains, “but you’re a vicious little thing aren’t you? What a pity. ”

That part he does actually mean. There’s a certain emptiness inside at knowing that Emilie and Nathalie are depending on him to save them while this woman prances around with half of what he needs and won’t give it to him.

It’s enough to extinguish any embarrassment he might have felt about his behaviour towards her. He has to fight her. This is no worse and seems to throw her off, and there’s a definite glee inside him at getting under her skin. Like it’s some sort of payment for the obstacle she represents to him.

She turns back to him, hissing, “I’ve no interest in a man who finds woman interchangeable.”

“Hmm,” the temptation to antagonise her is irresistible, “but you and my dear Mayura have such a vital difference. Only one of you has the ring I need.”

She steps back, “I promised Chat Noir to keep it from you.”

“Where is he anyway? Not that I’m not feline,” he grins, “quite delighted with the swap, but why would you promise him that. Aren’t you a little old to be a fan?”

“That’s none of your business,” her hands curl, preparing to claw at him again, “and if I were you I’d run away.”

Playing along with her little game, he holds out his hands, “And why’s that?”

With a smirk she stalks towards him as their little game of predator and prey suddenly flips, “Because as you so clearly started I’m an adult, so,” she reaches out towards his chest, “I’m not limited with the number of times I can use cataclysm.”

Catching her wrist he stops her hand, “much as I’d enjoy your hands on me in other circumstances I’m sure you’ll understand if I stop you right there.”

They both stare at each other weighing up their opponent. Shadow Moth is larger and empowered by both the Butterfly and the Peacock, but both of those expect their wielder to fight from a distance, unlike the natural destructiveness of the Cat.

He has a few tricks up his sleeve. It’s not like Gabriel had no martial training before donning a Miraculous but something in the posture of the woman opposite him looks trained like Chat Noir never had.

A whack to the side of his head sends his ears ringing, and reminds him of the one definite advantage that…whatever her name is has over him.

She has a partner.

A partner with a yo-yo hard enough to leave a decent bruise at the side of his head that leaves him with a thudding pressure-pain at the side of his head that makes it hard to concentrate.

“Get his Miraculous,” that infernal bug shouts. “It’s two of us on one!”

But the Cat hesitates and he can’t read what exactly is going on behind those inhuman green eyes, but there’s something.

Then she calls up to Ladybug. “You need to stop the akuma. I’ll handle him.”

“But,”

A thunder cloud crosses the woman’s face, as she commands the girl, “Go. He’s laying waste to the city.”

Ladybug stumbles, clearly not used to her partner telling her what to do, and her eyes flick between them before her eyebrows meet in determination and she nods and runs off.

“I’m flattered you want to be alone with me,” he smirks back at her.

She crosses her arms, apparently unimpressed, maintaining a neutral enough façade as she drones. “Maybe I’m just worried about you hitting on the teenager.”

That does unsettle him a bit. Not least as her monotone doesn’t quite work to hide the disquiet in her voice; like she believes he could do it. Shadow Moth is supposed to be the repository of all Gabriel’s darkest deeds and thoughts, the textbook villain who shares none of Gabriel’s qualms. But it’s all for a purpose. All for Emilie, and intensified by the need to make Nathalie’s endangering of herself for his wife and his cause worth it.

An accusation like that is a tarnish indeed on his shining purity of purpose.

“There are depths of villainy too deep even for me. Besides, how could I ignore someone as purrfect as you.”

She massages her temples, but there’s a flush to her cheeks. “Please don’t do that. It’s bad enough coming from Chat Noir.”

“Someone has to provide the lack in his absence.”

With a shake of her head she sighs, “you really are incorrigible.” Then looking up at the horizon she says, “Go home Shadow Moth.”

“I thought you were going to handle me?”

He casts his eye over once more compartmentalising both his appreciation and his assessment of her likely abilities. There’s a strange lack of fight in her. Which is even odder given how she does in fact seem to be genuinely angry.

“That seems dangerous for of us. Either of us could lose a Miraculous. I’m new to this after all, and you’re quite given to…failure.” Her voice is a knife wrapped in velvet.

The world seems to slow down for a minute, his heartbeat thuds dully in his chest, the damage Ladybug did to his head and the Cat to his foot pulsates in slow waves of hurt, and he’s suddenly desperate for Nathalie as the void of her absence feels almost physical.

She would know what to say in response. Would have some cutting remark ready to shut her down as she stood between them the way she had with reporters after Emilie’s, after they had to put Emilie in the casket.

Gabriel has always been inadequate in that regard. A funny enough thing when how usually he has no problem cutting anyone down to size. But all they have to do is mention Emilie, intimate her disappearance is his fault and he's drowning in guilt and can't find his way to the surface.

Because it is his fault really. He'd helped Emilie to find the Miraculous, encouraged her ideas about it, and then when she'd got sick he hadn’t stopped her. A better husband would have forced the issue, wouldn’t have let her drive herself almost to death but Gabriel had been unable to refuse her, to provoke that sad disappointment in Emilie’s eyes.

It’s a lesson he’s learnt from, and bears in mind everytime Adrien looks up with him with his oh-so-beseeching eyes.

And now he’s had all of that thrown back in his face by a woman who he’s been flirting with instead of fighting with.

He really is a terrible husband.

“That’s a low blow.” He mutters.

“I’m sorry,” she says and despite all odds he believes it, as she places her hand on his and seems to suck out his stress and self-recrimination with her gentle touch. It’s a feminine thing he thinks. Both Emilie and Nathalie have that talent. To make it all feel all right so long as they’re there with him.

But of course she’s not with him.

She’s fighting against him.

And how can she understand his cause with how he started it?

But still she looks at him like Nathalie does, and all he wants to do is to fall to her knees and cling to her thighs and beseech her intercession in his cause because some stupid part at the back of his brain wants to tell him she’d understand.

“Are you?”

“Go home.” She squeezes his hand in reassurance, “and think about you really want to act like this with me.”

Something rears up at him in irritation, that she’d take her heroic morals and pretend that the two of them are close enough to impose them on him. There is nothing Gabriel won’t do for who he loves. Including seducing another woman. “You don’t know me.”

Her hair tumbles round in a shake of her head, “I don’t think this is you. I think you’re a better man than this. What are you doing this for?”

He stumbles back out of her hold as she strikes at the very thing he’s been ignoring in this conversation. Emilie seems to hover in front of him. Just as golden as her portrait. But her eyes aren’t narrowed in that tempest of fury she could muster. Instead they just look down at him sadly like the Virgin herself disappointed in humanity but still ever-ready to intercede on their behalf with her son.

“I can’t tell you that,” he continues to slowly back away from her.

“That’s fair enough I suppose,” she retreats backwards seeming similarly lost.

“Catwoman,” he calls out after her, “I’ll recall my amok. So you just have to deal with my akuma. Since you’ve been so nice to me and all.”

Her eyes widen ever so slightly, that strange green “Thank you. and that’s not my name. Though that does betray a few things on your part. You’re hardly Batman are you?

”I have better luck with women,” the devoted husband chuckles at the comparison to the eternal bachelor. “But what should I call you?”

She smiles and her teeth fairly glow in the dark with a Cheshire cat grin, “You’ll have to discover that. I’m not sure I’m decided.”

Chapter 2: Nathalie I

Nathalie falls to her knees as the transformation falls off. It had been dangerous to go back to the house transformed, but Adrien’s been doing it as Chat Noir all this time with them none the wiser and she’d had no faith in her own ability to walk once she was just Nathalie again.

And indeed her head feels wrapped in cotton wool and her limbs feel bone-tired with a fatigue that’s become all too familiar.

The bed is right there.

But she can’t quite reach it as her limbs refuse to respond and instead leave her tumbling to the floor.

“Hey lady,” Plagg’s tone is laughing ‘til he must see her, “lady, are you ok?”

No. In no way shape or form am I OK.

That’s what she means to say. Instead what she manages is a gasp for air betraying the pain in her ribcage.

“Right you are not OK.” The Kwami floats in front of her oddly hesitant. “Should I call the kid or?”

“No,” she manages to gasp, “he can’t know. Can’t know that I’m,”

Plagg flies right up to her face in something that looks like a threat but doesn’t quite make it over the barrier to being one. “Is that a command?”

“Yes it is,” she says with a calm that she doesn’t feel at all but is mustered from years of professionalism, “As much as you can’t tell anyone but Adrien who I am, you can’t tell him how I am either.”

Concern makes his body seem to curve in on itself. “You’re setting him up for a fall.”

“Do you think I don’t know that?” She tries to ineffectually push herself up and fails. It’s fine. She’ll just lie here for a bit. It’s fine.

Strangely despite the total weakness In every cell in her body she seems to have lost the urge to cough. Maybe her lungs can’t muster the strength to do that either.

Or maybe the Black Cat Miraculous is effecting her damaged self differently to the Peacock. She can’t pretend that she thinks it a good idea to try using another Miraculous after one had already condemned her to weeks of bedrest. There’s a reason that Gabriel chose to wield both the Butterfly and the Peacock himself rather than letting her try the Butterfly, never mind the newly fixed Peacock.

Then again she should have never used the Black Cat Miraculous at all. Should have ran to Gabriel with it the moment Adrien had pressed it into her hands in panic blabbling about Chat Noir not being able to go out with a broken leg and please Nathalie, Ladybug needs help and you can’t tell father.

But Adrien had looked at her with such trust and she’d transformed and suddenly it was like she could breathe clearly since the first time she’d put the Peacock on, and she’d told herself that she’d just use it as a way to get Ladybug’s trust, and then she could present Gabriel with both Miraculous.

And hopefully with the fulfilment of all his dreams in his hands he wouldn’t think to question whatever story she came up with for how exactly she’d got hold of the Black Cat Miraculous in the first place and why she hadn’t told him.

Then ideally in the world he’d create Adrien never would have been Chat Noir and the relationship between father and son could go on, whole and unbroken, and steadied by Emilie’s deft hands.

Plagg broke her out of her thoughts. “Are you seriously just going to lie there because you don’t want the kid to know you’re ill?”

“He knows I’m ill,” she corrects him. “Surely you don’t want him to feel guilty and…”

“Urgh. Try and take the ring back himself. I get it.”

“Besides help’s on its way.”

Gabriel’s just lost a battle. He’ll be here to complain soon enough. It’s almost a routine at this point.

Plagg turns to look at the door. “What? You know something I don’t?”

“Something like that,” she half gasps and half laughs and then as she hears Gabriel on the stairs panic makes her voice harsh, “You have to stay hidden from Mr Agreste. That’s an order.”

“Bossy,” Plagg frowns, “you people used to worship me you know. But I was going to do that anyway.”

“And you can’t tell Adrien anything of what we say here.”

That provokes a grimace. “Gross. You better get me some good cheese if I’m going to have to sit through that mushy stuff. It’s bad enough with the boy.”

“Really not the problem.” She mutters as she hears the door open.

“Nathalie I,” Gabriel starts and then there’s a pause as he must take in her apparent absence, “Nathalie?”

The footsteps echo against the hardwood floor as he abandons any attempt at his usual stoicism. Not that it usually lasts long in here anyway.

Then warm firm hands are round her shoulders, and Gabriel’s staring down at her with naked concern shining from those eyes that betray everything he tries to hide inside.

“Oh my Nathalie what has happened to you?”

“Too weak.” She manages back, while she tries to keep a blush off her face as her body becomes uncomfortably warm as his hands move down her to better pick her up, and the memory of Shadow Moth pressed up against her back, his larger size meaning he almost seemed to encircle her, suddenly flares back to her mind.

He frowns, unware of her traitorous thoughts, and how her insides squirm as he lifts her onto the bed.

“Where’s your exoskeleton?”

“Thought I was OK without it. Apparently I was wrong,” she attempts to smile.

It doesn’t bring any levity to Gabriel’s face, “Nathalie.” His forehead drops against hers and she’s pinned under his fierce stare as that passion and determination he uses to fuel himself in his fight to save Emilie is suddenly turned on her, “you have to wear it. You’re the only person I can rely on. I need you. And that means I need you to look after yourself.”

Feeling stupid, like his regard has melted her brain with its heat, she reminds him, “I know. I’ll always be there for you Gabriel.”

“Do it for yourself too,” a sadly familiar furrow forms in his forehead, “what I’ve let happen to you is the only part of this I regret.”

His hand brushes against her cheekbone, so very soft despite the roughness and callouses of his fingers, leaving tingles in its wake.

A heat coils inside her as she imagines the leather of Shadow Moth’s gloves in its place. Of what he might have done had she not stopped him.

Taken the ring, she reminds herself, that’s what all this is for.

Emilie is the woman he loves. She’s the only one he’d want to hold like that. Nathalie as the Black Cat was just…just a ploy.

“It’ll be worth it when you win.” She reminds him.

Gabriel sighs and sits up. “Sometimes I wonder when that will be, and in the meantime everything else is spiralling out of its place. Your health. The business. Adrien’s health. He’s broken his leg playing rugby and I didn’t even know he played rugby. How can I keep him safe when I don’t know what he’s doing or where he is?”

“You’re a busy man. You can’t be expected to watch Adrien’s location all the time. That’s my job. And it’s my fault that I never checked what sports Adrien was doing at school.”

Even if she’s fairly sure that there was no rugby involved at all, given that would have been more likely to result in a broken collarbone or nose rather than a leg, and more likely Adrien had probably had some misfortune as Chat Noir after the Ladybug cure had already done its job and couldn’t help him.

With a shake of his head Gabriel dismisses her apology. “You’re still not well. Though I will be demanding his school excuses him from anything similar in future. I’ve had to cancel two photoshoots. Though admittedly between you and Adrien I’m starting to think I should just give in and design a line of crutches and supports.”

“It would be a new market sir.”

“Hmm. Perhaps,” he sighs again, “but this trying and failing. It’s all getting so monotonous Nathalie. Though a new piece has entered the board.”

The world slows down as Nathalie’s mind frantically draws up a flowchart of the repercussions of faking that she has no idea what he’s talking about, or pretending she only knew of her alter ego’s appearance on the news.

“The new holder of the Black Cat Miraculous,” she says steadily, after what feels like an eternity even though it most have only been seconds. “I saw on the news. Though I didn’t manage to get much information.”

“Exactly,” Gabriel smirks, and belatedly she’s suddenly furious with him for his actions out there.

It was only a ploy to get close to her, she reminds herself but Gabriel’s supposed to love Emilie. To be faithful to Emilie. Nathalie has staked her own life on the idea that his wife is the only thing that can make Gabriel happy again. That Gabriel, unlike her own father, or any of the men she’s know is a man devoted enough to his wife to fight against death itself for her.

He’s not meant to be the sort of man that goes out and flirts with another woman. He’s not supposed to enjoy flirting with another woman the way that gleam in his eyes right now betrays that he does.

It’s a very different sort of heat that rises in her now as Gabriel announces, “and I think she likes me.”

“What.” The flatness in her voice is no performance. This is not a situation she ever thought she’d be in.

Though at least it shows that Gabriel is unlikely to figure out that was her when despite the Miraculous on his chest he’s never betrayed any sign of any awareness of her feelings. Which is all for the best. She’d be mortified obviously. It’s good he respects her well enough to deliberately not prey on her love for him. After all it’s not like he’d even return them. Not like he’d ever look her way. Not like he’s attracted to her.

Unless apparently she doesn’t look like herself.

Perhaps, and the thought makes her wish she had those claws again so she could vent some of her rage by ripping into the pillows, it’s the green eyes.

“You wouldn’t have seen it on the news but…she let me go.” The a tinge of marvelling in his bafflement, “and anyway I can tell when a woman is attracted to me.”

It takes Nathalie some effort not to roll her eyes, and she can’t quite keep the dryness out of her voice when she says. “Can you?”

“Oh she was certainly affected when I had her in my arms.” A plan is forming in Gabriel’s head, she can see it, and her instincts are screaming at her that she needs to stop this now. That is going to be a worse mistake than that time he decided he wanted an ice rink for a catwalk. “I’m going to have to seduce her.”

“Sir?”

She’s embarrassed by how much of a shriek she makes.

Gabriel draws back a little, seeming equally shocked, but it must affect him at least a little from how he doesn’t meet her eyes.

“I realise it sounds bad. It’s not that I mean to cheat on Emilie. Hopefully it won’t get that far.”

Never mind hopefully. Nathalie won’t let it. She’s not going to become his mistress in a secret even from himself when she’s so far successfully pushed any guilt she has at her feelings for him into working to get his wife back.

“It sounds like you’re going to have to do something.”

“All pretence. But don’t you see, if I can throw her off enough to get the ring, or confuse her loyalty and bring her over to us, or get her to reveal her identity, then we’ll have one of the Miraculous jewels we need.”

Nathalie could stop this right now by giving him the ring he hasn’t yet noticed on her finger.

Then Gabriel says, “I’ll need your help of course.”

The bottom of her stomach drops out. “You need my help to seduce…”

“Panthera,” Gabriel delivers each syllable with a self-satisfied smack of his lips. “That’s what they’re calling her. A fitting name. But yes, I’ll need your feminine perspective. It’s been a long time since I courted Emilie,”

“Mme. Agreste said she made the first move.”

“Details. And I approached her first, even if it was just as a model.”

“I don’t think this is a good idea sir.”

“What?”

“We’ve other plans. Other ideas. There’s no reason to debase yourself doing this.”

“I’m capitalising on a new opportunity. And I’d hardly think that.”

“By flirting with a pretty woman? What do you think Mme.Agreste would think of that? Surely you can’t think she’d condone it? Gabriel. Please. Think about this.”

But bringing up Emilie was a mistake. She can see that as his expression hardens and he stands up. Flint sparking in his eyes.

“People have died from my akumas Nathalie. Miraculous Ladybug brings them back but I’ve already made myself the blackest of men for Emilie’s sake. I’ll do anything to save her. And yes that includes going as far as I have to with Panthera.”

Once she’d been used to Gabriel’s beratement. Used to weathering his anger. Used to not speaking up or making any suggestions.

But that’s changed. Now she’s become accustomed to being able to calm him, to being the exception to his rages, to not quite being his partner, not quite being his co-parent and Adrien is still uneasy territory for her, but to having her contributions valued.

And why shouldn’t she?

Isn’t she his Mayura?

Isn’t she dying for him?

Isn’t she lying to him only because she wants to save him from himself?

Only for him to turn around and do this. To twist everything they’re done to something tawdry. To fill her mind with images of their limbs entwined as he tries to pull that ring off her finger.

She’s a cornered animal, and it’s half rage half self-defence, when she flings out, “you’re just justifying. You want an excuse to screw another woman. Forget about Panthera. You’re better than this.”

“How dare you,” Gabriel’s lip curls, “I think you’ve forgotten your place Mlle. Sancoeur.”

It’s a clear demotion from Nathalie and they both know it. And what’s worst is they also both know she’s just nudged him into fixing on this ridiculous plan.

Plagg reappears by her side the moment he disappears. “So. Paris’ foremost mockery of a father is Shadow Moth. He’s doing it to get his wife back. And my new holder is Mayura. And also you’re in love with him, and he’s just decided to seduce you.”

A damming summary indeed. Nathalie rolls over and screams into her pillow.

Then a moment later there’s a slam of her door, and a glass put down on her side table with a thump of ill-grace.

“Here,” Gabriel says gruffly, “I forgot to ensure you took your medication. Whatever your… problems with insubordination unnecessary martyrdom isn’t a good look on anyone.”

She’d guess it was Nooroo who reminded him. Even healed Duusu is still a little scatter- brained.

“Thank you sir,” she mumbles as she gulps down the water and pills, “and I’m sorry. I didn’t mean to diminish your loyalty to Emilie I just….”

There’s a slight loosening of tension in his jaw. “Had a bit of a shock. I realise my plan is a little out there. And Panthera is a very attractive woman. But I promise this isn’t just for my own selfish pleasure.”

“I understand sir.”

“Good. Your assistance will be invaluable.”

Once Gabriel leaves the room Plagg announces. “Lady. There is something very very wrong with you. Also you owe me the largest wheel of camembert possible for keeping mum about this.”

Chapter 3: Plagg I

As a rule Plagg doesn’t really go in for introspection. There’s stuff he's done and stuff he hasn’t done. There's stuff he can't do and stuff he can do. And short of combining with Tikki there’s nothing he can do to change any of that.

He’s not Fluff or Sass. Time runs linearly for him. And millennia is a long time for regrets and second guessing to build up over something he can't change, and couldn’t have even chosen to have done otherwise in the moment because he’s bound by the forces that tie him to his Miraculous.

So what's done is done. He hadn't stopped Adrien from thrusting his Miraculous into his caretaker’s hands because once the boy took the ring off there was nothing he could do, and besides Ladybug needed aid right that second. He hadn't acted quickly enough to cataclysm his true holder's, because he refuses to think of Nathalie that way, father once he figured he was Hawk Moth because it’s not like he could have in her hands. So it is what it is.

Not that Plagg particularly had any compunctions about the idea of destroying Gabriel Agreste before he’d known he was Hawk Moth. He’s killed before and he’ll kill again. It's what destruction does. Tears something apart for creation to re-assemble in a brand new way. The dance he and Tikki have done together since before they were Plagg and Tikki. Since before they had their Miraculous, when they soared through the vast abyss of space.

Still he’s grown attached enough to this place to be wary of using his powers unfettered. He’s had the time to become quite a connoisseur of cheeses and to have seen some of his favourites disappear with the passage of time, as they fall out of fashion, or as tradition of making it is lost with its people, or even as the grass eaten by the animal providing the milk has changed. He doesn’t plan to accidentally lose Camembert by destroying Gabriel Agreste for not being there for his son.

Unless the man gives him stronger ammunition to hate him of course, but until the scales are flipped that heavily Plagg isn’t going to chance that things will come up his way. Gabriel Agreste isn’t worth losing the country around him. Or he isn’t yet anyway.

Unfortunately no one else in the house shares Plagg’s feelings. Neither on the fact that this world would be rather better off minus one Gabriel Agreste since both Adrien and Nathalie inexplicably seem to want the man in their lives, or the fact that it’s not worth worrying about what’s done.

“It’s just,” Adrien bites his lip, “do you think I did the wrong thing by giving the ring to Nathalie?”

Does Plagg think Adrien made a mistake giving the ring to Mayura?

Yes.

But also. With everything he’s seen. Perhaps not. Perhaps Adrien has stumbled into a way out of this for all of them.

Besides he can’t tell Adrien that part. Nathalie won’t let him. So it’s not something to feel guilty about.

“Eh, she seems to be coping,” for a certain definition of that that seems to include a lot of screaming into her pillow. “And anyway I told you. I can’t heal that leg of yours. I’m not Tikki.”

“But,” Adrien is pacing on his crutches now, “she’s ill. Like half the time she uses that robot to walk. How can you transform her?”

“That’s different,” because it’s magic, “I’m not healing anything with her,” not that she doesn’t need it, but all Plagg can do is collapse some of those corrupted magical channels in her body which will give it a chance to recover at least, “just giving her a bit of a boost, like I give you. Unless I missed something and you can jump off buildings normally?”

“I mean usually I can jump from pretty high,” Adrien gestures at his rock wall as he collapses onto the bed, and Plagg stiffens to attention, waiting for the thrum in the vibrations of the universe that suggests he’s done further damage to his fracture but thankfully the kid seems fine, “but yeah I guess that’s true. Still. I don’t like it.”

“Hey, I miss you too kid,” Plagg flies down, to nuzzle against Adrien’s face, and then aware that’s a bit too affectionate for his reputation adds, “Nathalie won’t keep a cheese fridge in her room. She just tells me to go to yours.”

“She probably wants her privacy.” Adrien says.

“Maybe,” Plagg agrees noncommittally, but yeah she probably does want to keep whatever her new secret plan is for solving this secret from him given she knows he won’t agree with anything that includes making the Wish. Been there. Done that. Lived through the unforeseen consequences. Not planning to let anything like that happen to his Adrien.

And maybe even Nathalie, who despite being a villain, lacking backbone where it matters, and having the worst taste in men Plagg’s seen in 8 centuries, even if he was sleeping in the Miracle Box for some of them, is kind of starting to grow on him.

Totally unrelated to her having introduced him to cheeses he’d not yet heard obviously.

Course with her it’s equally likely that she wants the privacy to make mushy faces at Hawk Agreste, or Gabriel Moth or whatever he ought to call him now, without Plagg making gagging faces after.

He’d do it over his shoulder if he was bold enough but he’s not stupid enough to let the man catch sight of him.

Nathalie might hold back out of, mostly embarrassment for the whole situation really, but also plus a little love for Adrien but the boy’s father will not. That Plagg’s sure of, and it’s a real pity because apart from Adrien deserving better it also takes the fun out of the whole thing.

Suddenly Adrien makes a face, “you are giving her privacy right Plagg?”

“Yeah, yeah,” he brushes it off as he phases into Adrien’s cheese store to grab some camembert.

His wielder is sitting up looking as serious as Plagg’s ever seen him. Not sad in that way that Plagg hates, the way he does when he looks at that picture of his mother sometime, or when Tikki’s chosen apparently forgets his is an actual person with his own problems and not just a joke machine she can rely on to back her up when she needs it. But serious. Set on something like its life and death that the woman who schedules every second of his life gets time alone for hers.

Adrien shoulders set back as he sits as straight as he can with the bottom of his leg in a cast, “I mean it Plagg. It’s not like you can be in there when she’s changing like you do with me, and never ever go into her bathroom like you did that one time I was showering.”

Plagg rolls his eyes, “I thought you were over that.”

How was he to know that humanity was going through one of it’s “bathing is private thing” moments?

“I am,” Adrien’s doesn’t sound like he is but Plagg refrains from pointing this out, “but that’s not the point. The point is you shouldn’t be around when Nathalie’s undressed.”

“You don’t care.”

“That’s different.”

“Why?”

“I’m a guy. And a model.”

“OK so, you do know I’m not human right? Not really into your whole reproductive thing.”

“Ew Plagg,” he dodges the pillow thrown at his head.

“Anyway I don’t know why you’re bothering me. I’m here aren’t I? It’s your dad you should worry about. He’s always dropping in there unannounced.”

“Uh,” Adrien blinks, and then contrary to Plagg’s stored knowledge of human behaviour, which admittedly was less than it probably should have been for how long he’d been around, his boy grins, “he does?”

There’s something more to that grin on his holder’s face. Plagg is a Kwami not an actual cat, and yet, that sudden sharpening of the Adrien’s eyes looks like it ought to belong on Chat Noir’s instead, belongs to a being who’s scented his prey and is ready to pounce in a way that Plagg instinctively knows.

“You gonna tell me why what’s a good thing?”

“Look, I know Father said there was nothing between him and Nathalie when I asked, but maybe I wasn’t wrong. Maybe he was just in denial. I mean, he’s not very good with emotions. It makes sense.”

Plagg has to swallow his amusement at the irony of Shadow Moth, exploiting of any Parisien’s strong feelings, being bad with emotions. Not that it wasn’t true but it was a much less enjoyable thing to swallow than a good brie.

“Again, why is that a good thing?”

“Because,” Adrien’s wildly gesturing, “if Father and Nathalie get together again then he’ll be happy again. And you don’t know what he was like when my mother was around but-“

“Did he let you out the house?”

“Well, not except for basketball and fencing but,”

“Did he let you go to school,”

“No I had tutors but,”

“And he made you model?”

“I volunteered for that, and,”

“Doesn’t sound like it was much better.”

“Well it was,” Adrien snaps, and then his face crumples, “Sorry. Plagg, please I didn’t mean to snap, I just. I’m sorry.”

“Hey,” Plagg floated right up to his face, “no problem. I’ve seen much worse kid. Everyone’s got to snap sometimes.”

Adrien swallows. “Right. Well he was better. I know I spent most of my time with Mom but my father didn’t shut himself away in the Atelier all the time like he does now. He used to always be there for dinner, and we’d rehearse stuff together on the piano to play for her, and he wasn’t, he was just nicer before.”

“That doesn’t sound like much.”

“It was for me.” He mumbles looking down at his cast, “and anyway, you know Nathalie helped convince him to let me go to school, and I think she’d let me go out more,”

“Schedule-Lady?”

“Plagg.”

“OK so maybe she looks sad sometimes when she says no to you, but she still does it.” “Yeah, because she’s my father’s assistant. If she disobeys him than Father could fire her, but if she’s his girlfriend,”

“Huh. Then he’d have to listen to her,” Plagg considers, there’d be no more ‘know your place’, and it was true. She clearly did have some boundaries when it came to Adrien and his father since she hadn’t betrayed that the boy was Chat Noir yet. Or given him the ring.

“Exactly. And that would basically make her my stepmother. Or eventually anyway.”

“I don’t think it would be that eventual,” Plagg mutters. A man who looked at Panthera and took about a second to decide he was into her didn’t exactly seem like a man who moved slowly.

“What?” Adrien stares up at him.

Plagg shakes his hand. “Nothing, so?”

“Right. So that would mean Nathalie wouldn’t feel the need to hold with me, she’d be more like,”

“A mom,” Plagg finishes softly, “Adrien do you think…” he didn’t want to pop the boy’s bubble but he’d seen how Ladybug’s constant rejection was wearing down the boy. He didn’t want him to suffer the same here.

“I’m being a little optimistic?” The boy shakes his head, “I’m not deluded Plagg. I know Father and Nathalie are Father and Nathalie. I know them getting together isn’t going to magically turn them into my friends’ parents. I’m not sure ever of them know how to be anything other than reserved. But we could, all three of us, be happier than we are how. And…I know it’s stupid but,”

Plagg stays quiet giving Adrien the chance to expand.

“I know Nathalie cares. And I know dating my father wouldn’t magically cure her. But I guess her being ill makes me think of when my mom was. And….I’d not thought about it before but, Nathalie is technically my dad’s employee. Theoretically she could just leave for another job. There’s nothing keeping her here.”

“Wouldn’t sure be more likely to leave if she broke up with your dad?”

Plagg was no Nooroo or Duusu but he was pretty sure the woman was halfway to stomping out the door out of anger at Shadow Moth’s shenangigans as it was. Which he wouldn’t condemn, and would in fact encourage, preferably with destroying the house on the way out if it wasn’t for Adrien.

Adrien laughs hollowly, “oh trust me. I’m not worried about my father ever letting her go if he works out he likes her. He doesn’t exactly cope well with that sort of thing.”

Now that was an understatement. Though he guesses Adrien, thankfully unware of Shadow Moth, doesn’t know that.

And despite the fact that if magic didn’t force him to keep schtum on the matter Plagg could end Shadow Moth’s reign in seconds but telling Adrien who his father really was, by telling Tikki who Shadow Moth was Plagg was thankful his boy was unware.

It might have been better for the rest of the city, and for Tikki’s chosen if Gabriel Agreste could be unmasked as Shadow Moth.

But Adrien, unfathomable as it seemed to Plagg, loved his father. And he wouldn’t want him to be Shadow Moth, wouldn’t want him to fall to whatever type of justice was currently in vogue. He didn’t want to be off with the rest of his family, though if they were all like that Felix then Plagg couldn’t blame him.

His boy wanted his family here back together.

And Plagg couldn’t give him Emilie Agreste back even if he’d wanted to.

Maybe though, he could give him a new one, and the best thing was that it was basically the path of least resistance. Even if it did include working out a way to talk to Nooroo and Duusu when his their holder didn’t let them go more than metres from him. And possibly Tikki too if he was to figure out a more permanent way to save Nathalie which was now a much more difficult thing to work out since both their holders weren’t going to the same school.

“Heh,” seems pretty fair as a way to no give anything away.

“I’m serious Plagg!” Adrien says, “All we need to do is make Father realise that he likes Nathalie!”

The easy answer to that seems to be to dress her in black leather, but alas Plagg’s already done that and she’s been oddly resistant to all of it despite the fact her mind must have had impact on the costume.

A grin slides up his face as the idea forms in his head, “sure you don’t have an idea of what might stop someone from looking at the girl right by them because they’re in love with someone else?”

Adrien’s nose wrinkles, “why would I know that? You know I tried with Kagami but I still loved Ladybug even if I now I’m starting to think it would be better if I didn’t.”

“I don’t know that. I just think you should cast your net elsewhere.” Preferably to the version of Ladybug who was actually in love with him just to cure Plagg’s headache.

“I’ve loyal to Ladybug even if she isn’t to me,” Adrien’s held his hand to his chest with such passion that it almost pushes Plagg to the floor with the fact that Adrien is in fact his father’s son, despite his previous theory of him and Félix being swapped at birth.

It’s something that both does gives Plagg an insight to what he’s dealing with with the adults and simultaneously makes things so much worse. Still. He does have the benefit that Adrien was too young to react to Lady Noire in quite the same way Shadow Moth reacts to Panthera.

“But you definitely want your father to get over your mother?”

Adrien’s eyes well up like a little kitten desperate for a parent, “Mom’s gone. I know that. There’s nothing I’d like more than for her to waltz back in our door but it’s been too long Plagg. People don’t, don’t come back after as long as she has. Beside I need Father to admit to Nathalie he likes her or maybe she’ll give into Shadow Moth.”

The boy makes it sound like a curse word.

“You noticed his interest then?”

A crutch suddenly whips up like an order to fire, “Me and everyone else. I knew he was a supervillain. But I didn’t know he was a pervert! And a cheater! Look at poor Mayura, he’s just abandoned her.”

“Eh, maybe she’s gone.”

Adrien lunges forward, “you think she’s dead? Oh my god, Plagg what if I’ve sent Nathalie into the jaws of a serial killer? What about Ladybug? Now he hasn’t got me as distraction what if he goes after her?”

Plagg didn’t have much awareness of what happened when he transformed a hero but, “I’m pretty sure he’s only interested in Panthera.”

“That doesn’t make it better,” his boy splutters.

“Doesn’t it?”

“OK maybe a bit. But still.”

“Calm it kid. You and Nathalie get me that stilton you’ve promised and I’ll make ensure your dad and Nathalie aren’t flirting with anyone apart from each other.”

“Errr, I’m not sure you should tell Nathalie about this?”

“Oh don’t worry. I know exactly what I ought to tell Nathalie.”

Chapter 4: Gabriel II

Chapter Summary

If you'd like to see Gabriel being this ridiculous please see the fanart Despicablebird drew for this scene (and some later ones on the last slide) here

Fate has hardly been on Gabriel's side these last few years. Even before he started to fight one of its chosen. But this evening he seems to have been blessed with some unexpected luck. He doesn’t know what made him decide to stroll back from the event instead of taking the car but it’s put him right in the path of the two people he’s been wondering how to find.

Strange for them to meet in dark empty park and he files away the information that the heroes either don’t have any sort of headquarters or that Panthera isn’t trusted enough to be brought in.

Despite the shadow they stand in and her dark outfit he can make out the annoyance in Panthera’s posture.

“Can we get this over with?” She says, “I need to get home before it’s noticed I’m gone.”

The irritation in her voice reminds him of Nathalie’s when he causes delays to her schedule. Not that she’s had such chances to be annoyed recently with her confined to her bed. Guilt squirms in his stomach, but he forces it away.

If he can manage to get the Miraculous he needs then Nathalie will be whole and well as if she’d never not been.

“Chat Noir and I manage well enough.”

Panthera’s claws tap against her leg, “apparently. I assume you hide it from your parents too then?”

Ladybug’s arms cross, “it’s not that I want to. I can’t risk them becoming targets for Shadow Moth.”

“Or you don’t trust them not to take your Miraculous from you?”

“I trust my parents,” Ladybug says mulishly, but her distrust for Panthera can be easily felt. It’s not strong enough for an akuma no, but it’s an interesting crack that Shadow Moth might be able to exploit later.

“And yet you won’t let them keep you safe.” Unlike her younger counterpart interestingly guilt seems to be the main emotion for the Cat. He wonders exactly what the relationship between her and Chat Noir is.

She’d referred to Ladybug’s parents earlier in a way that certainly doesn’t suggest that she isn’t Chat Noir’s mother but neither does she seem to particularly resemble the boy in personality or appearance. She’s too old certainly to be just a friend, and the boy seems a little old for a nanny but he doubts the boy would just give away his Miraculous to someone he didn’t trust.

Her guilt does suggest that it’s possible she took it from him but in that case he’s surprised she’s yet to make any play for Ladybug’s, unless she’s still trying to gain the girls trust.

It’s something they might have tried in other circumstances. Had the Peacock not been broken then perhaps Mayura could have been their Trojan house into the hero’s inner circle.

But then if that’s her play then it’s odd to get Ladybug on the defensive like this.

The girl’s hand goes to her yo-yo, apparently thinking similar thoughts to him, “were you lying about Chat Noir being injured? Did you take his Miraculous from him?”

“No. But if I had then he wouldn’t be injured.” The chagrin in her voice certainly sounds like a parent. Gabriel hadn’t given much thought to why Panthera had taken Chat Noir’s place but if that’s why he almost feels sorry that he has to deceive her the way he does. It’s what any parent should do for their child, but unfortunately he’s fighting for his own family and that has to come ahead of any sympathy he might have for hers.

Ladybug shakes her head, “that’s not your fault. It’s Shadow Moth’s.”

The image of the laughing hero hurt somehow looks wrong somehow in his brain. It’s not something that happens in this mind numbing routine of theirs. But he ignores the emptiness inside him. This is all for a reason and one day it won’t have happened at all. All of Paris would be happier if the heroes would just let him win.

And anyway whatever happened to Chat Noir can’t be down to him or Ladybug’s powers would have fixed it.

Panthera echoes his thoughts, “If it was Shadow Moth’s wouldn’t your magic have fixed it?”

“OK so it wasn’t exactly Shadow Moth’s fault. But still Shadow Moth’s the reason he has to be Chat Noir. That’s not your fault.”

“It doesn’t make me feel much better,” Panthera mutters, before shaking her head, “anyway why did you even want to meet up?”

Ladybug straightens. “So I thought we should discuss patrol.”

“Patrol? Look you and Chat Noir might have the time to spare hanging out, but I don’t. I have a job. And it’s not like us being seen is going to discourage Shadow Moth when getting our Miraculous is what he wants.”

“But it makes the people of the city feel better, and that doesdiscourage akumatisations “Patrol if you want to Ladybug,” Panthera extends her staff. “I’m going home."

“Hey,” Ladybug yells after her. “You can’t just leave.”

But the woman does, much to Gabriel’s amusement. He really could like her under better circumstances.

Thankfully the young heroine seems too affronted to make chase, so he ducked behind a building and transformed and did so himself.

By sight alone he’d have lost her but the steady annoyance and concern the woman feels shines out like a beacon in the dark for him to follow. It’s novel trying to focus on just one person when their emotions aren’t the strongest calling out to him. Like trying to use untrained muscles.

It’s something that perhaps he should practice. A way to track Ladybug, or indeed Panthera more meaningfully. As it is he can barely keep track of her. But to do that he’d have to practice on someone and the idea of having to feel someone’s petty annoyances and enjoyments all day gives him a headache just thinking about it.

His own grief and guilt is quite enough to deal with.

Nathalie alone he might be able to stomach in his mind. He’s thought of it before. Sending out an akuma but not transforming her to connect them psychically rather than through easily hacked technology. Or even trying to go further, the user sets the limits, that’s what Nathalie’s told him. And it makes him wonder if he could do something more subtle. Akumatise Nathalie into Nathalie but well and healthy. Surely it should be easier than his usual transformations.

Only his butterflies and the damage the Peacock Miraculous has done to Nathalie don’t seem to mesh well together, or so his last attempt at Catalyst seems to suggest.

Besides doing to Nathalie what he’s doing to Panthera now, reading her emotions to take advantage of her, without the benefits of the above seems like a level that despite all else he’s done he’s hesitant to stoop to. She’d let him. He knows that. Nathalie offers him all she has, more even than he has offered to Emilie. Which is one of those thoughts he tries to forget because that’s easier than living with it, though a vice in his chest never quite lets go of it. Whatever secrets Nathalie has are hardly any danger to him so he won’t reward her loyalty by expecting her to give them up.

Or so he tells himself. Because the alternative is that it’s cowardice. That he doesn’t want to be forced to recognise the pain she’s in everyday because of him.

No. Panthera is the safer target.

The cool slide of her staff across his neck seems to belie that.

“Silent as a cat indeed.” He chuckles. There’s no true violence in her right now.

“Stop stalking me,” she announces, no commands with a force that reminds him of the declarations Emilie used to lay out making him almost want to obey.

Except that of course would defeat the purpose of the whole thing.

“Who says I’m following you on purr-pose?”

“Did you seriously?” She releases him and walks around to face him, “did you just do what I think you did?”

“Purrhaps.”

She makes an incomprehensible sound and her emotions are a bright confused jumble too that fairly burns against him.

“Why am I even, I thought it was a fluke before. You know I’m the one wearing the Cat Miraculous right now?”

“Trust me. I’m well aware.” It’s his turn to almost growl.

“Stop trying to make me see a resemblance between you and Chat Noir then.”

Her skin shines in the twilight mix of the moon and the streetlights, more touchable than any fabric he’s ever used, and he suddenly wonders whether if Chat Noir is her son, or even her step-son given their disparate appearances, then if the boy’s father is in the picture.

“Are you married?”

“What?!,” She shrieks, “what sort of question is that?”

Widened with shock her eyes glow in the dark, so inhuman in a way that perhaps would be offputting if he wasn’t used to Mayura’s gemstone gaze being such a source of hope. Instead it makes him wonder what colour her eyes are. Not green he thinks. Despite the striking contrast it is it doesn’t seem right in her face, so much sharper than Emilie or Adrien’s. Brown perhaps, or no blue seems to fit better. She’d be beautiful with blue eyes.

“And what’s that?” She gestures behind him.

“What’s what?” He glances back confused.

“Your coat.” She clarifies. “Why are you making it stick up with that?”

“Like,” he looks down and in that moment is extremely thankful for the mask hiding his blush as how the tail of his coat has flared up like, well like the tail of a peacock displaying himself in front of a possible mate. “I’m not doing this.”

“It’s your suit.” Her own tail belt jabs behind her as if to punctuate her point.

“It’s not deliberate!”

“Why is it-“ She pales and her eyes go downward, “this never happened to Mayura.”

“How would you know?” He tries to press the damn coat down. This was not going to impress Panthera.

Her lips press together. “I guess I don’t. But no one ever reported this.”

“Well despite the colour of her costume Mayura is a peahen. I suppose it works differently.”

“Peahens display too. More for intimidation or out of excitement than for,” she flushes, “mating purposes. But thanks for clarifying that she was a woman. Just in case I hadn’t realised.”

“Don’t be sarcastic. Besides, it’s not like Mayura had anyone to react to. Chat Noir’s father wasn’t subbing in for his son back then.”

He can’t quite make out what her reaction to that means on the situation he’s fishing for. Panthera swallows and her voice is oddly strangled when she says, “there was you.”

For a moment he doesn’t take her meaning. It’s so natural to have Nathalie by his side he’s never considered her in such a way despite the various idiotic rumours over the years. And he’d never brought up the fact that Hawk Moth and Mayura had had rumours of their own. It had been a useful enough subterfuge in that those who thought Hawk Moth and Mayura were a couple would never link his wife’s disappearance to situation but there had been no reason to embarrass him and Nathalie both by discussing it.

“Me?”

Panthera’s gaze on him makes feel small, as if Emilie’s soul has inhabited her and is asking him what the hell exactly he’s doing.

He shivers a little in the suddenly chilly night. There never was anything between himself and Mayura, and so he can give Panthera the truth. Must give Panthera the truth if his plan is to succeed. But that very plan means there must be something between him and a woman not his wife, that he is being unfaithful after all just like the heroine thinks he is.

The other night he’d told Nathalie he wasn’t doing this for himself. That this was just for Emilie, and for her. And it is for them. This really could work. She’s already shown a surprising softness towards him, and he’d felt the coil of attraction in her belly as he’d held her. It’s just another avenue to exploit.

But she’s also a very attractive woman. One his own stupid coat has betrayed he’s attracted to as if his body wasn’t capable of that by itself. It’s a game that until now he’s been enjoying.

And wrong as it might be he can’t stop when he owes it to then to exploit any chance he can. He’ll just have to ensure it doesn’t go too far.

“You called her your partner.”

“In my quest. Not. Mayura and I. We were never romantic.”

“What were you then? Whe-“ her words are curtailed by a cough, “Where’s she now?”

Instinctively he reaches out to steady her, but unlike Nathalie she flinches back.

Her unconscious rebuff of what had been an honest gesture destroyed by their places on opposing sides makes all his lies and sins feel very heavy, in a way that with Nathalie as his only unjudging witness they usually don’t.

“She’s my best friend.” He swallows as if that can relieve the tightness in his throat, “and she should never have got involved like this like she did. I should never have involved her. She’s, she’s not well. That’s why Mayura won’t be making any more appearances.”

“Your-“ Panthera’s grasp on her staff suddenly tightens as she sways, and coughs again.

“You’re not well either,” he realises suddenly.

Her other hand goes to her which chest rises and falls in way that betrays the deeper breaths she’s taking. Someone trying to stifle a cough that isn’t quite coming he recognises.

“Hey,” this time she allows him to place a hand on her back and rub soothingly, “you need to get home. And tell Chat Noir to find someone else.”

The relaxation he’d been pressing into her vanishes. “What? Aren’t you?”

“Aren’t I what?”

“Nothing,” she says, laying her head against her staff. “Anyway how I can head home now with you following me?”

“The same way I can trust you not to follow me.”

“You rather have the advantage there on me,” that stubborn pout reappears on her lips, “I wish I could trust you.”

“We both know any reason worth becoming Shadow Moth isn’t something you can just give up an opportunity to get my Miraculous because you feel sorry for me.”

“It’s not.” He agrees, feeling like quite the cad and unsure who he’s betraying, “Although Mayura might object to me bringing you home to share her sick bed while I worked on getting Ladybug’s.”

“I think she’d work out what happened if I didn’t appear and you suddenly had my Miraculous without you needing to hold me captive so I couldn’t communicate with her.”

“Perhaps,” she does have a point and he’s not quite sure why the idea even came to him except that while he’s the villain he’s not trying to be the bad guy with her. He’d look after her after defeating her. “but you let me go once, turnaround is far play.”

She turns her head to look him in the eye. “Alright. It’s not like I have much alternative I guess. You leave to the right. I’ll leave to the left.”

Chapter 5: Nathalie II

Plagg clearly isn’t as sympathetic to Nathalie’s plight as Duusu would have been had Nathalie still had the Peacock Kwami by her side. Though he was rather more with it than her poor former kwami had been, and therefore more sensitive to the possible fallout of the whole situation, even if he didn’t care to well, care. Or he didn’t like to admit he did anyway.

Unfortunately for him Nathalie’s in a fairly similar position regards Adrien herself, if for slightly different reasons so that pretence of being unbothered doesn’t really work on her. Besides which she’s deeply familiar with the idea of transactional relationships and given that she, or technically Gabriel, is providing the kwami with housing and with rarer and more sublime cheeses than Adrien had been aware of with Gabriel’s credit card, she feels that it entitles her to have Plagg as, at the very least, a listener.

“His flipping coat flew up! What the hell was that,” she spits, “oh Nathalie I’m only doing this for Emilie,” it’s perhaps not the most accurate impression of Gabriel, certainly not even necessarily the best impression she could do of Gabriel but right now she’s beyond infuriated with that man and in not mood to be generous, “lies, he’s attracted to Panthera.”

Plagg swallows the bite of cheese he’d just taken, “OK, so I just want to check that you do know you’re Panthera?”

“Of course I,” her throat convulses trapping her words inside it, and instead expelling air the series of ferocious coughs that still pain her ribcage with their force despite the fact that she ought to be used to this now seem to get trapped in her throat. Instinctively she’s brings her hand to her mouth trying to get whatever’s blocking it out.

“I’ll take that a yes,” Plagg continues munching.

Ignoring him she finally seems to shift whatever it is and it feels like her entire breathing system clears out, even as she tries not to gag on at the unexpected taste of salt and ash. Thankfully she manages to expel it from her mouth instead of trying to swallow it back down.

Focuses on regaining her breath she waits until she feels less likely to pass out before holding up her hand and trying to work out what exactly she’s managed to spit out onto it.

The dark mess was a sticky tarry consistency, but less fluid than she expected blood to be for all that she knew the colour usually represented old blood that had had time to sit in the body outside the veins.

“Hey,” Plagg suddenly appears over her shoulder, “looks like I’m doing something.”

She lets the substance catch the light as she tilts her hand to inspect it and croaks out, “are you killing me? That doesn’t seem like it would help Adrien? Or is it not deliberate?” There’s a pause and then he mutters, “really a good thing that you got me and not Tikki you know? You two wouldn’t get on at all.”

That’s not even worth acknowledging with a comment though, “Don’t suppose you want to tell me who did get Tikki?”

Plagg just raises his eyebrow.

“Thought not. So,” she waves her hand, “this?”

“I’m not trying to kill you sheesh. That would upset the kid. And better you as my holder than that trashbag of a father of his who also happens to be Shadow Moth and is currently enslaving my friends.”

“Didn’t think you were the type to have friends. Save for Tikki. I know you have your” she makes quotation marks, “celestial bond.”

“You humans are the one calling it that, though at least it’s less tiring than what you humans get up to. I thought the kid was bad enough but,”

“Wait.” Nathalie sits more upright, and then regrets it a moment later as her maybe-bloody hand makes contact with her duvet as she steadies herself, but that’s not important because “Adrien has romantic issues?”

Adrien is a child. That wasn’t supposed to be an issue for years. Not until they get Emilie back at least. Nathalie is sure she didn’t even have crushes at that age, and she’d found her classmates’ claims rather shallow and performative. Adrien can not be love with someone.

She refuses to entertain the possibility.

Because if he does then she’s going to have to do something about it. She’d have to talk to him about…things, and it’s not her place and goodness knows she doesn’t have any good advice, so maybe she should get Gabriel to do it but it’s such a strain to get him to make time for Adrien these days and the idea of having to talk about emotions to his son would probably send him fleeing in the opposite direction.

Besides what if he doesn’t approve of whoever it is?

No, better to deal with it herself first and then work out a plan of action. Even if somehow Adrien handles it perfectly, and Gabriel approves, then there’s still so many logistical issues to handle. How the brand will handle it from a PR perspective, how she and Guillarme will handle it from a security perspective, how to be sure whoever it is isn’t just after Adrien’s fame and money as a step up.

Which wouldn’t necessarily be a problem if not for the fact that she has a sudden dread certainty that Adrien’s going to take after his father in this department and then that would crush his little heart and she will tear any wannabe girlfriend to pieces before she lets that happen.

“Yeah you know he does.” Plagg sounds infuriatingly unbothered.

“No.” She holds up her clean hand to gesture to him, “No I do not. I would be aware if I had an issue of that scale.”

“Uh, Ladybug ring any bells?”

“Oh my god.”

And she’d known Adrien was Chat Noir, but between everything, and especially that damned father of his she’d never even processed any of the implications past the fact that she and Gabriel had been fighting Adrien all this time, that the boy trusted her to fight his father, that Gabriel would eviscerate her for keeping this secret- both for failing to keep track and control of Adrien and for preventing Emilie’s revival, that they could have seriously injured, could have killed Adrien…

There had been a lot of things on her mind of rather higher priority than the fact of how Chat Noir acted with his partner.

“Mmm.” Plagg hums around his cheese, almost as if he enjoys her dismay.

Unfortunately even to their enemies it was violently clear that, “Chat Noir is in love with Ladybug.”

“Mmm.”

“Adrien is in love with Ladybug. In love. With Ladybug. Gabriel’s son loves Ladybug. The very obstacle in our way. Oh my god I have to defeat that girl and Adrien loves her.”

“Can’t agree on that last bit.”

“Look, yes, Gabriel is. I’m not sure he’s the man I thought he was,” that lust that she saw in his eyes in burned into her retinas, “and maybe he’s not the man I fe-.” She shuts her eyes. She’s not ready to say that out loud to even to someone as unable to tell Gabriel as Plagg, “that I chose to aid. But that doesn’t mean that I should just abandon Emilie to her death.”

“Eh, everyone dies.”

His blaséness is a shocking clang against the reverence she’s used to hearing Emilie spoken of with, and slips under her ribs forcing air out in a gasp, “She’s Adrien’s mother.”

“Yeah.”

“How can you,” she wants to punch, to claw, even to walk around to and pace off this energy but she’s too weak to do any of that, “you said you wouldn’t kill me because it would upset him but you’ll leave his mother to rot? Are you just too lazy to actually do anything for him”

“Look. Lady. She’s dead. You’re not. Very much not the same thing.”

“She’s not dead, she’s…sleeping.”

“Hmm. I like a nap as much as anyone but if you need the Wish to wake her it doesn’t sound like she’s sleeping.”

She shakes her head, “it’s complicated.”

Emilie was alive when she entered suspended animation yes, but it’s equally true that should they remove her she will die. Not in minutes necessarily, but probably within hours, Nathalie doubts she’d last a day.

They’d come very close to the line when it came to putting her into it, Gabriel had been frantic throughout the set-up, and in truth he’s never quite shucked that nervous energy despite the glacial front he puts up.

“I get it. You wanted to help. You thought you were clever. You thought it’d be a few attacks and wham, perfect world with her and whatever else you-“

“Gabriel won’t have fought Adrien.” It’s suddenly clear as anything to her. A solution to the whole sorry mess cresting over the horizon.

“What?”

“If I take Ladybug’s Miraculous and make the Wish then none of this will have ever happened. No one will remember Shadow Moth, or Mayura, or any of those akumas. Isn’t that better?”

“Nice idea, if it worked like that.”

“What do you mean?”

The kwami goes impossibly still, stiller than still somehow, as if his every atom has stopped in place. His eyes glow, not in the way cat eyes reflect, but actually casts a green tinge over the room, and the air starts to vibrate.

Then Plagg shakes his head and croaks, “camembert.”

Nathalie reaches for the drawer she’s turned into an impromptu cool box. She’ll have to order a mini-fridge and tell Gabriel it’s for make-up or something.

Plagg almost snaps her fingers off taking the camembert from her hand.

“What was that?”

His voice is oddly serious as he says, “why I shouldn’t try to remember the last time a Wish was made.”

“It has been done before then?”

Her research had certainly suggested it but she’s too good a historian to assume a story shown in something thousands of years old literally happened the way it’s written, especially without other supporting sources. The Wish could be made. That she had to believe. But the story of it being made could have been a parable, or a prophecy, or-

A warning.

Plagg shakes his head, “I can’t, I can’t remember exactly what happened. Maybe Sass or Fluff could. Pity you can’t ask them. But what he wanted. It wasn’t what he got.”

“That doesn’t make sense. If you Wish for something it has to give it to you. There’s a price- I’m aware of that and I’m ready to pay it but,”

“You’re where he gets the self-sacrifice from then?”

She feels sick as without any conscious input her brain starts to replay a loop of how Adrien throws himself into danger for his lady.

“That’s not what we’re talking out.”

“Fine then. Yes. You can get the universe to give you your wish but it,” his mouth makes a few odd shapes before he seems to settle on, “flexes. Find the easiest way to do that and bounces into that shape. You want her to never have died? Have a universe were she never lived, or one where she turned left instead of right and married a different man, or one where she grew up to be totally different, would never chose to use the Miraculous and she’d look like your Emilie but you’d never recognise her. You can’t just make things like they were but for one small change.”

“That sounds more complicated than what I read of the price.”

“The price powers the Wish. It doesn’t form it. You could die for her but you don’t get to pick how she comes back.”

Nathalie doesn’t even try to not collapse back onto the bed. She wants to disbelieve it but she’s not sure that Plagg can lie to her like this. Omit information yes, failure to be offer helpful suggestions certainly, but outright lie about something he’s not compelled to?

When she wants the truth more than anything?

That’s not a bet she’ll take.

“So I’m even more screwed?” She groans, “I can’t even Wish my way out of this?”

“Cosmic destruction and recreation seems a bit much when there’s an easy solution,”

“What-“

A knock at the door breaks their conversation, and Nathalie deliberately looks to her drawers without moving her head making it clear to Plagg to make himself scare.

“Come in.” She calls.

Unsurprisingly enough it’s Adrien. It’s been easier training him to knock than his father, to the extent that Nathalie thinks there’s an equal chance of her dying from her magical wounds and dying from a heart attack from Gabriel opening the door when she’s talking to Plagg.

At least she wouldn’t have to deal with this then.

Only she’s not sure she could rest in peace with the idea of Gabriel knowing she was Panthera. Or with him having the Black Cat Miraculous with all that Plagg had told her.

“Hey,” Adrien says, making opening the door look effortless despite his crutches, and smiling as Plagg mulishly reappears as if he begrudges Nathalie having told him to hide.

“Hi,” she echoes back unsure. Adrien is much easier to handle when she was a reason to be interacting with him. Even if it’s one she’s made up herself. She’s never been much of a child person, and avoiding the void of Emilie’s absence makes it hard to find firm footing.

“I hope you too are getting on.”

Plagg rolls his eyes but offers, “she buys good cheese. I’ll give her that.”

“We’re managing.” Nathalie says, “he’s not that difficult.”

Like a child Plagg sticks his tongue out of her as if to disagree.

“And you’re doing ok as Panthera? With-“ Adrien stops and goes pale in front of her eyes. “What’s that?”

“What?” Nathalie belatedly remembers the unknown bodily fluids coating her hand and curses.

“Is that blood,” Adrien comes closer and grabs her hand, sitting down beside her and for a moment the image of Gabriel doing the same after one of her times as Mayura overlays him, “Oh I know I shouldn’t have let you take that ring. I knew you were ill. I should have just gone out myself I,”

“Hey.” Nathalie interrupts. “None of that. You, Adrien Agreste, are not going out with that ring until your leg is fully healed.”

Ideally he’s not going to go out with that ring ever again but given her previous plan for that was smashed into smithereens a few seconds ago she’s still got to work out exactly how to finagle that so there’s no point on dwelling on it.

Adrien looks likely to argue back as shadows gather on his face, and then the tiniest hint of a smirk lifts the corner of his lips, “that didn’t earn all my names?”

“Not unless you do something stupid and act on it.”

His face falls again, “but it it’s making you even more ill then….”

“Calm down,” Plagg interrupts, “like I told her. I’m not killing her. I’m helping.”

Nathalie looks away from Adrien towards Plagg. “You didn’t tell me you were helping.”

If one Miraculous alone can still her deterioration then perhaps it could do more. Maybe she doesn’t need both after all. Either way it, well, it suggests that she has more time which should be a relief.

Only she’s got so used to living with her ticking clock counting down she’s not quite sure what to make of the idea she might be alive after all of this.

“We got distracted.” The Kwami shrugs, and it’s not not the truth even if it undersells Nathalie’s whole reality being shattered.

Again.

As if Gabriel isn’t enough.

Adrien’s frowning, “but how can your powers help Nathalie? We destroy things. Which is pretty good in a fight but if she’s ill how does that help?”

“Thought I’d taught you better than that kid. Destruction’s just as important as creation.”

“I know but,” she can see his eyes move as he thinks, “are you destroying what’s making her ill then? Isn’t that dangerous?”

“A little,”

“a LITTLE?” Adrien shrieks, and this time it’s his mother than he sounds just like, “Plagg.”

“Adrien,” Nathalie places her clear hand on his shoulder to stop him then addressing Plagg, “so it’s sort of a hmm, magical chemotherapy?”

“Something like that.”

“Hmm.” Nathalie accepts. She’ll have to question it more but it doesn’t sound hopeful for Emilie and her weakened body.

“I’m still not sure I like it,” Adrien, sweet Adrien who has no idea of the state Nathalie was in before Plagg stating using his power, says.

“It’s fine Adrien,” Nathalie reaches for a tissue to at least not give him the mess to stare at on her, “what else did you come in to ask? You were saying something when you,” she hesitates, “noticed.”

“I wanted to know how you were doing as Panthera.”

“Fine.”

“Really?”

“Yes.”

“And wish,” Adrien sheepishly reaches behind his neck, “uh Shadow Moth? In the news footage he sort of seems to,”

“Shadow Moth’s an idiot that thinks I’ll be so flattered by him flirting that I’ll hand over my Miraculous. I can handle him.” She shuts that idea right down.

At least Adrien doesn’t know that’s his father out there.

“Right. Um. You’re sure?”

“I’ve handled worse working.”

“Uh. Does Father know that?”

“Why would your father know that?”

“So he could stop it?”

She just stares at him, “I’m paid to help your father not vice versa.”

“Yeah but, but I’m sure he’d want to stop it. And,” he gestures upwards in triumph, “doesn’t he have a responsibility to give you a safe working environment or something?”

“Adrien,” she sighs, “there’s a lot of stupid men out there. If it was anything more that I couldn’t handle I’d tell the appropriate people.”

Unfortunately she’s not sure that HR can help with the fact that her boss while moonlighting as a supervillain is unknowingly hitting on her sort-of-pretending-to-be a superhero officially because of his mission but unfortunately apparently because he’s attracted to her-but-only-as- Panthera.

Or with the fact that even though she wants to punch in him in his stupid captivating face it also makes something inside her go weak.

“Alright,” he bites his lip uncertainly, “so, what about Ladybug?”

“What about Ladybug?”

“Do you like working with her?”

“I’ve certainly worked with worse people,” unfortunately true much as she’d prefer to be working with one of the girl’s parents. Especially since Adrien wouldn’t be in love with them.

Adrien’s nose scrunches up, “you don’t like her?”

“I didn’t say that.”

The girl’s stubborn, convinced she knows best, and secretive. Unfortunately she’s also some sort of genius and committed to what she has to do. Which is rather unhelpful for Nathalie. And what she represents is more damning than all the rest of it because frankly the rest isn’t that much unlike Gabriel. So Nathalie could work with her if that was what she wanted to do for real instead of just pretending to while she bides her time.

“I thought everyone liked Ladybug.” Adrien grimaces, “mostly.”

Probably remembering Volpina or someone similar.

“She does her job. That’s all that should matter.”

Adrien pouts, “you’re not being,”

“Being what?”

“It’s just,” he runs his hand through his hair, “apparently you can be, that is some people at school think you’re scary?”

What. “Are you saying you’re worried that I scare the heroine of Paris?”

Nathalie’s got a good face on her for scaring off nosy and/or meddling teenagers but that girl faces akumas every week. She doubts Panthera anything frightening to Ladybug.

Adrien’s eyes go horribly soft, “no, she’s the bravest person I know. But it wouldn’t hurt to be nice to her? Like you are to me?”

Nathalie spends half her time with Adrien telling him what he can’t do.

He continues apparently having not noticed that, “I just. I hoped you’d get on. It would be nice.”

“You like her don’t you?”

He looks down at the ground, “I think everyone knows that.”

“It’s brave of you. To proclaim it like that.”

“That’s what you do when you’re in love isn’t it?” Adrien says, unware of how he’s making her squirm, “Look at Father.”

He’s far too calm to know Shadow Moth is his father and “what?”

“His love for my mother is all over the house,” Adrien says, “it’s what you do.”

“Right.” And knowing it’s just that should make her feel easy but instead there’s a pit in her stomach. “You’re sure you love Ladybug?”

“Yeah.” Adrien laughs, “it’d be easier if I didn’t. I’ve tried that, but even when I’m cross with her, down below it all I still love her.”

Nathalie tries not to react, despite the alarm bells blaring in her head, “I’ve heard love is like that.”

“So you’ll try to be nice to her.”

“I’ll try,” she allows totally defeated, but then manages to speak more firmly, “and you have a photoshoot in an hour you need to leave the house in five minutes for.”

“Thanks Nathalie!” and he’s out of the door in a whirlwind.

“I’ll break his heart if I betray that girl,” she mumbles.

“Yeah,” Plagg helpfully chimes in, “that’s why you have to seduce Shadow Moth.”

“WHAT?”

Chapter 6: Adrien I

Being useless is a trait that Adrien’s unfortunately intimately familiar with. He was unable to lighten his frail mother’s burden as she became more unwell, and then unable to keep her there with them. Unable to find a way through to the father after the shutters came down after her death to the extent that frankly he’s given up.

And now he’s unable to even help Ladybug. As if Chat Noir wasn’t being relegated to the same level as one of the temporary heroes already now he’s totally failing to turn up.

Instead Adrien sits here with his stupid broken leg, itching under the cast in a way he can’t even manage to scratch, and Panthera, serious hardworking Panthera who somehow manages to turn up for every attack despite being supposed to be with his father all day, is Ladybug’s new partner and maybe that’s a better fit than the joker he used to be given how much he always seemed to say the wrong thing to Ladybug.

Except these days he can’t say anything to Ladybug at all.

Unless he sends it through Nathalie, and. Well. Nathalie know how he feels about Ladybug obviously but it still feels awkward as anything to expect her to bear a message to her. Especially given that he knows that she doesn’t approve of him being Chat Noir and, though he doesn’t get it, she doesn’t much seem to like Ladybug much either.

Which. He knows it’s really really terrible but he almost sort of appreciates. He’s not quite sure if it’s just that it’s nice to have someone else, that is he loves Ladybug but she does tick him off sometimes when she acts like he’s failed some test he doesn’t know he’s taking, so it’s sort of reassuring to know that there isn’t something inherently wrong in him for being annoyed at someone so good. But if Nathalie who can’t fault Ladybug for doing her job, which in Nathalie terms is one of the best compliments she can give, can dislike Ladybug maybe Adrien can too.

Beside a little voice that sounds like Plagg keeps whispering that if they don’t get on, though once again Adrien isn’t sure why- they even share a colour scheme, then maybe Ladybug is less likely to pick Nathalie over him.

It’s possible that voice is Plagg.

Nathalie’s pretty good at letting him roam free, which is kind of ironic really, except that he guesses she doesn’t let him out of the house at all unless he’s going with her.

Which is pretty rare these days given she’s still not well whatever Plagg is, hopefully for the best, doing for her.

Adrien has tried to ask but Plagg does keep dodging the question which isn’t the most reassuring thing in the world.

He trusts Plagg.

He does.
