It’s just. Plagg’s Plagg and he’s not really sure that Nathalie’s his priority.

Which, it’s not like Plagg could really do much, but Adrien kind of thinks she needs to be someone’s that isn’t Shadow Moth’s.

The photo on his computer screen is grainy in the dark, and Adrien doesn’t get what the hell Alya was doing at out at that time of night anyway, but it’s very clearly Panthera and Shadow Moth.

And it’s so unfair because Alya doesn’t know Nathalie at all, and Adrien can’t correct her because he can’t admit that he does, but the implications in the paragraph are so very very unfair.

Alright Nathalie and Shadow Moth aren’t exactly fighting in it, and maybe they’re standing a little too close, and maybe Shadow Moth is kind of smiling but it’s not like they’re kissing or something.

And what Adrien does know, but can’t correct Alya about, the same way he can’t stop Shadow Moth from being a creep and sexually harassing Nathalie, is that Nathalie can’t be betraying them because she still has his ring.

Sure if it’s all an attempt to trick Ladybug then maybe she’d hold onto it but what Alya and whatever over conspiracy theorists casting aspersions on Nathalie don’t understand is that Nathalie could never have planned to acquire his Miraculous the way she had.

It had been pure dumb the opposite of luck that Adrien had fallen of a rooftop just as his timer went off, and after Ladybug had done her magic, and messed up his lower leg. And then that he hadn’t been able to sneak the ring back to Ladybug or at least to one of his classmates though he wasn’t sure who he could have chosen.

Marinette would probably have been a good choice, though his face feels warm as he pictures her with that ring, and his stomach squirms at how actually she and Ladybug probably would have got on perfectly and they’d both have smiled at him and very kindly explained that they wouldn’t need him back as Chat Noir after all since they worked so well together.

But that is very much the not the point. The point is that if Nathalie was working with Shadow Moth, which she isn’t then, she could never have set it up to get his ring.

Plus it’s not like she has any time to be having secret rendezvous with Shadow Moth when her entire life is basically spent keeping his father functional.

And also he was pretty sure that however unready his father might be for it, that nonetheless there certainly was something between them regardless of their denials.

Maybe Adrien should have felt worse about that. More defensive of his mother. But for all that he’d exchange this life in a moment for one with his mother in it he also knew that after how long it had been she wasn’t coming back.

Someone new, had his father somehow been able to find his own outside the house might while shoving Adrien aside might have felt different but Nathalie wasn’t that. She’d been there as long as Adrien could remember, and she, just like Adrien himself had been simultaneously thrust into the spotlight and pulled back into the insular environment of their home.

Adrien had lost his mother.

He wasn’t willing to lose Nathalie too.

But right now it seemed like there was nothing he could do as Shadow Moth tried to steal her.

All that separating him from the scene in the garden was a window and a few metres and how his stupid rubbish leg was failing him.

He could make his way out there but anything could happen while Adrien stumbled his way out there, especially while he’d lose sight of them making his way done to the door. And then when he did get out there what could he do?

What was he supposed to do?

Much as trying to whack Shadow Moth with his crutches would have been very satisfying it didn’t seem likely to be very successful. Were he Chat Noir right now then he’d burst out and hit the villain with his cataclysm.

The fact he wasn’t Chat Noir was the problem.

Panthera fumbled the box of what looked like pastries in her hand only for flipping Shadow Moth to catch it and offer it back to her.

Then then same Nathalie who Adrien had thought was devoted to his family was frozen in place as the supervillain held his hand back towards her with the box in his hand.

Shadow Moth dares to look kind like he wasn’t the worst type of man, like there was nothing else that Panthera could do in the circumstances but look star-struck by him as she hesitantly took it back.

And then Shadow Moth takes a step closer to her and Nathalie still doesn’t move.

Adrien wants to scream.

Run.

Fight.

Do Something.

He’s not asking her to cataclysm the supervillain. That seems too much to ask of her for when it’s hardly like she eagerly volunteered for this job, and he can tell she doesn’t like it however much she holds back from saying so.

That if it’s going to happen to Chat Noir’s job, and right now as the supervillain reaches out to cup her cheek he feels much less hesitant that normal.

If he’d been able to hear what they were saying before through the glass he certainly wouldn’t be able to do know with how his blood thunders through his ears as his hands form fists.

Maybe he should shout or do something and break Nathalie out of what spell she’s under, because he does not like how they look together.

They look, almost, like a couple.

Moreso even than his father and Nathalie do sometimes and they’re always standing next to each other and exchanging loaded glances and whispers.

And. Look. Adrien doesn’t believe in criticising other people’s clothing choices. What you wear is as valid a method of expression as anything. But while originally he’d been touched by how Nathalie’s costume had echoed his own, save for the missing bell and larger mask, it had still been a little odd seeing her in what wasn’t her usual workwear.

The mask had made a certain sort of sense. Nathalie might have been less famous than him but she was hardly an open person, and she seemed terrified of his father finding out.

The latest change to her costume though.

It might not have been a problem if it wasn’t for the fact that a certain supervillain certainly isn’t ignoring that the zip of her costume now seems to be fixed in place pulled partially down so that now it exposed her neck, and gave the faintest hint of cleavage.

So much for Plagg’s claims that he'd put a stop to.... Whatever this was between Panthera and Shadow Moth because while he couldn’t accuse Nathalie of flirting back she wasn't stopping this either and Shadow Moth was definitely flirting.

The pervert was obviously pretending he was just looking down at Nathalie because of his stupid height but Adrien wasn’t stupid. He could see what was actually going on here and he didn’t like it.

Finally Nathalie takes a step back shaking her head.

Adrien frowns as Shadow Moth reaches out for her again but then the super villain pulls his hand back, and says something else, and doesn’t stop her as Nathalie keeps stepping back away, thankfully at least having not completely lost her sense as she never turns her back on the villain for as far as Adrien can see her as she rounds the corner of the house.

Shadow Moth meanwhile just stands there with the dopiest smile on his face.

Adrien sort of feels like he should have a little camera drone or something to send out after the man and follow him home. Or wherever he lurks anyway. It’s hard to imagine Shadow Moth having a home, having a life outside being Shadow Moth but then maybe people think the same about Chat Noir.

Maybe Mayura got fed up and left him and that’s why he’s using her Miraculous now, and why he’s so obsessed with Panthera because he can’t get over being dumped and wants to prove himself, or he just can’t work out how to live without a woman by his side so he’s fixated on her.

It would certainly explain why he’s acting like this about someone he doesn’t even know.

Unless he’s just like that with woman generally.

Adrien really doesn’t know why Nathalie doesn’t just at least punch him.

He’s sure Ladybug feels the same way.

But his attempts to make sense of any of this are shattered by the sound of the front door bell. Adrien rushes to it, or as much as he can in his current state, well aware that it’s generally best not to let his father to be the one at the door.

When he gets there, and flips on the camera he’s shocked to see Nathalie, as Nathalie, outside looking likely to faint.

“Wasn’t planning to come in this way,” she gasps, explaining why she didn’t just use her keys, “take these and hide them from your father.”

She holds out the macaron box.

Confusion rises in him as he stares at it, “What? Don’t you,”

“Now Adrien,” her tones broke no disagreement, “he can not see it.”

Leaving her here feels wrong but it’s not like there’s any danger. There’s no reason Shadow Moth should be in the house, and besides Nathalie’s only Nathalie now. He’d have no reason to pay attention to her.

Unless he really is that indiscriminate when it comes to woman to hit on.

No. He’s asked so much of Nathalie. It’s only right that he does this for her.

“OK,” he takes the box, even though it doesn’t really make sense to him. It’s true his father wouldn’t approve of him stuffing himself with sweets and pastries but at the same time it’s not like every single thing he eats is monitored that closely, elsewise he’d never get away with ordering all that cheese for Plagg.

Unless Nathalie’s been hiding that from his father too.

The idea makes him rush a little more, and it’s lucky that he manages it because he only just manages to get up the stairs when he hears the door of the Atelier knock against the wall as it opened and then his father’s, “Nathalie, what are you doing down here without your exoskeleton?”

With a skill honed from years of spying on his parents grown-up parties while he’d been theoretically confined to his room he carefully ducks down behind the bannister, difficult to bend leg and all, and watches as his father carefully picks Nathalie up.

It’s a kindness that Shadow Moth would never be able to muster no matter how hard he tries, and thankfully from how all that fight seems to drain out of Nathalie it looks like whatever spell the supervillain has her under the it hasn’t yet broken the draw his father has on her.

But she did really want to keep this box secret from him, in a way that if Nathalie hadn’t seemed panicked by Shadow Moth’s appearance in the garden he would have thought that was because they’d come from him.

Wherever they came from his father’s making his way up the stairs with Nathalie in his arms and Adrien needs to scram.

Once in the relative safety of his room Adrien gingerly opens the box. However all that’s in there are macarons. His heartbeat slows down a little at the reassurance there’s no obvious danger. He’d almost thought they might be something dangerous, or a secret message from Ladybug or something with how adamant Nathalie had been able hiding them.

…Although, now he looks at it they are in particular colours.

Red isn’t unusual, and though it’s less common neither is green that strange, but the black is. They’re in the colours of Ladybug and Chat Noir.

And on the interior of the lid there’s a not bad at all drawing of two hands fist pumping, and above it spelled out in alphabet stickers it says “Get Well Soon.”

It’s a message from Ladybug. His Lady hasn’t forgotten him after all.

Not even Shadow Moth could dampen the fire that’s ignited in him right now. He’d run round the room in celebration if he could.

No wonder Nathalie didn’t want his father to inspect this, for all there’s no reason he’d put together that this wasn’t just a gift from a schoolfriend. There’s no way for him to know Adrien has any link to Ladybug past being a fan.

Still a message from Ladybug reminds Adrien that he does still have responsibilities. Chat Noir can’t fight out there, but Adrien Agreste can take the field here. Metaphorically. He has to make sure that Nathalie doesn’t make herself ill being Panthera even if Plagg claims to be helping, that she works with Ladybug, and that she doesn’t fall to Shadow Moth’s foul seduction tactics.

As Chat Noir Adrien knows how to fight with very single weapon he grab on the battlefield. He’s…he’ll make his father fit his purpose somehow.

It’s not like his father hadn’t successfully wooed his mother, so despite his generally awful skills at gift giving he must be good in some way when it comes to romance.

The issue is making him realise that he wants to woo Nathalie.

Adrien’s pretty sure that he does even if he feels guilty about it, and it’s not like he doesn’t understand why. This house is a shrine to his father’s love for his mother. It must feel like betraying her even though he’s not.

Adrien knows it’s easier for him, because for him Nathalie’s always been part of his family, it’s not like he’s trying to suddenly make her his mother. Whereas for his father….yeah he guesses it’s different.

Not that Adrien wants to get shouted at either.

He needs to find a way to make his father realise that he needs to get out of denial and date Nathalie or they might lose her.

“Hmm,” he considers, and carefully hides the macarons under his desk, “maybe the truth is the best course of action.”

Heading to the door, he decides to wait until he can ‘accidentally’ intercept his father coming out of Nathalie’s room. It might take a while. Those two can talk for ages.

“Father,” Adrien calls, and despite how his father’s face seems to shut down on hearing him he perseveres, “how’s Nathalie?”

“Fine.”

So that’s not giving him much to work with but he tries again, “just working too hard again, hey?”

“Perhaps you should consider emulating some of that work ethic yourself. Shouldn’t you be studying?”

Adrien puts on his best, mother-taught, innocence face, “Nathalie was going to go through my history with me but if she’s not feeling well then…”

His father stops. He’s caught him. “Can’t you practice by yourself?”

“She says being quizzed is a more effective learning exercise.”

That gains him a pinch on the bridge of his nose. “She’s sleeping now. Why don’t you do it later? I’m sure there’s other things you can do for now.”

“Is there anything I can help you with?” He decides to give a hint, “things must be busy in Nathalie’s absence.”

“I can manage without a teenager’s help.” His father turns away.

“Wait!” Adrien calls after him, “do you think Shadow Moth is hot?”

For once he seems to have really shocked his father. His jaw drops wide open, and his eyes are bulging out. “Do I what?!”

This hadn’t really been his plan, but he guesses he can work with it, “I mean some people must right? He’s uh,” OK Adrien has no idea what people see in Shadow Moth, “strong?” think think think, “and uh, tall?” strike at his jealously, think, “I mean he must even be taller than you, I didn’t think anyone was taller than you.”

“Adrien,” his father has gone so pale he looks sick, “what the hell are you talking about?”

He’s successfully thrown him enough that didn’t even sound that angry, time to aim for the goal “I mean I guess that must be what Nathalie sees in him.” He can’t see what else it could be.

“Wait, you think Nathalie is attracted to Shadow Moth, oh thank god,” his hand brushes over his forehead, “of course that makes so much more sense, why would.”

He comes to a sudden stop, straightens up, and turns that stare that usually heralds trouble down on Adrien.

“Wait. Why would you even, look. I’m sure that Nathalie has no interest in Shadow Moth whatsoever. You keep she’s very dedicated. If you’ve caught her following videos of the attacks I’m sure that’s just so she can try to find a way to get us safe.”

“That’s probably how it started.”

“How it started?”

“But she gets this look in her eye when she looks at pictures of him. All dreamy like the girls at school when they have crushes on people.”

Technically the look Nathalie gets when Shadow Moth gets close to her is probably more like a deer in the highlights but that doesn’t sound very romantic.

“Look son,” his father’s voice sounds rather strangled, “I think you’ve got the wrong else of the stick here. What you’re describing sounds very unlikely. Just go to your room and….practise piano or something”

Adrien’s not quite sure if that counts as a victory or a loss.

Chapter 7: Gabriel III

His gloved hand is startlingly dark against her fair skin, and as his thumb passes over the almost black corner of her lips it makes it clear how red in fact they are. Her mouth parts at the pressure, betraying a glimpse of her white teeth, making her lips even more striking.

No mask covers her face, despite the familiar catsuit under his other hand on her hip. As she shifts closer on his lap, his eyes are drawn up, past the curls hanging over her cheekbone, to the bold eyeliner around those aquamarine eyes that he can make sparkle with delight despite her usual face of stone.

The rose window of the lair casts strange patterns of light over both of them.

“I love you,” she says, her tongue brushing against his thumb, making him wish his hands were bare and that he wasn’t Shadow Moth, that he was, “Gabriel.”

And then it feels like his heart stops as a shrill ringing blasts through his ears and his eyesight goes dark. He grasps in confusion, one hand meeting empty air and the other fabric where she’d been a moment before.

Sunlight beats against his face, his eyelids not quite capable of keeping it out. When he tries to open his eyes he finds himself squinting.

His bed is comfortable, Emilie had insisted they only had the very best, but it’s nothing compared to the feeling of her against him.

Her.

Which her?

He groans. Panthera is one thing. He has, for the most part, made his peace with the fact that he wants her. And with the fact he should stop the game he’s playing and that despite all of that he’s not. He just needs to make sure it doesn’t go too far.

But Nathalie is quite another.

Panthera is Shadow Moth’s. Nathalie is Gabriel’s.

Panthera is a fantasy. Nathalie is real.

Panthera is an enemy he can manipulate with no compunctions. Nathalie is his steady rock to whom even thinking of doing that to would be an ill return to her loyalty indeed.

Panthera is unattainable. Nathalie is right by his side.

Panthera should he mismanage this and lose her he can manage. It would only be another setback in a string of them. Nathalie he daren’t impose upon, she’s irreplaceable.

Panthera has no idea why he’s doing this. Nathalie knows exactly how much of a betrayal of Emilie anything would be.

Panthera is attracted to him. Nathalie is ... not.

This whole confusion, it's all Adrien’s fault. Until his son had put into this head this idiotic idea of her having a thing for his alter ego he’d never considered the idea.

And much as it had been a relief after the brief horrifying moment where he'd thought that either his son was trying to confess to a crush on him, the very thought of which makes him imagine Emilie beating him bloody in her fury, or that somehow he thought Gabriel had a crush on himself which was only not distressing in comparison to the other, right now it’s not a comforting thought at all.

Nathalie can’t have a thing for his alter ego because she knows he’s him. If she was going to be attracted to him then she’d be attracted to him. Clothes can be transformative but given what he’s fighting for, what he knows he’s fighting for, he can hardly see that a change of outfit and a little more height could send Nathalie’s feelings off in such a wrong direction.

No.

If anything Adrien’s just misunderstood Nathalie’s admiration of Shadow Moth. It makes every sense that his most loyal, if only, supporter would watch his exploits hoping for the best, and look, Gabriel knows Nathalie’s loyalty is strong, knows she’s committed to what he’s trying to do, knows she wants him to win because he akumatised her with it.

He’s aware that Nathalie doesn’t just help because it’s her job, that despite her normal reticence he’d shocked her with his declaration that he would get Emilie back, and that that was perhaps when Nathalie began to admire him as a person, and not just as a designer.

Duty alone would not have let him create Catalyst.

The strength of feeling he’d found in her could easily be mistaken for something else. Especially by a boy without much experience and who was looking for it after-

Ah.

No.

That had been Gabriel and Nathalie whose behaviour his son had misinterpreted before; having seen that they had secrets and true, their relationship had shifted, and coming to completely the wrong conclusion.

As if Gabriel could let Emilie go so quickly. When Adrien falls in actual real love one day he’ll understand and he’ll laugh that he could have thought otherwise.

Still. He’s being just as bad as Adrien at making the wrong conclusions here. His son believes there’s something between himself and Nathalie. That much is correct. But Adrien’s doesn’t know that he’s Shadow Moth. So it can’t be the first that made him think the latter. To Adrien Nathalie is the only connection.

Although it does make that time look a little different. Gabriel had been so offended at Adrien thinking he’d just move on like that, as if all his time with Emilie had been meaningless, that he’d focused on the insult to himself.

He’d never considered that it might have been Nathalie’s behaviour that had caused his son to question it, but since Nathalie is the common denominator here perhaps that’s something that Gabriel should be considering.

Though he supposes it’s just the same thing really. Adrien misreading Nathalie’s support. It’s not as if he’s ever felt any jealously from her while he’s talked about Panthera.

Anger yes, but not envy, though it’s true that as a matter of course he tries not to read Nathalie’s emotions, especially when she’s less than happy with him, so it’s not impossible that he might have missed something.

But he can’t forget that memory of her loyalty, warm and all-enveloping as he’d reached into it. A woman who feels that for him, who’s never been other than steadfast in support of his quest, who has endangered herself for his sake to the extent she’s only just re-learning to walk….

It seems unfair to accuse someone like that of undermining his attempts the way that surely any misplaced feelings would.

The best thing would be to put it out of his head to concentrate on his mission.

Though, as the reminder on his phone bings, he does unfortunately have a life outside that as well and little as he cares sometimes about the brand the way he used to he does have a reputation to maintain if he’s to keep his secret safe and that not being the sort of man to left his personal life trouble the success of his business.

Work it turns out was exactly what he needed to forget the whole thing for a few hours. It says there hovering at the back of his mind, how could it not with Nathalie’s constant presence, but the constant calls and emails the two of them have to field do at least help make it difficult to devote much in the way of brainpower to Adrien’s claims.

Still as he slumps over his screen he can’t say it feels like much of a respite really.

He groans. “Have I done everything I need to do today Nathalie?”

“I believe so sir.” Her answer is efficient and professional and Adrien has to be wrong. Although it does now belatedly occur to him that just because Adrien’s interpretation is wrong doesn’t mean that it’s not dangerous that his son is linking Shadow Moth and Nathalie in his mind.

After all from Nathalie it’s only one very small step to him.

“Good,” he acknowledges her while stalling on how to bring it up, “I do have to talk to you about something else though.”

She looks away from her computer, “Sir?”

“My son decided to inform me of his belief that you have a celebrity crush on Shadow Moth.”

Nathalie’s mouth drops open before she immediately closes it.

“Yes, I was shocked as to how on earth he came to that conclusion too, though I must admit it was something of a relief to get clarification that he wasn’t the one with the crush.”

“Adrien,” Nathalie seems lost for words, “what?”

“My son desperately needs to work on his communication skills apparently,” archness allows him to hide his ongoing discomfort on the topic, and maybe a joke with help Nathalie feel a little better about it too, “perhaps you could sign him up for a seminar.”

“I can look into that sir,” she pauses, “did Adrien say why exactly he thought I…”

“Ah yes, he seems to have spotted you reviewing me in battle and rather got the wrong end of the stick. I can’t see this will cause any issues for us, but it might be wise to disabuse us of him of the notion. We hardly want Adrien to get the idea to investigate Shadow Moth do we?”

Nathalie inclines her head and doesn’t betray anything but agreement, “No we certainly don’t want that. I’ll talk to Adrien and get this sorted out.”

Relieved to have the whole issue dealt with, especially with no response from Nathalie that couldn’t be explained by the circumstances, “Good. Unless you felt an apology from him was warranted? If he’d said that to anyone else it could have caused issues for you,” and therefore them but Gabriel isn’t stupid enough not to realise that Nathalie is the one being accused of an attraction to the man the city hates.

He could easily disavow her or ignore the press.

She’d be the one with her reputation in tatters.

Nathalie seems unworried as she goes back to her work, “I’m fairly certain Adrien wouldn’t have said anything about it to anyone other than you. But I’ll sort it all out when I talk to him anyway.”

“Thank you Nathalie. I don’t know what I’d do without you,” and then because it has been an utter nightmare of a day and he’s just given her another task, “did you want to eat together today?”

“Thank you sir, but I’ve just realised I’ve another errand to run.”

“Another? Is that wise? You’re still not healed you know. Couldn’t someone else from the company do it?”

“I don’t think someone else could go to the dentist for me, no. And I was lucky to get this late slot. Actually I should probably be headed off now if that’s OK sir?”

“Of course Nathalie,” he answers immediately but he feels a little like his balloon’s just burst as he watches her head out.

He’s got used to having Nathalie to decompress with since he’s lost Emilie.

Not that it’s the same thing at all. Nathalie has a way of making it all seem manageable, helping him to relax and accept what’s done is done for now because she or he will have a new plan another day.

Emilie had been more of…a distraction from whatever happened. She always had some new thing to share, or something she wanted to do, or indeed needed distracting from her own troubles, so there had been no time for to dwell on any of his.

Whatever Adrien might think his relationship with the two woman is very different.

Still now that his plans for tonight have changed he finds himself a little lost, when a sudden fit of anger burns through his Miraculous and suggests a new plan for this evening.

But even as he engages the entrance to the lair it fades away leaving him once again at a loss and moving up to the lair for no blasted reason at all.

Perhaps he’d make a good target for an akuma right now but he’d prefer to have Nathalie back here ready to play back if should the need be there, for all that he knows he can’t risk asking her to use a Miraculous again.

The Peacock might be fixed but despite the fact there’s been more colour in her cheeks lately and she’s been managing mostly to work Nathalie very much isn’t. She’ll seem as good as she’s ever been and then the next moment she’ll have collapsed again like the other day when he found her in the hall.

He really really needs to make this Wish. For Emilie. For Nathalie. For everyone.

The world he creates will be better than this he promises.

Gabriel will be a better man in that world.

But here he isn’t and even as he turns to leave he considers that there is another woman he could go bother right now.

Logic tells him it’s unlikely. That there’s no reason that Panthera would be out tonight, and even if she was this is a big city- there’s no reason she’d be anywhere near him.

Besides it’s dangerous to leave the lair too often. Each time he leaves and returns he might be spotted and the fact that neither Ladybug or Chat Noir have even been spotted returning to their own homes or wherever they come from isn’t the reassurance it should be.

However if he was the sort of man that listened to the odds and gave up when they were against him then he wouldn’t be himself. He’d be someone who would have given up years ago. Besides it’s dark outside. It’s unlikely he’ll be spotted but he considers attempting to darken his cowl somewhat regardless.

Perhaps it was a premonition, or fate choosing to smile on him for the first time in forever, but he finds his prey sound enough sat on a rooftop staring up at the clouded sky, and it’s odd not quite gloom reflecting the lights of the city.

Only there’s many words that fit the Panthera he knows but none of them seem to fit the woman sat on the roof with her legs drawn up in front of her.

She seems small. Tired. Like Nathalie when she doesn’t realise he’s looking.

The excitement that had grown in him on spotting the green flash of her eyes ebbs and instead of flirting he just sits down by her. “Bad day?”

She doesn’t move to look at him. “You could say that.”

“Want to talk about it?”

“That’s a very transparent attempt to find out who I am you know.” Panthera says, but he can feel she’s more tired than angry.

Not that he doesn’t feel bad adding to her burden, that too will be anything he’ll fix with the Wish. “I wasn’t-“

“Look,” she says, “I’m really not in the mood for this today. I mean I’m never in the mood for this but,”

“You are,” he can’t stop but himself smirking, “I can feel what you do you know. And even if I couldn’t your costume change certainly suggests you do.”

She buries her forehead in her knees, “of course you use your Miraculous to spy on my emotions. Why wouldn’t you?”

“It’s nothing personal. It’s just,”

“What you do. I know.” She turns her head, so it’s still resting on her knees but looking at him, “do you really use it on everyone? Even…”

“Even what?”

She purses her lips. “Even people in your personal life? Even, and I don’t expect you to admit anything about who you are, but hypothetically speaking, your family?”

For some reason he feels caught in her gaze, “No. I don’t. Not people close to me.” He looks down at the ground, away from her eyes, “I tell myself it’s because I care but maybe it’s cowardice.”

“Cowardice?”

“Maybe I’m scared to know what they feel.” Scared to feel Adrien’s grief, Nathalie’s pain, that either or both might hate him for not having fixed this all earlier.

Her head is suddenly against his arm, “I think we all are. Scared of what others feel about us. I think they’d appreciate you not snooping though.”

“So I tell myself.”

“And on very different topic, trust me, the cleavage? Very much not my choice. You can thank my kwami for that.”

He looks down at the crown of her head, “but it’s your costume.”

“You’ll telling me you consciously pick every aspect of yours?”

“Well no, it’s like my akumas, it’s a-“

“Joint effort.” She finishes, “exactly. He apparently thinks I need to be more sexy.”

“That’s idiotic. You’re already that. And just showing more skin doesn’t necessarily have that effect anyway,” sometimes not showing can be more effective.

“Your eyes beg to differ. And anyway you wouldn’t think that if you knew what I really look like.”

“I doubt you look that different. Most Miraculous Holders don’t, It’s the magic that stops you recognising people. I doubt you’re secretly hideous.”

“I never said I was. Just not your type. Which is yet another reason you should stop all of this.”

“What other ones do you have?”

“I’ve already told you. It’s demeaning to both of us. And I have to somehow explain this to Chat Noir somehow.”

“Oh.” He doesn’t even her that. Thank god Adrien has no idea he’s Shadow Moth.

“Oh is right and-“ she stops. “Is that rain?”

He holds out a hand but it’s unnecessary as more droplets come, “Apparently.”

“Can I not just have one night of peace and quiet?”

Gabriel isn’t fond of the rain either but Panthera certainly feels more put out. A cat thing maybe. Or wait-

“Are you feeling better?”

She frowns, “what?”

“You weren’t well before. If you’re ill you shouldn’t be out in the rain.”

“I shouldn’t be out anyway. I just wanted to be alone.”

“I’m sorry.” But a solution to at least one of their problems begins to paint itself in his brain, “my dear Cat I have an idea.”

“Why does that worry me?”

He ignores her, and shuts his eyes, he’s never tried this before but it’s not so different to an akumatisation really. Almost a semi one of sorts.

“Nooroo, Duusu transform me.”

He hears her voice through the crackle of power running over him, “but you’re already…”

Once the purple light leaves his vision he opens his eyes, glad to see Hawk Moth’s suit back in place and a welcome new weight on his shoulders. Reaching up he finds the cape, he assumes of a Peacock’s tail, he was aiming for.

“Here,” he says, “you can shelter under this until it eases off.”

Her eyes widen but she lets him pull her close and wrap it round her.

He seems to have shocked her into silence though he’s unsure why. “Better?”

She swallows against him, “You know sometimes you make me wish you actually were in love with me.”

Chapter End Notes

Chapter 8: Nathalie III

Nathalie has decided she loves the Tsurugi car. She’d hated it when Gabriel bought it. Hated the confirmation that she was no longer an independent person who, in the very unlikely chance the whim took her, could just get in a car and drive away from all of this.

A taxi is manifestly not the same thing. Doesn’t carry the same freedom. And doesn’t give her the privacy she needs for this conversation.

For any of her conversations.

She likes Guillaume well enough, trusts him to do his job, but having him here for this-

No.

Plagg hovers in front of her, “I don’t see why it’s such an issue.”

She opens the eyes she’s been taking the chance to rest in the break from being around either Agreste for a few minutes of her day.

“Did you put him up to this?”

The kwami yawns, as if to show just how unbothered he is, “Why would I bother to do that?”

Nathalie wishes she could be equally unruffled, right now she can feel the pressure building up in her head for a migraine worthy of the record books.

“Because of your stupid plan.”

Plagg shrugs, “you’re the one calling it stupid.”

“Gabriel is not going to give up and let Emilie go because he’s fallen in love with me because Gabriel is not going to suddenly fall in love with me.” She points out.

Plagg starts, “But,”

She doesn’t let me continue, “And if he thinks himself in love with Panthera then he’s definitely not going to be once he discovers she’s me. Because he’s going to be so betrayed by that discovery that I am going to be beyond fired and also homeless. And even if he could get over that without attempting to destroy the entire world on the way he’s still not going to want me because he he’s had plenty of chances to fall in love with me and he hasn’t and he won’t.”

“Has he though?”

She’s never thought herself stupid whatever else she might be but she doesn’t get his point, “What?”

“Look lady. He’s doing all of this for his wife right?”

Nathalie shifts a bit to better look at Plagg, and see if she could follow his somewhat convoluted and usually designed to get him either cheese, or just an easier life in general logic.

“Yes.” She confirms, “you know this.”

“So if all the time you’ve known him, he’s been married or trying to get her back then when was he going to fall for you? Like, look at the kid, he’s other options than Ladybug. Girls he likes, but he won’t let himself consider because that would be betraying Ladybug.”

That makes her squint, “wait, don’t you want Adrien to date Ladybug?”

Plagg turns away from her, “I don’t want him to get hurt. You’ve seen how they are.”

“I have,” she concedes but the idea buzzes in her brain anyway, there’s something important there, possibilities in Plagg thinking Adrien likes someone else that Adrien knows and pushing him that way.

Still she files it away for something to think about in the future, and sighs.

“Have you ever thought I don’t want to get hurt too?”

“Yeah, how messed up your body feels every time I have to transform you kind of suggests not.” The snark in his tone isn’t even hidden.

With every single bit of frost she’s even blasted impertinent journalists, or tardy suppliers, or people who just thought she was too unimportant to bother with, over the years she glares at Plagg, and wishes the Kwami would fly a little lower so she wasn’t looking up at him.

“I meant emotionally. Maybe,” and drat, her blasted voice isn’t staying level, she can’t control it and it doesn’t make sense. She’s shoved her feelings down inside herself for years. She talks to Gabriel without betraying how she feels about him every day.

It’s completely unfair and inexplicable that her emotional control is failing her now, just because she has an option for someone to talk to, when said person doesn’t care about her at all and just sees her as a way to improve Adrien.

Not that she’s censuring him for that. It’s just he’s hardly someone to pour her heart out to.

“It doesn’t matter,” she blinks her to get rid of any traitorous tears before they get too close to Adrien’s school.

Plagg tilts his head and then flies into the front seat, and apparently plays with the controls.

“What are you doing?”

He comes back triumphant, “giving us a longer route to Adrien’s school. Or possibly crashing us. Hopefully the former.”

“Oh my god.”

He looks up at down, “you don’t seem to be changing it though.”

“Like you said. I’m not scared of physical pain.”

There was an uncomfortable pause as air in the car seemed to get very scare before Plagg said, “you know. My holders aren’t supposed to destroy themselves.”

Those glowing green eyes Gabriel apparently finds so appealing on Panthera are hard for Nathalie to meet, as they seem to pierce past all her layers of protective pretence of non- caring and nonchalance and disinterestedness.

“Maybe I’ll be the first then,” she caves eventually, because Plagg isn’t who she wants to talk to, but she has no-one that she can truly talk to, no one who will understand without judging unfairly and running to the law, and at least Plagg isn’t able to betray most of this, the heart of this, due to the magic that binds him.

It’s probably better than drinking wine locked in her room and getting tipsy enough to wistfully imagine getting drunk enough to actually talk to some random stranger in bar about any of this.

“What are my options?” She asks him, “Really? The Wish that I’ve been fighting this whole time to get apparently won’t work the way I want it at all. Gabriel isn’t,”

Plagg’s mouth opened as if to say something.

She held up a hand to stop him. “You’re not entirely wrong about him and I. It’s true. My feelings have changed. I haven’t loved Gabriel all this time. Admired him certainly. He gave me the chance to be part of something. Made me feel valued. And I can’t pretend that it was after he lost Emilie the first time I looked at him, and realised that actually in a way he was attractive. But it wasn’t until the moment he told me what he was going to do, until all the barriers between us collapsed as he leaned on me not only for what I did for him but also for what Emilie used to do too, that I realised how far I’d go for him. And only later that I realised what that meant.”

Maybe she is stupid.

Or maybe she’s just shamefully inexperienced when it comes to relationships for someone her age. Then again with parents like hers she’s not sure where she’s supposed to have learnt to do this healthily.

“Yeah and?”

She could feel the wrinkles forming in her forehead, “and?”

Plagg’s face screws up. “So far you just sound even worse than our kid. I thought he took the crown for mushiness.”

“You’re not so bad really are you,” overcome by a rush of affection she reaches up to stroke his back like he was a real cat and smiles as he purrs.

For love of Adrien she could forgive a lot, “but you see Gabriel’s feelings changed too. I’m not going to claim they didn’t. He does care for me. A lot even. After Emilie, and then Adrien, I’m his next priority. But that’s not love. And Panthera proves that.”

“I don’t get it.”

“Gabriel might have done things that other people might think inappropriate,” her skin both crawled and tingled at the memory of him helping her out of her clothes after Mayura had left her too week to do to do it herself, “but the reason that he could was because it would never occur to him to think of me like that. Because maybe you’re right, maybe he’s not the man I fell in love with and he can move on. Maybe Panthera proves that. Because he just saw her and it was at the very least lust at first sight, and the worst of it is that I think it could grow to be more.”

Especially after the other night where he’d held her so close, and put that creative mind to work sheltering her, and they’d both admitted things they wouldn’t to anyone else, including for him her, “But that only proves that he’s not in love with Nathalie. So what am I supposed to do?”

“Well,”

“Give him the ring? And destroy what’s left of his relationship with his son, and either make the Wish and possibly destroy everything or let him continue on without Ladybug’s Miraculous but unable to give up because he has one of them?”

“Please don’t do that.”

“So should I betray him? Betray the man I might still love to prison and infamy, still destroy Adrien’s life as a result and probably end up behind bars myself?”

“Honestly Ladybug would probably be kind of,”

Nathalie almost wants to laugh. “Oh so I should just steal both Miraculous and give them to her should I? And yeah, maybe for Adrien’s sake,” because it turned out Ladybug did care for her missing partner after all and this was how Nathalie got talked into trafficking possibly identifiable pastries back into the house and almost destroying this very fragile safety they had, “she might accept that. But Gabriel wouldn’t. He’d know it was me and bam, I’m out of his house and a job again. But then maybe you wouldn’t care about that. But if you think things would work out better with him and Adrien there you’ve got another thing coming.”

“Trust me. I don’t think that.”

“No. You think I should be whatever I can to make him fall in love with Panthera, because you think it’s possible she could get him to give up on Emilie, only for it all to fall to pieces when he asks to meet the woman behind the mask which, in that scenario he definitely would. Gabriel might compartmentalise but if he loves her he won’t be happy with just rooftop trysts forever.”

This time Plagg didn’t even object.

“So what exactly am I supposed to do? The only way I can see this not leading to disaster is if I could somehow get Gabriel to decide to give up both his Miraculous jewels of his one decision, not just because he’s in love with someone new. And if I knew how to do that don’t you think I’d have done it before?”

It’s embarrassing how she spits the words from her mouth, but she’s needed this, needed to yell at someone about how impossible this all is for a long time.

“How would I know?”

Plagg’s words puncture her growing fury.

“What?”

“Never seemed to me that you did much to stop him. Not with the kid. Not with Hawk Moth. Didn’t Mayura save him when the heroes could have put a stop to all of this?”

Guilt prickles at the corners of her eyes, “you don’t understand.”

She had tried with Adrien. But she wasn’t his mother, she had no right to impose the parenting decisions she would have made on him. She still tried sometimes because it might have been wrong, but it felt right, and sometimes Gabriel even asked her.

She had tried with Gabriel. But she wasn’t his wife. She didn’t have the ability to raise his mood the way Emilie had. To announce that this was what they were doing and make him do it. To waft around high society and make Gabriel’s reserve seem mysterious rather than haughty, to make people believe there must be something about a man who could catch a woman like her.

Gabriel had caught Nathalie’s heart by being something more than anyone could imagine but that wasn’t a story anyone wanted to hear.

And maybe what she’d fallen for was only an illusion anyway, but she didn’t know how to feel when she couldn’t help but judge him for looking like he might be able to give up, the very thing she know wanted to do.

“No.” Plagg says, “I don’t. And people call me selfish.”

Acid sat just behind her tongue threatening to come pouring out her mouth but Nathalie had been sick enough that now even though it still hurt she could see the difference between the symptoms of the anxiety she’d survive, and the damage from the Miraculous she might not.

So she shut her eyes again, and let her head hall back against the chair and tried, if not to swallow, not to heave anything up.

Letting herself consider it she thought it was possible Plagg had a point. For all it didn’t seem fair that whether she was working to try to get Emilie back or preventing the very same the only options seemed to be to sacrifice herself.

“I think we’re the same actually. We care about the people we love and that’s it,” she announces eventually.

“Kwami’s don’t,”

“Oh come off it. Maybe you don’t love romantically. Maybe however you care is in a different way. But it comes round to the same place.”

“So you’ve going to make him give up?”

“I’m going to try to do something. I’ll work out the what later.”

Opening her eyes she notices the Kwami has his hands held out and she sighs and reaches into her now-larger-than-she’d-usually-pick handbag to retrieve some brie from inside the little cool bag she now kept inside it.

Adrien might be happy to keep cheese in his pocket and drench himself in cologne to hide the smell, which is something she probably needs to do something about, but Nathalie has standards.

“Here,” she holds it out, “payment for listening to my emotional breakdown.”

Plagg grins. “See, this is why I almost like you.”

“Hmm,” she acknowledges as she checks out her make-up mirror to get her face, and see if she needs to tidy herself up before Adrien arrives.

“By the way,” he announces, “we’re basically here.”

“Great,” she sucks air. “At least Adrien is unlikely to care we’re a little late.”

As many great qualities as he has punctuality doesn’t seem to be one of them. Though she guesses she shouldn’t really censure him for that now that she knows the true reason why.

From his smile as he opens the car door it certainly looks like he’s been glad enough to have a little extra time with his friends though.

“Hi Nathalie, hi Plagg,” he smiles that great big sunshiney smile that he got from his mother that makes the guilt curdling in her stomach even worse.

She almost wants to ask him if he still misses his mother. If maybe he would want to save her.

But that’s stupid. Because Adrien does miss his mother. Her photographs in his room, how desperate he’d been to see Solitude all make that clear.

Yet he’s also moved on in a way his father hasn’t. In a way that scares Gabriel, and only widens the chasm of misunderstanding between father and son.

Even if she could believe the Wish works the way she’d once thought it would she’s not sure that Adrien would betray Ladybug for his mother’s sake. He’s too much an Agreste. Too much Gabriel’s son to betray his lady.

Or too much the son of the Gabriel she’d thought she’d known. Not this one who gathers Panthera into his arms like she belongs there.

“Hey kid,” Plagg saves her from the awkwardness her reverie could have created.

“Good afternoon Adrien,” she hopes her voice comes out level and unemotional, “I thought we might talk while your father isn’t around?”

His voice cracks a little, though she’s not sure if it’s puberty or embarrassment, “talk?”

Taking a deep breath in through her nostrils to settle herself she manages to say, “your father has informed me that apparently you think I have a crush on Shadow Moth.”

Adrien’s hand goes to the back of his neck, “he, uh, told you that?”

“I’m assuming you didn’t mean him to.”

He didn’t meet her eyes, “are you angry with me?”

“I don’t understand why you did it.” She hates how much sympathy she has for Gabriel’s inability to predict his son’s actions right now, “Adrien you have to understand that it is vital that your father doesn’t realise either of us have worn this ring. Connecting me with Shadow Moth in his mind is the last thing we want to do.”

It’s true enough as far as it goes.

Adrien seems to straighten up in the car seat, “do you though?”

“What?”

Finally he looks her straight in the eyes, “do you like Shadow Moth?”

Deflecting as best she can she turns that right back on him, “why would you think that?”

But Adrien isn’t looking like he thinks he’s the one who should be ashamed, “I saw you with him in the garden.”

“He almost discovered me coming back to the house. I had to distract him whatever my own feelings.”

He almost pouts like a toddler and not a teenager, “well what’s this then?”

She takes his phone and stares in horror at the photo of her curled up under Shadow Moth’s cloak the other night.

“Nathalie?”

“I won’t give him the ring,” she tells him, “you can trust me on that.”

“That…” he teeth worry his lip, “kind of didn’t sound like denial? This is actually real? I’ve been telling everyone it’s photoshopped.”

“Please continue doing that.”

“But is it?”

Her nails did into the palm of the hand he can’t see, “no.”

“Why would you-“

“We both needed someone to talk to. If I play along with Shadow Moth’s little fantasy then maybe I can take him down.”

“And you think you can do that without falling for him?”

“I think it very unlikely my feelings for Shadow Moth will change,” after all if her current anger and disillusionment with his father hasn’t killed his affection for him then it seems very unlikely anything will.

“I’m just worried about you Nathalie,” Adrien does look a little contrite. On the other hand both his parents are good at faking things even if Gabriel’s nowhere near as seamless with it as Emilie was.

“Is that why your told your father?”

“Something like that.”

But that doesn’t sound like the answer at all, “Adrien why did you tell your father?”

For a moment all of Chat Noir’s reckless bravery is on display as he says, “I thought if you knew he might lose you he might come out of denial.”

She does almost wish the car had crashed earlier. It would have been easier to deal with than this. Nathalie’s used to coming up short with the Agrestes. But this is a new angle that first Plagg and now Adrien are throwing at her.

“Your father isn’t in denial,” or at least not about his assistant. About why he’s going after Panthera is a different matter, “I understand our relationship might look different now but,” the only woman he’ll ever love is your mother, she means to say but the words die in her throat and she weakly trails off, “it’s not like that.”

“You reacted better to the idea than Father did,” Adrien seems suddenly less confrontational, “I’ll give you that. But you don’t have to hide it. You’re already part of my family to me.”

She takes her tablet into her hands to distract herself, because she can’t look at her after that with all the secrets she’s keeping from him. “Thank you Adrien but I’m only your father’s assistant. And that’s all I’m ever going to be.” If she even manages to continue to be that.

“You don’t really believe that.”

It’s suddenly very easy to understand how Gabriel cracked a tablet like this. “I’m afraid I do. Now. Did you have anything you wanted to convey to Ladybug? She was asking after you.”

A glance out the side of her eyes shows that Adrien is still scowling but the mention of his Lady has at least distracted him.

Chapter 9: Ladybug I

Ladybug doesn’t hesitate.

Panthera gracefully lands on the roof, showing exactly which Miraculous she has, saying “So what have you called me here for this,”

She doesn’t get a chance to finish as Ladybug’s yo-yo wraps round her and yanks her to the ground.

The woman hits the ground with a gasp as the air is knocked out of her and Ladybug tries not to feel too good about it. If Chat Noir’s in danger then that’s what she needs to focus on. Not just taking revenge for him tempting as it is.

But Panthera’s still gasping for air, when usually an akuma or their minions would already be fighting back, and Ladybug’s own hand spasms in the air looking for a Lucky Charm she doesn’t have to tell her what to do.

It could be a trick.

Or…and this shows just how woefully unprepared as a Guardian she is, and really it would be nice if maybe Su-Han actually tried to teach her something instead of just kind of threateningly promising to watch her since she’s now having to try to figure out during an actual situation if maybe a Miraculous doesn’t cure its holder of asthma or things like that while they’re wearing them.

She knows it doesn’t exactly get rid of flu, so maybe it wouldn’t solve that sort of thing either for all it makes little sense with the increased strength and agility it does grant. Though it does fit with why Panthera had claimed Chat Noir couldn’t appear.

If that’s even true.

Not that Panthera looks like she could tell her either way even if she wanted to right now.

It looks really bad.

Now Marinette feels horrible, and her anger is being drained away by panic. Alya should be around here somewhere watching, and she almost wonders if she should yell for her to get an ambulance or something.

Bending down by Panthera she held onto her yo-yo tightly but asked, “are you alright?”

She might deserve in when in between she laboured breaths the woman gasps out, “seriously?”

“OK look,” Marinette decides, “I can’t let you go. But if there’s anything else I can do,”

Panthera gestures upwards.

“Oh you want to sit up? We can do that.”

She carefully lets a little more slack into the rope and helps Panthera rest her back against the wall.

The woman’s eyes flick over her and she rests there before she evidently makes some sort of decision and shuts her eyes as she starts to take deeper breaths, clearly trying to get her breathing under control.

It doesn't escape Marinette’s notice that she covers the hand with the ring on it with her other.

But Marinette can wait.

Information is almost as important here as the Miraculous.

“That was quite a greeting,” Panthera wheezes out eventually.

“What’s wrong with you?”

“Now that's a question with a long answer,” the woman mutters.

Marinette frowns in confusion because, “What?”

Panthera’s expression is somehow haughty despite the fact she's looking up at Marinette.

“Do you think I’m obliged to give you my private medical information after that?”

Marinette tried to mirror the expression right back at her but wasn’t sure it quite worked, it was throwing her off having someone as a theoretical ally who didn’t automatically accept her leadership the way the temporary holders did.

“Do I think I’m expected not to have questions about you canoodling with Shadow Moth?”

“Canoodling?” Panthera’s brow raised over her eye, “really?”

Letting her anger overtake her embarrassment despite the heat of her face Ladybug points out, “You looked pretty cosy.”

“Urgh. I am going to kill whoever took that photo.” Panthera groans.

“Um,” she didn’t think she actually meant it but she did wield destruction and she was maybe-dating a villain so it wasn’t certain that she didn’t need to say something but…

Panthera rescues Marinette from her uncertainty, “Metaphorically.”

Under the yo-yo string her arm moves as if that was going to be a gesture before her restraints caught it, and she glances down with a wince.

Ladybug does not fidget. She had a reason for what she did and she did it for that reason. Replacement partner or not she has a duty to this city and to the Kwamis.

With a sigh the woman sat against the wall says, “I don’t suppose you’re going to untie me until I satisfy you that I’m not betraying it you to Shadow Moth?”

Marinette startles. “How did you-“

Panthera cuts her off, “you tied me up and then confronted me about…”

Even as she trails off some little wicked part of Marinette can’t wait to see what words she’d choose to describe that picture. Canoodling had been a perfectly acceptable choice given they weren’t kissing or anything, and wow Marinette was glad of that because she never wanted to see Shadow Moth making out with anyone ever, but they’d basically almost been cuddling and just affectionate in a way that screamed out their interest in each other.

“Being too close to Shadow Moth,” the older woman finished.

“Alright,” Ladybug allows, “and are you going to try to convince me you’re not too close to Shadow Moth? Or should I just take your Miraculous now?”

Panthera didn’t seem to recognise the kindness of her bringing that idea up in a question instead of yanking the ring off her finger while she was tied up, and could make it difficult, but probably couldn’t manage to stop her, as she bares her teeth and almost hisses at her.

“Chat Noir gave me this.”

Her nails dug into her palms as she made fists instinctually at the name of the partner she didn’t know was safe, “Did he?”

“He did.”

“Chat Noir doesn’t have the right to pick a new wielder,” he wouldn’t without asking me, she means to add but she’s stopped as Panthera yells.

“Cataclysm.”

And then a boiling hot knife seems to stab into Marinette’s hand as her yo-yo disintegrates, as Panthera shakes off the disappearing strings.

“Tikki!”She screams in panic, because pain is something temporary, but she’s never had damage quite like this, from another Miraculous, before.

Panthera snorts air from her nostrils, “oh so you do care for other people then?”

Marinette’s still trying to regain her own breath, and work out that her hand’s still working, and “what?”

Her apparent enemy starts stalking over, her hand up ready to use her power again the way she can as an adult and, “do you even care about Chat Noir at all?”

Weapon are not she takes a fighting stance because one thing is clear to her no matter what, “of course I do! How dare you say that when you’ve probably stolen his ring and have him tied up who knows where, and are you even feeding him and-“

“Wait,” Panthera holds her hand up, “you think I’ve stolen this ring from Chat Noir and I’ve tied him up somewhere?”

“If you took that ring from him by force and then let me go free then I know that Chat Noir would find a way to tell me so, yeah, I do kind of think that’s likely.”

Panthera arms went behind her hair as if pulling it into a ponytail, before releasing it, and her exasperation was clear when she said. “Look, Chat Noir trusted me with this ring, and I don’t want to fight you.”

“You just attacked me!”

“After you tied me up and then acted like you didn’t even trust Chat Noir, like he didn’t have the right to decide who should wield his Miraculous, like he has to get permission to do anything, that boy loves you and you treat him like garbage!”

“I never asked him to love me!” It comes out like a yell and she almost feels as startled as Panthera looks.

Swallowing, more quietly she adds, “and I don’t treat him like garbage. I just.”

That gains her a cocked head, “you just?”

“He gets on my nerves sometimes, and I have a lot to handle, and he puts himself in danger for me without me asking and I hate that, but then other times he doesn’t turn up at all and what am I meant to think? But that doesn’t mean I’m just going to abandon him.”

“Right, look.” Panthera holds her hands up to stop her, “Chat Noir’s busy. He’s…let’s just say his family life is complicated. He has a lot of eyes on him.”

“Including yours?”

“Yes. As a matter of fact. Yes. And you don’t know how awful I felt when I realised he was Chat Noir and I’d missed it.”

“Chat Noir isn’t a little kid. He needs some independence. You can’t micro-manage his life!” Adrien, “One of my friends has that and it sucks, it’s like he never gets to be a normal teenager,”

“Because being a superhero is normal teenage behaviour,” she spits with bared teeth, but then her face regains a more human position, “Look. It’s complicate and he has a lot on. I can guarantee you that if he’s not turning up it’s not because he’s not bothered. It’s just he can’t.”

Marinette wants to believe her, because much as it would all be so much easier if Chat Noir would drop this idea of them being together and they could just be friends she doesn’t want to believe that all his claims of love and loyalty are false but, “I always manage to turn up. I don’t get the choice not to.”

She’d tried with New York, and look what had happened there. It had turned out that there was exactly where she and Chat Noir needed to be, for all the people caught up in Mayura’s attack might have disagreed, but part of her was beginning to wonder if Hawk Moth had somehow followed her there.

It was her Miraculous he wanted after all.

And Chat Noir’s.

Panthera’s face seemed to soften despite the hard mask covering its entire upper half, “No. You don’t. So I guess it’s pretty lucky the Miraculous were handed out the way they were even if red is much more my colour than green.”

“What?”

“I don’t know you. I have no idea who you are when you’re not Ladybug but like I said I can almost certainly guarantee you that Chat Noir has more eyes on him than you have.”

“What do you-“ she stops herself, “Don’t let me tell what that means.”

Panthera’s looking at her like she’s mad. “OK?”

“I can’t know Chat Noir’s identity. That’s important.”

“O…K.”

Chat Blanc seems to grin over Panthera’s shoulder as her lack of conviction bites into Marinette. “It is!”

“Alright. I won’t tell you anything more about Chat Noir then apart from,”

“From?”

“You didn’t ask him to be in love with you. That’s true. But he didn’t ask to fall for you either. I’ll talk to him about toning things down because you’re right, it is too much and I don’t know where he.” A grimace passed over her face, “scratch that, but I’ll talk to him. But. Be kind to him? Sometimes your heart can be inconvenient and even if he shuts up about it that doesn’t mean he doesn’t still feel it. You’ve made your feelings clear. That’s enough.”

“I don’t understand how you can sound so much like you care about Chat Noir now, and be fraternising with Shadow Moth the next moment.”

“You know how Shadow Moth acts around me.”

“Chat Noir acts like that around me. And I don’t go around cuddling with him, and he’s the opposite of supervillain.”

“Urgh.” Panthera groans, “I really did not need confirmation that I’m not seeing things there.”

Everything they’ve just spoken about floats in front of Marinette like some imagined pin board with different clues tied together with string, and a few phrases fairly glow.

“I don’t know where he-“ followed by a sudden realisation.

“Chat Noir acts like….” “How Shadow Moth acts,”

“I’m not seeing things there.”

“Shadow Moth reminds you of Chat Noir,” Ladybug slowly says.

Panthera’s face doesn’t change. It doesn’t make any expression. But that very lack of a reaction is a reaction. It’s a refusal to react not her not having one.

Then she cocks her head, and something rings false in her tone of voice though Ladybug can’t quite work out what it is, “I suppose he does. You don’t have to worry about me forgetting who gave me this ring when I’m with him.”

“No, I mean…” but Panthera knows what she’s means and she’s being deliberately obtuse.

“I know what you mean. And you’re not wrong but if you’re worrying about trusting Chat Noir then I can tell you that they’re very different people.”

“I know that. Obviously. Chat Noir would never terrorise,” but the words stick in her throat because it hasn’t happened, but it could do, Chat Noir is capable of destroying the city when akumatised, and that has to the be akuma, that has to be Hawk Moth’s fault just like it is with every other akuma victim, or hers even with however discovering each other’s identities led to Chat Blanc, because she can’t believe her laughing kind kitty has depths of anger like that, “he’s nothing like Hawk Moth.” She finishes more quietly.

“You didn’t sound quite certain there.”

She can’t help the frown that forms. Panthera’s no Lila or Chloé, but she seems to almost be as good at winding Marinette up, “Shouldn’t you be defending him? He’s your son isn’t he?”

Apart from why else she’d know him if she hadn’t just stolen the ring by chance, and who else Chat Noir would trust his Miraculous to, how she’d harangued Ladybug about keeping secrets and not being an adult had certainly suggested that.

“I thought you didn’t want to know anything about either of our identities.” Panthera says coolly, “As it happens I agree. Chat Noir is,” she frowns a little and then seems to throw away whatever she was going to say, “he wouldn’t do what Hawk Moth is doing. But that doesn’t mean he’s nothing like him. Unfortunately there’s a shared sense of humour there.”

There’s a rueful twist to her lips but a softness around her eyes like when her dad talks about her maman sometimes that suggests a certain fondness, and more things start to slide together.

Embarrassing a question as it is, “You’re not in a relationship are you?”

“I’m sorry. What sort of question is that? I’m not going to talk about that sort of thing to a child.”

She can’t help but be a little offended by that. People don’t talk down to Ladybug like that. Paris…isn’t unware that Ladybug and Chat Noir don’t look like adults, or she hopes not given her dad had thought she was dating Chat Noir but they don’t seem to think about or remember it much either.

Except for Panthera who for some reason seems to be an exception to this. She guesses it’s because she’s thinking of her as Chat Noir’s friend and she’s hardly going to forget his age.

“How young do you think I am?”

“Not an adult. I’d like to hope you’re just short and you’re just in lyceé but,”

Wait. Was Chat Noir in lyceé?

He didn’t seem mature enough to be older than her but everyone said boys matured more slowly, so maybe, if she squinted he could be….

Marinette shook her head frantically. She had to not think about Chat Noir.

“Are you alright?” Panthera asks, “or was that just really bad acting in an attempt to pretend you’re not at lycée? Ha. Maybe I’ll tell Chat Noir not to chase after older women and see if that gets him to leave you alone.”

“Chat Noir’s not at lyceé?”

“What do you think?”

“I can’t tell when you’re lying and when you’re telling the truth. Which brings me back to how can I trust you? Especially if you’re projecting Chat Noir’s father onto Shadow Moth?”

Panthera’s jaw dropped. Then she made a couple of sounds that weren’t quite words before shaking her head and straightening up and saying, “how on earth did you figure that?”

“Well.” Marinette shrugs because it how only came to her as she said it but it did make a lot of sense, “I’d guess if you’re letting him hold you like that then you’re single. And by the way? I’m pretty sure most of Paris is talking about that picture.”

“Oh my god,” Panthera covers her face with her hand, “maybe I should give Shadow Moth my ring and let him create a new world where none of this has happened.”

Marinette threw her hands in the air, “seriously?”

“I wasn’t talking literally. So continue.”

“Right. And you’re clearly attracted to Shadow Moth even if that makes no sense whatsoever.”

“It will when you’re older.”

Ew. Ew. Ew. “I really think it won’t.”

Panthera gave her a sardonic look. “Or maybe not.”

“Definitely not. But anyway you do because he’s tall or whatever,” she really in retrospect wished her mother hadn’t decided to let her know that was a thing after seeing a fashion profile of Gabriel Agreste and giving her wink saying it looked like Adrien would sprout up sometime soon and she was a lucky girl, “And he reminds you of Chat Noir soo,”

“So?”

“It makes sense Chat Noir would take after his father, so you’re projecting the man you,” oh no, what if he’d died and she was being really insensitive, or what, given that apparently Shadow Moth was like him he was a total dick and she was better off without him, and what non-committal word could she even use and, “…lost?”

That high sqeaky end note was supposed to be only a Marinette thing not a Ladybug one.

Still she forged ahead, “on to Shadow Moth.”

Panthera blinks, “you seriously need media training. Do you have media training?”

“Uh Paris likes me.”

“So long as you keep winning. And actually you have a small but virulent set of haters online.”

There was some information she had never ever needed to know. Everything was bad enough already. She was just going to forget that. She was.

“Why would you tell me that?”

Panthera held up a hand, “OK yeah that was on me. But seriously I think you do need some training, or a PR team, actually have you looked into official branding? There’s a big monetisation possibility here, maybe some cross-marketing with hmm, do you have a celebrity crush?”

“WHAT?” She shrieks, “how is, what does that have to do with anything?”

“I supposed we could always just ask Adrien,” Panthera seems to muse, “the voice of Chat Noir and Chat Noir together. That could melt a thousand fangirls hearts.”

“Adrien Agreste?”

For some reason it was hard to imagine Chat Noir and Adrien together. She’s sure they’d get on. Adrien would mellow Chat Noir, and Chat Noir would help Adrien loosen up. They could, they’d get on she’s sure, they could become close if she introduced them.

It just seemed a little overwhelming to deal with her feelings for Adrien with Chat Noir there having feelings for her. Her brain feels like it wants to shut down rather than imagine it.

The older woman looks amused. “Do you know another famous Adrien who voi-, oh my god. You like Adrien.”

“Who wouldn’t like Adrien? He’s perfect. He’s kind, and he’s not a snob at all and-“

“Alright,” Panthera holds up a hand to stop her, “I don’t need to hear a litany of his amazingness. I’ll take it from you. But to get us back to the point where you don’t break Chat Noir’s heart by showing you don’t trust him at all, and simultaneously discover his identity. I’m not going to just give this Miraculous to Shadow Moth because I’m attracted to him. I can promise you that. Besides if I was going to do that I’d have done it already.”

“When you were cuddling with him.”

“I was sheltering from the rain.”

“Why were you even there with him?”

Panthera took a deep breath, “I do get lonely sometimes, you wouldn’t understand but,”

“Oh,” Marinette could laugh, “trust me I understand.”

That gained her a look up and down and then an apologetic, “yes I suppose you do. And…no I’m getting ahead of myself but sometimes it’s easier to talk to an enemy than fight. Especially if you need someone to talk to. And if the only decisive weapon you have is well,” she raises her hand, “a little much unless you want to debate manslaughter versus murder with the law.”

“Tikki,” she feels like the worst person in the world as she remembers that, “you might have hurt her, I,”

Panthera’s hand closes on her wrist, “She’s fine.”

“You can’t,”

“You’re still transformed aren’t you? That’s her power. If you get a countdown when she needs to eat I’m pretty sure you’d have one if she was hurt.”

“I guess that makes sense. Though I’m not sure if I can trust a Shadow Moth sympathiser.”

“Ladybug,” Panthera stares somewhere past her, “what if Shadow Moth’s goals aren’t so bad? What if he doesn’t want to take over the world or something? What if he wants a good thing?”

Oh no. Whatever she claimed Panthera clearly was falling for whatever he was selling. “What sort of good thing could possibly still be good after all he’s done? Do you know the pressure he’s put on this city? My friends live in fear of having their darkest secrets exposed to the world because some horrible man wants to exploit them for power.”

Her voice is awkwardly stiff as Panthera says, “He panics. When I seem unwell. I don’t think it’s a new thing.”

“You think he’s lost someone?” She wasn’t sure how she felt about that, “not a wife or girlfriend with how he treats you, but….a child? A sibling?”

“I do.”

Her defences almost melted but she had to stay strong, “like you have. That’s what you recognise in him.”

Panthera just inhales.

“Did….Chat Noir lost someone too, didn’t he?”

Her eyes are suspiciously wet but her voice is steady as she admits, “yes. That’s why. Look I will stop the flirting. He needs to learn that sometimes loving someone doesn’t mean they’ll ever love you and you have to make your peace with that. But. Be gentle with Chat Noir. Don’t…don’t let him feel unwanted. He…he has reasons to feel more rejected than most kids your age.”

“I…I’m glad I sent him those macarons.” That episode with Alya played in a different light. He’d been afraid he’d not see her again, and she’d brushed it off because he was her friend and of course she wanted to see him but maybe he had a Chat Blanc of his own.

“He appreciated them.”

“Good.”

Neither of them quite seemed to know what to say.

“I’m not sure I trust your judgement,” Ladybug admits, “and trust me I’m going to have eyes on you so don’t go sneaking off with Shadow Moth and traumatise us. But. I,” she wasn’t sure why she was saying it but the right path blazed ahead as bright as with any Lucky Charm, “am not going to take your Miraculous right now. You’d better justify that choice.”

“I’ll do my best.” Panthera says, “if you trust in nothing else trust that I want to do the best thing for Chat Noir.”

“Hey, maybe you could convince Shadow Moth to give up,” she tries to smile.

“That really would make everything very simple wouldn’t it? Though I can’t see him agreeing to go to jail.”

“I’d like to see him make things right,” Marinette admits, “but that doesn’t have to be jail.” In some ways it felt like jail wasn’t enough for his victims, like instead Hawk Moth needed to make amends for he’d done to each of them. "Honestly I just want all of this to be over.”

“That’s…a very mature viewpoint. Thank you. There’s more to you than I gave you credit for Ladybug.”

“Thanks and-“ was it a weird thing to say? Maybe? But it felt like the right thing, “maybe you could let Chat Noir have his Miraculous back sometimes so we could message?”

“I could probably do that.”

“And you know. Maybe you should try messaging Shadow Moth. If you wanted to talk in a way that didn’t mean he could grab your Miraculous.”

“Hmm. That’s not a bad idea actually.”

Chapter 10: Gabriel IV

It’s probably not a good thing that he’s got in the habit of checking the Ladyblog first thing in the morning.

Firstly it’s a distraction. It means Ladybug and Chat Noir are the first thing on his mind, and that sets the tone for his thoughts and his moods for the rest of the day making it difficult to concentrate on work.

Even Audrey’s started to call his designs lacking and that’s embarrassing from someone who used to rate him a favourite, never mind the rest of the ties that bind them.

And then to make it even worse there’s never enough information on the heroes to satisfy him. Just enough to tantalise him with the fact that others are seeing details he missed and yet it’s still not what he needs to find the heroes out.

Panthera replacing Chat Noir has only made it worse. Now he wakes up tired and lonely and aching for a woman in his arms, only to fumble with his phone and let habit lead him to pages upon pages of Panthera, fierce and glorious in battle.

He’s pretty sure she’d cataclysm him in an instance if she knew.

Never mind the slick guilt on his stomach everytime the blogger decides to speculate about Mayura and her disappearance and Shadow Moth’s apparent choice to move on.

It’s not reality.

Their ideas of the truth what’s happened between Hawk Moth and Mayura, and his new pursuit of Panthera, are nothing like the actual truth of what he owes Emilie and why he’s going after Panthera.

But the guilt remains.

Guilt for betraying Emilie, for enjoying himself on this quest he’s set himself.

Guilt for lying to Panthera, who has none of Ladybug’s brazen ideals of heroism and is just a tired parent trying to do the best for her family like he is for his.

Guilt that stops him admitting the above to Nathalie. Because for some reason the idea of her knowing how he feels about either of those issues makes him feel so much worse.

Guilt for pulling her into this. Guilt for getting her injured. Guilt for letting the world think of Mayura as someone to cast off. Guilt for putting her in a situation where Adrien would think her attracted to Shadow Moth.

Because of course she’s attendant to every fight he has with the heroes. Of course she’s devoted to his cause. Because it’s the only way to save her too.

So guilt is his constant companion.

And his strongest cheerleader.

Winning is the only way to fix everything.

So Gabriel goes on. Goes on sending out akumas. Goes on making plans. Goes on checking the Ladyblog first in the morning.

Today it might even be a good thing he does so.

Even if the phone slips from his grasp and he has to retrieve it from the floor hoping against hope that it hasn’t smashed.

Thankfully Tsurugi technology is better than that.

Unfortunately he did just read what he thought he read. The only virtue of the situation being that at least he was alone when he did so, because he doesn’t want to know what sort of face he just made, and because at least now he knows before….

He shakes his head to try to get his brain to work.

No one is going to call Gabriel Agreste for a press release on whether Shadow Moth is Chat Noir’s estranged father.

The so-called article, conspiracy theory, goes on to explain its idea. Shadow Moth’s clear attempts to win over Panthera, her apparent inability to not respond both with annoyance and distraction, apparent similarities between “father” and “son.”

They’re utterly ridiculous linkages of course. Just because he and Chat Noir might both use puns sometimes means nothing, especially when he’d been semi-aping the hero to get under Panthera’s skin. And he’s doubtful about any claimed similarity in their fighing techniques given they have different weapons and anyone who can’t tell that is an imbecile.

As for the pictures of Chat Noir and Ladybug plastered in comparison of him and Panthera, well that means nothing. It’s obviously that any two couples flirting might look similar.

Besides Gabriel knows that his son isn’t Chat Noir.

That’s an important fact he can’t forget.

For all the superficial physical similarities between his son and the hero, if their differing personalities weren’t enough, there’s the fact that Gabriel has considered the idea before and discarded it because he’s seen the two of them together.

And there’s further proof in the form of Panthera herself.

It’s true, and in other circumstances he would worry about it since it’s possible what he’d seen before was faked, that Adrien injured himself around when Chat Noir disappeared but Panthera doesn’t fit the pattern at all.

Emilie sleeps below them. She isn’t out there taking her son’s place. And there’s no one else that Adrien could theoretically ask. Apart from that teacher of his he doesn’t think he knows any adult woman he could possibly entrust such a secret to, except for well, Nathalie.
