Nathalie who entrusted with that ring would have brought it to him immediately. She might, because he’s not blind to her affection for his son and upon occasion disapproval of how he raises him, have lied about how she’d acquired it to him and how she’d lost it to Adrien in an attempt to shield them both and do what they’d both asked of her.

But she’d certainly have given to him.

She’s not Panthera.

And Adrien isn’t Chat Noir.

Unlike the rest of the world Gabriel knows the truth there.

The question though is why someone would think that. It can’t be the non-existent resemblance between him and Chat Noir that the author keeps trying to show with lines on both their faces. More fool them given that no one anyone has ever said his real son resembles him.

Their conclusion is wrong but how they’re come to their conclusion might give him some type of clue to Panthera and her life behind the mask. Perhaps there is an estrangement between her son and Chat Noir’s father and she’s accidentally admitted as much.

Perhaps he does somehow remind her of the man.

Both options are theoretically helpful to him and yet only the former fills him with any happiness. Gabriel will never abandon his family and he hates the implication, even by a meaningless resemblance that he would.

Groaning he forces himself out of bed. How exactly he’s going to face Nathalie with all this nonsense he doesn’t know. Obviously she knows it’s nonsense but he can’t help but feel like she’s going to look at him with judgemental eyes that make it oh-so-clear that he’s brought it on himself.

Not that it actually matters.

It doesn’t change anything.

He just doesn’t like it.

“You’ve seen it.” Nathalie says, the moment he enters her room. Apparently he’s not the only one checking the Ladyblog first thing in the morning.

He allows himself one pinch to the bridge of his nose. “Yes.”

“It doesn’t change anything.” She points out, “you know you’re not divorced from Panthera.”

He moves his hand away from his face, “divorced?”

“I thought that was the gist of the article yes.”

“Well I’m certainly not that.”

“No. You’re not.” She pushes her hair, which he know notices isn’t quite in up to its usual neat standards back away from her face, “this might be a good thing.”

“A good thing?”

“If the heroes think this is the truth then it makes you less of a candidate. And I suppose they might go less hard on Chat Noir’s father.”

“The heroes?” It takes him a moment to take the connection, “right. Rena Rouge.” Which rather begs the question of, “do you think Ladybug thinks this?”

“That’s the question I’ve been asking myself, but apart from maybe it giving you a better chance should Panthera intercede with you to Ladybug I can’t see it does us any harm or good that she does.”

“Apart from the embarrassment.”

“If you cared about the public embarrassment you wouldn’t have done any of this.”

It takes a moment for Nathalie’s words to sink in, a tendril of her hair has escaped back to her face, and there’s the slightest wave to it, and now he’s busy wondering how she’d look with other hairstyles, with it down, with something a little bit more wild like Panthera and, “hmm, wait, are you trying to insult me?”

“Are you taking that as an insult?”

“I suppose I can’t pretend I’m not a risk-taker,” he chuckles.

A knock at Nathalie’s door throws them both off.

“Adrien,” she mouths at him, and ah, that does make a certain sort of sense. His son, despite his new predilection for rebellion is a good boy. He’d want to make Nathalie’s life easier by coming to her room in the morning to get his schedule rather than coming to hers.

That’s something he’s not really thought about enough with Adrien. That his son might be more like himself than he’d realised. More motivated by the people he cares for than by rules.

It must be the stupid article making him think about resemblances but maybe this is something to consider when disciplining Adrien. He must ask Nathalie.

“Come in son,” he calls.

Adrien looks shifty and awkward as he enters the door, “uh I was just coming to,”

“Ask Nathalie for your schedule I’m sure. I’m afraid I had some urgent business to discuss with her.”

“Uh, that’s fine. She can. Um. Email it to me.”

Adrien’s nerves almost shine off him. It reminds Gabriel of once when Emilie had tried to prepare a surprise with Adrien with for his birthday and their five year old son had reverted to hiding behind Emilie’s legs at every moment he saw him in his attempt to keep the secret.

The memory makes his heart twist in his chest.

Had that article got it right then Gabriel would have been fighting his own son all this time. Would have injured his son countless times. Jeered at him.

Killed him, perhaps if not for Ladybug.

It’s true that for Emilie he might do much, might do anything, and if Adrien did dare to stand between him and those Miraculous knowing that it was his mother’s life he was ending by doing so then….then maybe he’d have to tell himself none of this would have happened in the new world he’d created.

Now looking at Adrien he’s filled with a sudden immense gratitude that could almost make him burst that his son is Adrien and not Chat Noir. That the worst he has to deal with are a few silly ideas about wasting his life, and not the boy deciding to be a hero. That their fights are never physical because that’s not a line he ever wants to cross.

Bruises long-healed decades ago suddenly feel sore on his legs.

Without Emilie’s prompting he’d forgotten just how lucky he is with Adrien. Just how thankful he should be with his son. That they’re been able to raise him into a child it’s not so difficult to be a better father to than his own was to him.

“If you’re sure,” he says to Adrien.

“Yeah. It’s fine.” Adrien gives a slight, not-disrespectful but conveying it’s not a big deal shrug, before turning to leave the room.

“Adrien,” he stops him, “you’re a good son. Most of the time. I’m glad you’re mine.”

Adrien is polite and obedient and the furthest thing possible from the wisecracking hero, and he’s not even sure that’s why he feels those words with such certainty in this moment. But he does.

His son’s wides widen, but then he grins, “I’m glad you’re my father too. So glad.” There’s more force to that last sentence than might be expected. He can’t be going too badly with raising Adrien in Emilie’s absence. Probably Nathalie’s influence. Maybe he should listen to her more.

He smiles down at her once Adrien’s left, “well if Adrien gets round to reading that article at least it might stop him worrying about you having a crush on Shadow Moth. A man hung up on another woman and fighting his own son is hardly a romantic idol is he?”

“No. I doubt Adrien would think that. Though I’d guess he’s already read the article.”

“You think?”

“All teenagers are obsessed with the superheroes. That’s why we let him voice Chat Noir.”

“True,” concern hits him, “do you think we need to distance him from the hero? If he’s being linked to Shadow Moth now?”

“I’ll monitor it.”

“Good,” he looks at her, so ready with her tablet, and determined just like Panthera and wonders, “Do you think Panthera did say something to Ladybug?”

“What do you mean?”

“Well we know that their idea about Chat Noir and Shadow Moth is complete nonsense, but is there a grain of truth at the middle of it all? Is there an estranged father?”

Panthera’s always been cagey on that question with him. But there’s a glaring flag in the very fact that in all the reasons she’s told him to leave her alone they’ve never included that she’s in a relationship.

He wonders whether she might actually appreciate it if he sent an akuma after such a man. He’d deserve it. Gabriel’s never been able to fathom how people can justify leaving their families.

“I’d say it’s likely the girl’s got the idea from something real but I don’t think you should go out making assumptions. Maybe it would be for the best if you acted as if she is still married.“

“I don’t think she is,” Gabriel says, “there’s a…”

“A what?”

“A yearning in Panthera,” but words feel weak for the emotion he feels from her, “for….she wants have a happy family, to be loved I think.”

“I can’t deny that sounds familiar, uhm, as a single woman,” she glances down, and it hits him that despite Adrien’s ideas he’s never really considered that Nathalie might actually want something like that for herself, she’s always seemed so content to devote herself to his cause despite the effect it must have on any sort of social life even before she put on that Miraculous.

Nathalie continues with a sigh “And you don’t feel bad for taking advantage of that?”

“Of course I do. But I am a villain after all.”

Nathalie’s face creases as her brows draw down, and she leans towards him, and sincerity shines in her eyes, “that’s not the truth of you Gabriel. I know that.”

“I swore that I’d see this through. Nothing else matters in comparison to that.”

“Of course,” Nathalie murmurs quietly.

He can’t help but take her hand, “this will come to end Nathalie. I promise you that, I’ll make the wish and you’ll be well again and you’ll be free to go wherever you want to go, though I can’t deny I’ll miss you if you decide to abandon my to the clutches of an inferior assistant.”

“Let’s focus on winning first. And you should go get dressed for work. So should I really.”

Still the article and how Panthera must feel about if, and if there is some failure of a man out there lingers in his mind.

And maybe it makes him a little bit more satisfied than usual to find an akuma that night close enough to the house that it’s easy for him to slip out and find the superheroine after the battle.

“You,” Panthera hisses. “Could you not just hold off for once?”

“And there was I thinking maybe we could go for a nice stroll in moonlight?”

“So we could get photographed again? I don’t think so, or you definitely won’t be seeing me again.” She raises an eyebrow, “or did you really think Ladybug was going to trust me hanging out with you?”

“I didn’t think you were beholden to a little girl.”

She scowls, he’s hit a weak spot there, “that’s not the point. I’m not interested Shadow Moth, leave me alone.”

Or so her words say but as he reaches out with his power the mix of emotions inside her is much more less of a definite brush off. She’s irritated at him, lots for an akuma to work with there certainly, but below that there’s a part of her that wants to give in. Wants that choice to be taken from her so no one could blame her.

Alas for Panthera that might in fact turn out to be one of those boundaries he’d claimed not to have to Nathalie.

Still he has to wonder about why she feels she can’t. If it is just her responsibilities as a heroine and that he’s a villain, or if there is another man after all.

"Why?" He chances, "does someone already hold your heart? Chat Noir's father perhaps?"

Her hair seems to fluff up somehow. A Cat indeed apparently. She bares her teeth shining in the dark, "That's irrelevant."

Oh.

His little kitty cat is good. She's no Nathalie with her impenetrable walls that have taken years of careful erosion for him to learn as much as her as he has and still stand strong, but she is good at guarding anything more personal than the anger any heroine should have for the villain of the piece.

Though he supposes he's never tried quite this trick on Nathalie; who knows how she might react?

Still he's managed to hit something real there when he hadn't really expected to and now it feels like he's in completely different terrain.

Shoving aside his disquiet he grins, "so defensive. Tell me are the press right? Things aren't so rosy amongst your darling superfamily?"

Her shields slip back down, and his question. "The press would have it you're his father."

"Touché," he says, then "do you love him?"

He's surprised by how honestly the words come out. That wasn’t just about getting her Miraculous. He wants to know.

She raises her eyebrows, "Chat Noir's father? Yes."

And yet, his miraculous roils on his chest with turmoil, all isn't right in paradise. "Tell me about him?"

She crosses her arms, and inclines her head, "What? Are you actually serious?"

"There's no akuma anymore." he points out, "your little friend sorted that out."

She shudders, "please don't call her that."

Her considers Panthera again, and the fact that she’s never quite admitted to Chat Noir being her son. Just apparently the son of the man she’s in love with.

Then he considers what she’d look like with human eyes, with the blue he keeps going back to when he tries to imagine her out of costume.

"Little sister?" He offers instead.

Both eyebrows raise this time. "Seriously?"

Perhaps not. Though she could still be acting despite there being no emanation from her heart suggesting fear at being caught out.

Still he shrugs. "Half-sister. It doesn't seem impossible. Perhaps it's not Chat Noir who recruited you after all."

She looks away. "I'm hardly going to answer that."

"You're a strange one to work out," he muses taking another step closer to her and offering her a hand, "want to go make your beau jealous with me?"

She makes a noise somewhere between an expulsion of air and a laugh, "I don't think that's going to work out the way you hope. No, he’s had his chance to notice me and he…" a tilt of her head, “hasn’t. I’m certainly not deluded enough to think a walk with you is going to change anything there.”

Personally Gabriel has always found jealously quite an effective motivator. It would have taken him twice the time to marry Emilie if he hadn’t thought Colt was interested in her rather than Amélie at first.

And it’s that same acid hatred in his gut that makes him ask, "Well he sounds like an idiot then. What makes him so special? Chat Noir's father?"

Panthera just looks away from him. Dangerous really when he could still attack. It this is trust then he feels uncomfortable with it.

"He doesn't know his son is Chat Noir." She says staring into space instead of answering his question. "And I don't know if I should tell him."

With all that stupid article in his head, and the guilt he has for deceiving her, here he doesn’t deceive her. Parent to possible parent, quasi-parent, whatever she is to Chat Noir he says, "I'd want you to tell me. In his position"

"You're Shadow Moth. Of course you'd want to know. You want his Miraculous." But there’s less venom in her voice than just exhaustion. Like Nathalie. If his guilt wasn’t bad enough.

"Well. Yes." But her assumption prickles at some soft spot in him, "it would be convenient but I still wouldn't want that to be the case. To think I'd been that blind. That I'd fought him, that," his voice cracks.

Everything he’d pushed away earlier, the scenarios he’d imagined, suddenly flood back in full force. A bruise blossoming across Adrien’s cheekbone from his cane, a cut destroying the perfect symmetry of his son’s face.

She reaches up and cradles the side of his mask, her fingers landing so softly on the same place he’d imagined wounding his son on, "why do it then? You don't know he isn't. And even if he is then, he's always been someone's son. This, this isn't all of you is it? You’re a better man this this I’m sure of that. So why do you do it? Why do you hurt my boy?"

He swallows, and steps back out of her hold. "You can't expect me to admit that."

"And you wanted me to tell you about Chat Noir's father."

"That's hardly equal." He tries to argue, he hadn’t mean to ask for any identifying details. He’d just wanted to know.

Panthera looks him right in the eyes. "It is to me. Good bye Shadow Moth."

Chapter 11: Nathalie IV

It’s not that Nathalie doesn’t appreciate what Plagg’s doing for her. It’s really not. And it’s not that she’s not willing to suffer through the side-effects. Not dying is pretty decent end result for a little bit of difficulty along the way, and as shown by her very situation she’s dealt with worse for less.

Only it is really really unhelpful for her limbs to decide to stop responding to her when she’s already trying to run back to the office to beat Gabriel and his non-insignificant advantage of a lift down to the place they’re both meant to be from his entry to the house.

Maybe it’s her own fault for not wearing the exoskeleton but it’s a irritant when she doesn’t really need it and besides she thinks then she’d just have been stuck in place anyway.

Though admittedly that might have been better than falling to the fall like she is now.

Time seems to slow down as the floor approaches, but thankfully her arms decide to obey the frantic signals coming from her brain, albeit sluggishly, like they’re moving through oil not air, and she manages to break her fall.

Hitting the ground still hurts, and she’s already taken a thump or two fighting the akuma even if Shadow Moth does seem to be trying to make them go easier on her.

Thankfully it doesn’t set her off coughing.

Not thankfully even as she rolls onto her side in an attempt to get to a position she can get up from Gabriel appears from the floor.

“What on earth?” His brows draw together, “Nathalie?! Are you ok?”

“I tripped,” she tries play to it off as nothing serious, to inject a little self-deprecation into her tone, as if she’s annoyed but not worried.

In fact she’s very worried. Her right leg still isn’t responding and if it doesn’t soon then Gabriel will notice. She has the cover of the damage from the Peacock Miraculous of course, but if she keeps having episodes immediately after Panthera’s been out in action he could notice a pattern.

Neither of them had with Adrien, something she’s still reproaching herself for, but unfortunately she spends a lot more time with Gabriel then either of them do with Adrien.

Really the question she’d like answered is how exactly his school never bothered to report all his absences to them. The confusion of an akuma action she guesses, or maybe more likely the administration wanting to protect the hero it’s so proud of.

Or heroes.

The fact that the other heroes are all connected to Adrien, and that Ladybug seems to be around the same age is something Nathalie is very carefully not thinking about given that she’s fairly certain at this stage that Plagg isn’t lying and the Wish isn’t the panacea she’d hoped for.

That’s an issue for another time with how Gabriel’s running over to her.

“You’re bleeding.”

“What?” She didn’t hit the ground that hard, and when she checks her hands in confusion they’re absolutely fine.

“Your nose.”

There is a dampness above her lip now he says that and as she reaches out with her tongue curling it upwards she comes into contact with a familiar warm saltiness.

“Nathalie,” he sounds appalled, and she thinks he really must have let Emilie handle the lion’s share of Adrien as a child if something like that disturbs him so much, given that he’s better than you’ve expect with her medical needs due to Emilie.

She just shrugs at him, “Do you have a tissue?”

Gabriel shakes his head, “Come on,” and half-carries half-helps her down into the recessed seating around the catwalk and then down…

On to his lap.

Breathe Nathalie. She reminds herself. You can do this.

“Here,” he pulls a handkerchief from his pocket but he doesn’t actually give it to her, holding it to her nose himself and supporting her back with his other arm.

Since it leaves her arms free she takes advantage to pinch her nose and incline her hand in and attempt to halt the bleeding.

It takes more effort than she’d like but at least this attacks of non-response from her body are only ever temporary. Plagg thinks they’ll probably go away. Probably.

“Oh my poor Nathalie,” Gabriel repeats her name again.

Harshly she wonders if she does it to remind himself exactly which woman he has in his arms right now. Because between her, Emilie, and Panthera she thinks it must get confusing.

That’s the uncomfortable thing, this thing she hates the most; that Gabriel, whatever other reasoning he might use for his actions, is clearly attracted to Panthera and yet he holds her the same way, with the same care, regardless of whether she’s wearing the mask or not. It makes her feel like he could want Nathalie too, especially with all the nonsense Plagg’s put in her head, and Adrien’s well-intentioned encouragement on top of that.

Makes her feel like maybe there is a way out where he chooses her and gives up as if Gabriel has ever been a forgiving man, and their platonic relationship isn’t built on his trust in her.

“This should never have happened to you,” he continues, and she’s gladder than ever to know that he doesn’t spy on her emotions as Nathalie.

She can feel his lips against the top of her head as he says, “one day I’ll beat Ladybug,” she notices how conspicuously absent Panthera’s name is, “and then I’ll fix all of this. This will never have happened to you, you’re be healthy and,”

“Umph,” she tries to express her disagreement despite her blocked nose.

“What?”

“You can’t do this for me,” for Gabriel and through him for Emilie she might be able to let this go but the idea that one day Adrien will be able to take his ring back, and then Gabriel will fight him for Nathalie’s sake is utterly unacceptable to her, “this is for Emilie isn’t it?”

She hates that she sounds so unsure when she says it but Shadow Moth comes on so strongly to Panthera, and then Gabriel holds her like this, and it’s hard to remember that she’d believed in his devotion to his wife once.

That she’d loved him for that.

Because it feels like then she shouldn’t love him anymore but she thinks that…maybe she still does because every uncertain beat of her heart in his presence says that and that’s a harder thing to deal with.

“Yes,” Gabriel’s voice deepens, rough with grief, “you know that’s why I started this, but surely, Nathalie, you can’t believe I’d leave you alone and swan off in my own happy bubble. I’m going to make this right. When I’m done no one will have suffered? You understand that don’t you?”

Gravity drags down on her, much more than it should do, out of nowhere and her head lolls forward as she mumbles, “what if the Wish isn’t that simple?”

Gabriel’s constant circles on her back with his hand stop, “What?”

“What if, what if we’ve underestimated the price? Or what if we can’t control it that much?”

Slowly his hand starts to move more slowly against her again, she feels his swallow against the side of her scalp, “you’re read something?”

“I’ve found new information,” she doesn’t lie, it doesn’t matter how she’s obtained it, the matter is she has, “we might not be the first to attempt the Wish.”

“I’d doubt we are,” Gabriel scoffs, “I might strive to originality but I’m not so pigheaded as to think I’m the first man in the world ready to rewrite reality to regain someone I love.”

“Or woman,” Nathalie corrects automatically before she realises what she’s just implied.

“Or woman,” Gabriel agrees with an even tone, and not the slightest jolt against or under her suggesting he’s aware of what she’s just almost confessed.

He continues, “and you think they managed it? Or is it just a warning?”

“It’s hard to know.” True Plagg has reasons to obscure the truth from her, “texts from those times can be unreliable,” she’s looked into it herself since Plagg had said what he had but had the Pharoah’s second wife died for his first and that, that’s not so bad, if that’s why Gabriel’s interest in her alter-ego has been leading up to she could cope. She’d be out of all this, all of this shame and pain and at rest and everyone she cares about would be happy.

And yet she can’t throw it all aside because she won’t manage to get that, if it’s someone Gabriel truly, actually, loves then it’s Adrien and she can’t allow that at it.

“Nathalie?” Gabriel prompts her, and she’s been silent too long she’s pressed this all up.

So there’s only one thing she can say.

“They’re consistent.” True. Plagg doesn’t waver on what he tells her and she’s yet to find a single thing to contradict it. “You can change the world but you can’t control how. What if…”

But her voice fails her unable to even speak the words into mind.

“What if what exactly? Nathalie, what if?”

“What if it stops Adrien’s existence?” She blurts out, “if Emilie lives because she never got the Peacock and you divorced as a result, or if you two never married, or never met? I don’t know maybe you’d lucky and the two of you just never met me but,”

“No,” Gabriel interrupts her, “I don’t want to diminish your skills my dear, I doubt anyone else could have jumped professions like you have, but I was determined to get Emilie those gems. If it hadn’t been you then it would have been someone else.”

He’s not wrong. She’s good. She might have done it faster than anyone else. But she’s not big headed enough to think she’s the only person who could ever find those Miraculous.

Which unfortunately destroys the idea she’s been halfway playing with of maybe working out a way to steal the Rabbit instead. It’s just too complicated, too likely to fail.

“I wish you weren’t right there,” she responds eventually. And she does, and not even just for Adrien’s sake or for Gabriel’s sake or indeed Emilie’s sake but for her own.

In a world where she’d never met the Agrestes, where Tomoe Tsurugi had never recommended her to them, it feels like everything would have been so much simpler. She’s not stupid enough to think it would be perfect or necessarily normal, it probably still wouldn’t be with her past, but the Agrestes are extraordinary and she doesn’t think it’s any exaggeration to say it never would have become as complicated as this.

“I…yes,” Gabriel swallows, “do you really think Adrien could be at risk?”

“I think we can’t trust that he wouldn’t be.”

His head makes an aborted movement back, like he was about to look at Emilie’s portrait.

“I couldn’t bring her back to that.”

“She mightn’t,” she stops herself. She wants Gabriel to not do this. To think about what he’s doing.

“Mightn’t what?”

She fumbles through an attempt to explain without making it sounds like a desirable outcome, “If you recreated the world then I suppose you’d recreate every person in it, me, Emilie, everyone else, we be a different version of ourselves who wouldn’t know better.”

“A version of you that wasn’t like this,” Gabriel sounds suddenly almost angry, and she’s not prepared for how his voice changes, “why would you tell me this Nathalie?”

“Sir?”

“What do you want me to do?”

“I- I wanted you to have all the information before you made a mistake.”

“Made a mistake? If what you’re saying is right then I’ve already made the mistake! I haven’t been Hawk Moth for fun, bridges have been burnt, everything but bringing Emilie back has been allowed to slide, even my own son has fallen down my priority list, and you, look at what’s happened to you, and now you tell me I can’t fix any of this without the chance of making things worse?”

“We can still fix things. The Miraculous isn’t everything. The company’s still successful, you can make it moreso, build friendships back up and-“

“That was Emilie’s role.”

She flinches back from the sudden increase in volume.

“And it doesn’t matter,” he continues, “because maybe some of it can be salvaged, maybe I take control of Adrien’s upbringing back even though I will never ever be able to be for him want his mother was but I will never be able to save her, never be able to save….you.”

Acids burns at the back of her throat at her own stupidity at not hiding her symptoms better. And her selfish pride stopping her from just wearing the damned exoskeleton. That if she’d just accepted help earlier then he wouldn’t be looking at her right now.

“You don’t have to save me.”

“Don’t I? you-“

She stops him. “Got cocky and did myself some damage.”

“Exactly.”

“I’m not getting worse,” she says firmly, “it might be harder for you to tell because it’s so minor a change, or not change. But I can tell how I’m feeling and short of me putting that Miraculous back on I am not going to die.”

This time his voice is quiet, the raging man replaced by a scared little boy, “Emilie said that too.”

“Emilie didn’t know what was happening to her until too late. And Emilie was-“ Nathalie’s had some uncharitable thoughts about the choices Madame Agreste had made over the years and her frivolity when it came to the Miraculous but now certainly not the time to air any of that. She should probably never air any of that to Gabriel.

“Stubborn,” Gabriel fills in for her, and yes that does sum up much of it, then he adds, “like you. I seem to be cursed in that way. You. Emilie. Adrien. Even his bloody bodyguard. None of you listen to me at all do you?”

“I do listen to you. But keeping you safe is more important to me than keeping your ego safe.”

“And yet you’re asking me not to do the same for you.”

“I’m not worth the danger of making that wish.”

He doesn’t respond at first, and then with a shuddering breath, the words seem to burst out of him. “Emilie would say she wasn’t either.”

She waits.

There’s nothing she can say that mightn’t unbalance Gabriel from the precarious tightrope leading to the solution she’s hoping for that he’s just stepped on to.

“She was quite happy for me to go after the two Miraculous you know,” he says, and there’s the ghost of a smile on his lips, “and I think she’d have liked the idea of the world being remade around her, for her. And she’d accept my sacrifice,”

Nathalie’s heart jumps into her throat, “your?”

There’s the slightest head of his head, “I considered it you know. I didn’t want it. I wanted her back with me. To enjoy the world I made for her. But if it took that, then, I thought it would be been romantic at least. Something worthy of a legend. She’d see that.”

“Gabriel,” now it’s her voice breaking.

He gives no sign of hearing her, “but Adrien. If I killed our son she’d never forgive me. Anything less than that and she could fix but if I snuffed him out of existence then she’d murder me herself if she knew. And even if she didn’t….I’d know. She wouldn’t be my Emilie if she could be happy without Adrien.”

“No,” that much Nathalie doesn’t have to force herself to concede about Emilie. Adrien had been a dearly wanted child by his mother. “She wouldn’t be.”

“Because my Emilie’s his mother.” He huffs, “that’s the real reason Panthera won’t give in to me you know?”

Nathalie’s truly lost at whatever connection he’s made there.

“She’s not a hero. Not at her core. There’s none of Ladybug’s self-righteousness there, she might actually be capable of understanding why I fight.”

“I don’t think she’d be impressed with the flirting then.” She can’t help but point out.

“Probably not. But no, the issue is Chat Noir.”

“Chat Noir?”

“That I’m his enemy is why I’m hers. I suppose I’m lucky I’ve never done anything worse to the boy.”

You don’t know how right you are there.

Nathalie reaches for Gabriel’s free hand, even though he’s never let go of the bloodied handkerchief, “you don’t have to do anything more to him.”

His tone goes dull and empty, lost of the conviction inhabiting it moments earlier, “you want me to give up too.”

“I’m not his mother but I,” love, “care a lot for Adrien too.”

“And that’s the only thing you’re frightened of going wrong with the Wish?”

“Well,” it takes a courage she didn’t think she had, for all she’s told Shadow Moth she loves Gabriel even if he doesn’t know that’s what she meant, “I wouldn’t want it to take you either.”

One moment his hand simply allows hers on his, and then it’s holding hers back, “And?”

“And?”

“Aren’t you forgetting something?”

Her frown almost hurts as she tries to work that out, though the pain might be from the recent burst blood vessel in her nose, “I don’t know what you mean.”

“Surely you don’t want to risk it taking you?”

“Why would it take me? You just told me that you two would have managed to get the Miraculous anyway.”

Gabriel lets go of her hand, “you’re the one telling me it’s unpredictable.”

“I suppose. It’s just not something I’d considered. That we might never had met seems probable but,”

“Do you wish,” there’s the slightest hint of a wince at his word choice, “that we hadn’t? I suppose you must. You wouldn’t be like this otherwise. Perhaps you that yearning of yours wouldn’t be a yearning.”

It’s harsh of her that she’s surprised that he remembers that admittance of hers. She’d thought between Emilie and Panthera he’s had no mind to spare for Nathalie beyond her health. Certainly not for her romantic life, or lack thereof.

“Don’t put words in my mouth.” She retorts and cringes a little that her irritation at herself is finding a target in him, “or try and blame yourself for my choices.” There’s plenty of blame for him for his sudden idiocy as Shadow Moth.

“Am I supposed to be happy about what’s happened to you? That you still might die?”

“I’m not going to die.”

“You don’t know that,” he snaps, “you’ve given me two unacceptable options here. Either I do nothing and let the world stay as it is with you crippled and Emilie gone, or I make the Wish and risk it going wrong. And we don’t even know that’s definitely the case, if it came from anyone other than you then I’d think it was a lie to make me surrender.”

“Not making the Wish doesn’t mean things have to stay as they are.”

“Yes, yes, I know you’d have me devote myself to the rest of my life.”

“That’s not what I mean.”

His eyes sharpen, “what do you mean?”

“The Miraculous isn’t the only magic in the world. The Wish isn’t even the only magic other than the standard abilities of the Miraculous.”

“Nothing else is strong enough to save you both.”

“We used to think that about fixing the Peacock Miraculous, and look where we are now. And Ladybug’s powers only seem to be growing more and more. Maybe we have more options than we thought.”

“Maybe we do,” his voice is strained, “but how we’d get the information I don’t know.” “We got the Butterfly and the Peacock didn’t we?”

“We did.”

“And anyway, maybe Ladybug won’t talk to you. But Panthera might.”

“I’m not sure Panthera is too pleased with me,”

Nathalie has to stifle a snort at that.

“But,” he continues, “if she thought that maybe this could bring an end to all of this. Then. Yes, I think that might make her talk.”

“It might even make Ladybug listen to her.”

“That I’m less sure of. But I suppose even pests must get tired. Having her time back might sway her. Hmm. Come on, let’s get you back to bed and you’re staying there all tomorrow.”

“No.”

“Yes. Because there’s no reason for you to get up to research these translations of yours and that’s your task for the foreseeable future. If I’m to change course it has to be because it’s the only option.”

Chapter 12: Nooroo I

The mood in the room is dark and oppressive.

So is the room itself, as Gabriel Agreste lies on his bed, with the lights off and the curtains shut.

The cavernous size of the bedroom doesn’t even offer the illusion of freedom to Nooroo as he flutters around in the darkness unable to see to the walls surrounding them. The butterflies that his master uses to make his akumas are freer than him.

Duusu stays close to his side. Neither of them wanting to attract Gabriel’s attention.

Their jailer seems unware of them. Lost in his own despair and angry. The two emotions fighting it out instead him.

To others he’s just a man lying on a bed.

To Duusu and Nooroo he’s a veritable storm. A hurricane of feeling. Holding the possibility to turn all that damage inside him outward and spread destruction over the city through the magic that binds them to him.

It’s hard when you feel things like Nooroo does not to be affected by it. He hates the man. But he pities him too. He wants to make things better for him. That’s what he does. Why he reaches out to those calling out to him to transform them into what they need to be.

What he’s forced to do now is a pervasion of his powers but unlike how poor Dusuu’s amoks are made and destroyed like nothing to none one’s benefit, not even Gabriel’s when it only gives the man enough hope to stay on this path of destruction even he doesn’t really enjoy, his at akumas might not be wanted but he still manages to make them, with Ladybug's aid, something of what people need. They can at least be a cry for help.

In that way he can claw back some of this for himself. Usually. Some of those who call out to him, like the girl who's more ally of sorts than victim of his master with the empty void inside of her looking for recognition, he can’t help.

Gabriel Agreste himself is much the same. He’s beyond Nooroo’s ability to do anything to help him climb out of the hole he’s dug himself.

If he akumatised himself now it wouldn’t be anything as controlled as the Collector has been. He doesn’t think his master would even know what he wanted, needed, in this case until the akuma took over and made him forget all those nuances, and those he loved, that might usually stay his down.

Nooroo stills as if he could disappear and avoid being called on if he’s not seen when he senses that Gabriel is about to talk. But there’s no command to transform him.

Instead Gabriel draws in a deep breath through his nose. “Did you two know?”

Duusu curls inward with a huff, “like you’d have listened.”

It makes him flinch a little. He’d forgotten, but when Duusu’s good relationship with Nathalie had been down to the broken Miraculous, that his fellow kwami has always had a sharper side than he has. Duusu no longer condones villainy but. Well. Duusu doesn’t feel bad for not being about to help Gabriel be better unless maybe for Miss Nathalie’s sake.

The man sits up, annoyance clearly drawn across his face, despite how little they can see. Tension clear in every muscle of his body.

“So you did,” it’s not the usual snappish dismissal Nooroo’s used to. This is still carry warning in every syllable, but it’s more considered more thoughtful, almost silky in place of the usual harshness.

It’s the type of tone that though he cannot understand why Nooroo has seen provoke a certain squirm Miss Nathalie, has felt, as much as he’s aware of anything when he’s transforming his master, from Panthera.

Humans seem have a connection between danger and attraction that Nooroo still doesn’t understand despite his millennia with them.

“Strange when the Guardians kept so much from you. Understandable, if unhelpful, but then why would they let you know this. Perhaps Nathalie’s idea of it being a warning is right, just a story to-“

“No.” Duusu interrupts with a confidence that Nooroo hopes comes from the Gabriel probably no being able to make them out. “They didn’t tell us. You’re right, they wouldn’t have. It’s sooo unfair.”

Now there’s that irriatated snap. “So? How do you know?”

“We felt it.” Duusu explains, “we just know. This isn’t the world as it was.”

“So it has been done before.”

“Yes,” Nooroo says quietly, “not for a long time now but…yes.”

“What happened?”

Nooroo wishes that he could be more helpful, wishes he could have been more helpful back then but, “we don’t know. Maybe Sass does, or Fluff but if they do they’re not telling. We just…we can feel that this isn’t how things are supposed to be Master. And the absence that’s lost from the world.”

“If I discover that’s you’re not telling the truth neither of you will eat until you’d too weak to transform me.”

“We are telling the truth,” Duusu lunges forward and Nooroo has to grab Duusu to pull the other kwami back as he looks ready to fly at their master to attack.

“Miss Nathalie wouldn’t lie to you,” Nooroo reminds him, and then remembers that’s not quite true or she’d not have been Duusu’s wielder for so long but still, “not like this. She wouldn’t tell you this if she didn’t think you needed to know it.”

“No,” Gabriel shakes his head, “Nathalie has every reason to want me to continue. To want me to win. She of all people has no reason to tell me not to go after the only thing that could heal her.”

“She’s doing better.”

“With the help of a machine,” Gabriel spits.

“That you got for her.”

The man huffs, “Hardly. Tomoe’s always loved a challenge and she’s hardly without reason to feel guilty for Nathalie’s condition too.”

With someone else what to say would be obvious but he knows just how volatile his master is. Still, for his own sake, and the sake of the world and his fellow kwamis he has to try.

“Maybe you should talk to Miss Nathalie about it? About what to do? She said you shouldn’t risk it and-“

“No.” Gabriel cuts him off, “Nathalie won’t admit weakness. It’s that damned stubbornness of hers. She’ll sit there and say, “I’m fine sir. Just make the right choice for yourself. And for Adrien. That will help me feel better. And not mention that she’s dying. Just like Emilie.”

Beside him Duusu startles and then frowns, “Mrs Emilie….I, I knew her.”

“You killed her,” Emilie’s husband curtly corrects.

Nooroo blinks and wonders if he’s aware that this is the first time he’s admitted that the woman Shadow Moth is fighting to save is essentially dead. That if she was taken out of that casket, or even if it lost power for too long, she would be.

In the circumstances, with him considering stopping more seriously, than ever before it’s not as reassuring as it should be.

“When my Miraculous was broken?” Duusu asks, “but why would she use it? She wanted to, I remember that. It was important. It was love. But why? She was so sick but it was….”

Gabriel almost blinds then turning on the light switch at the bed. “It was, what exactly?”

“Different to Miss Nathalie.” Duusu says eventually sounding unsure but still not scared enough.

“Different in terms of their health? Or in why I did it.”

“I don’t know,” Duusu whines, suddenly on the verge of tears, “it was like a nightmare.”

Nooroo feels for his fellow Kwami but Duusu feels things very strongly. Any volatility wasn’t wholly due to the broken Miraculous.

“Useless,” Gabriel hisses. “Nooroo, go check on Nathalie.”

“Of course master,” Nooroo flies through the walls as fast as he’s able to because, he’s just been allowed to leave his master’s side, to go out of Gabriel’s sight and this is new new new, and it’s not freedom, not liberty. He has a task.

Nathalie has always been a weakness for his master. Save for Adrien she’s the only other person he’s seen a plan be halted for.

And now, unless the villain remembers to reinforce his old command, thanks for Gabriel’s concern for Nathalie Nooroo’s pretty sure he’s not bound by that anymore. That he can at least roam in search of Nathalie now.

His flight is so fast he doesn’t manage to come to the stop he should of in Nathalie’s room elegantly just inside it.

Instead he slows over her bed, coming to a stop in front of her face.

This would be fine where it only Nathalie in the room.

And had Adrien been here he’d not have been able to do it. The laws that bind the Kwamis to the Miraculous would have stopped him entering. Gabriel’s command to never let his son see him is still in place.

But he’d not said anything about avoiding other kwamis. Clearly thinking his order keeping him by his side sufficient.

And floating between Nathalie, and the bottle of wine and the plate of cheese on the trolley in front of her is, “Plagg?”

“Hey Nooroo,” the cat Kwami looks just as chagrined as he should do, right here in the house Nooroo’s been living with and looking far too unshocked at his own appearance, “long time no see.”

“You! Here, how?”

Nathalie fumbles for something in the draw at her side and then pulls out a thimble. “Would you like some wine?”

Nooroo glares at little at her, for knowing this, knowing Plagg, his friend, was here and not telling him, but still, “yes please.”

She pours a little wine out for him and hands it over, even as Plagg comes over and looks a little serious in that way in does once in a hundred years, “I’m working to get you out of this. I promise.”

“But,” then Nooroo looks at Nathalie again, her hair half fallen out of her bun and her glasses up on top of her head. That familiar pointed chin, and slope of her nose, the curve up of her cheekbone.

Features he’s seen across the atelier day after day.

Features he’s been seeing on Gabriel’s screen as the man attempts to make sense of the woman of so intrigues him.

“You’re Panthera,” he almost drops his wine.

It does suddenly explain an awful lot about heroine. Why she doesn’t hate Shadow Moth the way she should. How she indulges him. The way his master is drawn to him. The comfort the man feels in her presence.

It makes sense, and yet she’s also the last person he’d ever expected for the same reason she’d be the last person Gabriel would ever expect. He’d thought her loyalty was forged in iron and now it suddenly seems flexible. Not nothing or she’d have turned Shadow Moth in, or taken his Miraculous, but certainly not what he’d thought it was.

“You can’t tell Gabriel.” She says.

“You know I can’t,” he points out, “Kwamis can’t reveal holders.”

“I thought that was only your own.”

“Trust me,” Plagg laughs but there’s anger underneath, Plagg has always pushed at the boundaries holding them back despite his lackadaisical front, “I’d have told the boy who Ladybug was ages ago if I could, but Tikki would never let me.”

“I thought that was Chat Noir and Ladybug’s choice? In case we,” she went pale.

“Kwamis’ can’t be forced to tell,” Nooroo offers before Plagg can upset her more, because that won’t help at all with the current waves of repulsion he’s feeling from her. “But, it sounds like Plagg and Tikki chose not to too.”

“Superplum’s more worried about it than me but,” Plagg shrugs.

Nathalie takes a large swallow of wine. “Of course she is. A-“

It should have been obvious the moment he worked out Panthera was Nathalie.

“Adrien’s Chat Noir.” Nooroo finishes for her.

Nathalie shuts her eyes. “I’d deny it if I thought that would work right now.”

“It wouldn’t.” Nooroo agress, “he’s the only one you’d want to protect like that. And not knowing who Ladybug is protects him as well as her.”

Plagg mutters something inaudible.

His new (temporary? Nooroo isn’t sure) holder shakes her head, “Adrien is Gabriel’s son. He wouldn’t-, he’d think he’d know how to demand answers from Adrien. Ladybug wouldn’t have that protection.”

Plagg’s teeth are bared, “And if he didn’t have any success in getting them?”

“He’d ground him forever. Take him out of school. Cut him off from contact with his friends. Not let him out of his room but he wouldn’t, he wouldn’t do more than that.” Her voice is firm, broking no arguments.

Only Nooroo has them and he thinks they might need airing, so he takes a swallow of his wine and says, “he might akumatise him. He wants to sometimes.”

“But he doesn’t. He deliberately doesn’t listen to Adrien’s emotions. And I mean, he asked me.”

“You haven’t betrayed him,”

Nathalie shrugs, lifting a hand with a silver ring with three green gems on her finger.

“You hadn’t then.” He amends.

“No.” Her eyebrows arc down, “I hadn’t then.”

The resignation and guilt in her voice are oddly familiar. Which makes him second-guess why she’d felt them before choosing to aid Plagg and Adrien against the man she loves and he’d thought, would follow down through every mistake he made.

Nooroo floats closer to her face, “you see him more clearly than I thought. Why do you love him then?”

“You know,” she huffs, “of course you know, you’re too close to me not to. Everyone seems to know my feelings that these days. Except for Gabriel, and even he does when he doesn’t know it’s me. But you know I’m not a good person either. And I saw the man he used to be, and the man he could be too.”

Nooroo takes his time to turn those words over in his mind, then considers what he knows of the man forcing him to do the wrong thing over and over again and, “you’re right. He’s capable of being better. And It would be better for everyone if he did.”

Plagg humphs, “well I hope so seeing as I’m staking my kid’s future on that.”

“Thanks.” Nathalie says, but she doesn’t seem bitter.

“Hey, you know I’m not in this for you lady even if it would be better for you too, which you know by the way.”

That gets a snort, “Nooroo can tell you that it wouldn’t.”

“I don’t understand.”

“Plagg thinks that I can get Gabriel to fall in love enough with Panthera that he’ll give up, and then me, him and Adrien can just live as one happy family. Obviously that’s not going to work but,” she grabs a piece of the cheese from the plate before Plagg can object, “I’ve been speaking to Plagg and to Ladybug and well…you saw my attempt at getting him to stop.”

She then popped the cheese in her mouth as if by eating it violently enough she could cow Plagg or at least distract herself from the world.

“You’ve unsettled him,” Nooroo says. “But he is thinking about it. That’s a good thing I think.”

Her face softened a little, “how is he?”

“Not good.”

She frowned, “I’d go to him but…”

Nooroo wishes he could take that weight of her but he has to agree. “Usually that would help but now….”

“I don’t get it,” Plagg says, “isn’t this the perfect time to go to him? She goes and makes him feel better, and he realises that maybe he’s got an option more available to Panthera, and boom! Maybe he starts to have some realisations.”

Nooroo comes to sit above her prostheses, “he needs her, but seeing her will only make him feel guiltier and more determined to succeed. Nathalie’s appearance could very well make him chose not to give up.”

“Gabriel doesn’t see boundaries,” Nathalie mumbles, “if he thinks it’s necessary he’ll tell himself there’s a way around the cost of the Wish.”

“And you tell me he doesn’t care about you.” Plagg rolls his eyes.

Nooroo blinks at that. “Why would say that? He worries about you all the time.”

“When he remembers,” Nathalie corrects, and she’s not wrong but then she’s hardly the only important thing Gabriel can get too drawn into his ideas to remember. Adrien of course too, but his actual work for another, and even on some occasions Emilie. When he gets into being a villain rather than remembering why he’s being one. Never mind how Nooroo despairs of how his captor seems to not notice when he’s hungry or sleep deprived.

He’s not supposed to have to worry about someone who’s currently misusing his Miraculous.

“And I never said he doesn’t care about me,” Nathalie said, “just that he doesn’t love me, he won’t love Panthera when he works out she’s me, and he’ll never get over the betrayal. Let me work out how to convince Gabriel otherwise. Nooroo’s just told us my methods are working.”

Nooroo reached out to where his master lies in a turbulent agony, and that yearning for someone by his side. “I’d better go back before he comes looking for me. But it’s not as dark a future for you as you think.”

The flash of hope in her is small. Barely an ember under all her attempts to smother it down. But it’s there. Ready to be coaxed to a flame.

“Don’t you start.”

“He’ll be furious. But he wouldn’t want to live without you either. And I wouldn’t hold his attraction to Panthera against him either. How he acted with her made no sense to me until I realised she was you.”

“What?” Nathalie tries to push herself out of bed to follow him, “Nooroo you get right back here now, NOOROO,”

Nooroo doesn’t wait. His master is getting more and more angry and he really doesn’t feel like him and Nathalie meeting in their current states of minds would go well. Not for them long term plans.

Behind him Plagg laughs, and then she screeches, “Plagg, claws out,” loudly enough he can only hope his master didn’t hear it.

But reckless as she can be he doubts she’d come after him now. Not when she must know that would make it too easy for his master to take her Miraculous.

And there’s her emotions seem stationary behind him, not strengthening in the way that heralds their owner coming closer, as he enters the master bedroom.

“She’s well sir,” he reports.

“Why did that take so long?”

“She was asking after you. And she fed me.”

Gabriel presses at his temples, “she’s not asleep then.”

“No,” Nooroo admits, “but she seems relaxed and surprisingly well,” which he should have asked Plagg about it occurs to him know, “she doesn’t seem in any danger tonight.”

“She’s not coughing?”

“She’s,” he yelps and forgets his words, as an energy not familiar for a long time runs through him.

His master looks down at his chest at the recurrent glow ebbing and rising there.

He puts a hand to his Miraculous as if to take it off, “what’s happening?”

“Someone’s trying to contact you through your Miraculous,” and of course, as Mayura Nathalie would have known the feel of it to grab onto, but he doesn’t know why she’d do this now.

Except that they’re agreed that Gabriel needed someone with him. And Nathalie couldn’t do it.

But they’d not said anything of the like about Panthera.

Clever.

“Can they find me through it?” His master asked.

“They can’t.” Nooroo reassures then.

The man shrugs with a smirk, “then, Nooroo, Dusuu, double transformation.”

Chapter 13: Gabriel V

Gabriel shuts the door behind him, and very carefully doesn’t think about the concerned look in Nathalie’s eyes as he’d announced he was having an early night.

It’s becoming a habit.

Carefully not thinking about Nathalie.

Paris is repaired by that bug every time she manages to foil one of his plans. If Gabriel just… decided to stop then the city would continue on with its life perfectly unchanged.

Nathalie would not.

What he’s allowed to happen to Nathalie is permanent. He doesn’t even know if she’ll have a life ruined by the Miraculous to live afterwards.

After all he has Emilie’s situation to make it quite clear just how badly using a broken Miraculous can go and now it seems like maybe he can’t fix the world anyway he can’t understand how he could ever have thought it was OK to let Nathalie use that Miraculous too, even if she’d forced his hand on certain occasions.

He doesn’t know how Nathalie can not blame him.

Besides his bed a photo of himself, Emilie and Adrien glints in the warm golden evening light. It seems to give life to his wife’s eyes. Making them flash in anger the way they had when she’d argued with him.

“I’m sorry Emilie,” he mumbles to the portrait in a way that he’s not managed to do to the real woman in her casket.

He’s been doing a lot of careful not thinking about his wife too.

It’s been easier than not thinking about Nathalie.

Another thing he’s been trying not to dwell on.

It’s natural after all. Emilie’s been gone for what feels like so long now while Nathalie is still here slipping away under his eyes. She’ll have periods where she seems to be doing better, and he’ll let himself hope. Let himself think that maybe it would be alright just to let himself stop. And then he’ll find her on the floor again, or with blood streaming from her nose.

Lying down on the bed he takes the photo of his family in his hands.

“You’d blame me for stopping wouldn’t you?” he asks the silent image of the woman who was supposed to be the love of his life for all of both their lives but now lies emotionless and cold deep below the ground, “but you’d blame me more if I killed our son to bring you back wouldn’t you?”

He gasps a little as he says it. Such a clear statement of what he’d be doing. His hands might never physically touch his son. His eyes might never see the light disappear from those ones so like his mother’s. His ears might never hear his child’s final breath.

Gabriel would still have been the one to kill Adrien anyway.

Even if he didn’t remember.

And even if he knew he wouldn’t remember, knew he wouldn’t carry that guilt, knew that Emilie would smile at him without any censure, knew that in truth he wouldn’t be murdering his son as much as stopping him coming into existence in the first place he still couldn’t do it.

Fatherhood has never come naturally to him. He’s never had that instinctual connection to his son that Emilie had had. He’s not able to be parent his wife was. There’s nothing growing to fill the gap left by Emilie between him and his own flesh and blood the way a bond he’s trying to ignore the development of is growing between Adrien and Nathalie.

When Emilie had looked so excited about the idea, luminous in a way there was nothing he could do but be sucked in like any moth to her flame, she’d convinced him it would be easy to be a father. That it would all fall into place once he had his son in his arms.

It wasn’t. It didn’t.

Gabriel has tried, even before Emilie left, to make up for the fact he doesn’t understand the boy in the slightest. That he looks at this child so like Emilie but instead sees a stranger. That the impulse to hug him, or ruffle his hair like she used to never comes until Adrien is out of the room and he’s suddenly aware that he’s missed the moment.

Instead he’s done everything he can to fulfil the other parts of fatherhood. The less touchy- feely bits.

Adrien is provided with anything he could need, and without him needing to ask and whine about it like a brat the way Audrey and André’s daughter does. His room might seem excessive to others but not only did Gabriel not work himself to the bone to not give his family a better life than his parents had given him, but in truth it’s a necessary provision for a child when it was so unsafe to let him outside when he was younger.

The boy has food prepared for him by a gourmet chef who ensures he gets all the nutrients he needs. Sometimes Gabriel hopes for Adrien’s sake that this doesn’t cause his son to end up taller than him. He doesn’t wish the nightmare that is ov”er-short doorframes on anyone.

Adrien is getting every education, every bit of training to prepare him for adulthood. The money from his modelling is set aside as his. His grades are ensured to be top-notch. He’ll go into the world with his fame already assured, and hobbies that will let him flow around the elites of the world naturally without it fitting like an awkwardly fitting way of clothes like it always did for Gabriel until he became rich enough to avoid all of that. No one will tell Adrien his future is nothing more than physical drudgery and anything more is girlish fantasies.

And Adrien will grow up with an understanding of rules. Of the necessity of not embarrassing himself, of obliging others even if he goes behind their back later. And most importantly he’ll never have broken that weak weak rope behind which Gabriel can feel his own father’s anger in himself, can feel the urge to punish his son properly the way he had once been. To treat Adrien the way he does Ladybug and Chat Noir.

That rope frays sometimes but strangely enough these days there’s an emptiness behind it as he realises that no he’s not his father, and no he has no desire at all to go further, never wants to cause Adrien’s death.

He wants his son to live.

And he wants him to live in a way that’s happier, yearns for it in an echo of how he hears the same from Panthera when she talks about how things could be for Chat Noir in a world without Hawk Moth.

He just has no idea how to achieve that.

No idea if he wants to give up on Emilie if he gives up on the Wish or to chase after other dreams of saving her.

No idea how to save Nathalie.

So he puts the photograph down on it’s face, transforms, and calls the woman he does in fact know exactly what he’d have to do to please the way he’s got used to doing these days.

Panthera sounds a little out of breath as she answers, “Shadow Moth?”

“Is this a bad time?”

“No,” she breathing steadies out a bit, “sorry- Chat Noir just wanted to talk to me. I had to run to answer.”

He chuckles at the image even as the something in his guts is unsettled. “It might be a little awkward if he caught you talking to me.”

“That’s an understatement.” He wishes he could see the chagrined look on Panthera’s face, “he’d probably do something stupid like try and steal the ring from me and go fight you.”

“That’s sort of admirable,” he admits despite himself. He’d feel different in her shoes. Adrien would be grounded for life for a stunt like that, but he can’t blame a child for wanting to defend his maybe-mother.

“It’s stupid.” Panthera retorts. “It’s not like us talking is a danger to anything and anyway. Well. It doesn’t matter.”

“It’s sweet that he cares that much about you.” He concedes. He can give his enemy that much.

“Yeah,” she sighs, “I guess it is.”

“What was your anyway?”

He can imagine that little furrow between her eyes visible even under her mask, “What?”

“You said us talking like this isn’t a danger and anyway, but then you didn’t finish.”

“Oh.”

“You don’t have to say. I just thought. Well you’re always listening to my woes,” Gabriel thanks his lucky stars that Panthera had rang him that first night and reached out a metaphorical hand to pull him out of his despair. “It only seems fair that I should listen to yours.”

“I wish other people could see the version of you I do,” she says tiredly.

“Oh I’d hate to ruin my reputation besides….”

“I know. And I also know you haven’t sent out any akumas recently.”

“Sometimes I wish that you’d never confirmed what the kwamis had told me,” if she thinks it odd that he’d listen to her and the kwamis he’s spared no care for so far she hasn’t said, and he’s kept Nathalie, the fact that this idea has come from someone he trusts implicitly his own little secret.

“No you don’t.” There’s no hesitation in her response, and no uncertainty in it either. She could almost be Nathalie with her trust in him.

“No I don’t,” he agrees tiredly, the image of Adrien lying in Emilie’s casket haunting him again, “even if it would be easier.”

“A lot of things would be easier if everything wasn’t like this.”

“You’re right there.”

“So what did you call me about? I feel like we’ve got off topic.”

“Not at all. I just wanted to hear your voice my darling cat.” He’s got dangerously reliant on their little chats as a way to keep himself grounded.

There’s silence down the line, only the sound of her breathing in his ears almost as if she’s next to him in his bed telling him that Panthera is still there.

“Panthera?” He queries, “is there a problem with me calling now? If you dropped everything because you thought it was something more important than that you don’t have to stay. Goodness knows I know about having a busy schedule. I could message you you know,”

“No no,” she says in a hurry, “I’ve told you you can’t message. I let Chat Noir borrow the ring to contact Ladybug that way, and,”

“He can’t see messages from me yes,” his body feels restless on the bed. He’s used to having secrets. Not being one. And part of his pride is offended at the idea that a woman wouldn’t want it be known she’s dating him. He’s the Gabriel Agreste for god’s sake. It’s not like he’s been reminded by multiple women that he’s a catch whom they wouldn’t mind comforting on the few instances he’s left the house.

Not that he’s dating Panthera.

And to her he’s only Shadow Moth before and he’d assume there’s far less people out there lining up to date him.

“It’s not.” There’s an exhale of air, “I’m happy to be your conduit to Ladybug but…maybe this is dangerous. The rest of the talking. Us.”

“I don’t understand.”

“Maybe. On our first encounters, you enjoyed flirting with me. It amused you.”

He can’t follow her train of thought at all. “Yes?”

“But I’m not an option for you. Not really. Maybe this, this joking with me and confiding in me, is stopping you from finding something real with someone you actually know in your real life.”

“Why aren’t you an option?”

The temptation there has only got stronger since it had started to look like the Wish isn’t an option. Because if the Wish can’t be done then Emilie is dead, and there’s nothing wrong with him starting something with a pretty woman he respects and doesn’t really want to hurt at all who clearly feels something to him both physically and emotionally.

“I’m not Panthera. And I’m,”

“In love with Chat Noir’s father.” He finishes for her before he has to hear those words from her mouth again.

“Yes.”

“Was that your anyway?” He asks, “Don’t think I didn’t see you trying to weasel out of answering that question.”

“My anyway? I suppose in a way.” There’s a rustle and it strikes him suddenly that maybe Panthera is also in bed, and his checks heat up, “you see part of the reason Chat Noir objects to me and you as an idea,”

“Apart from the whole being a supervillain thing.”

“Oh hush. We both know you’re not the villain you pretend to be.”

“It can be fun though,”

“I know. And that would not help your case with Chat Noir. But you see he wants me to get together with his father.”

He feels like he’s bit into a lemon. “And you’re sure that won’t happen?”

“Certain. He’s in love with someone else. Or I thought he was. Now he seems to be falling for someone new, which I guess proves I never really had a chance.”

“He’s an idiot then but…” Gabriel hesitates, “if he’s noticing someone new that doesn’t mean he didn’t love whoever it was before. When you lose someone it feels like you’ll never feel anything positive again but then one day it starts to creep in.”

He feels a little lighter for having put his feelings into words. It’s not something he can admit to his other confidante. Nathalie disapproves of his actions with Panthera and he can’t blame her after everything he’d done to get Emilie back with her.

Her voice is almost painfully tender, “you’re sure there’s no one in your life?”

He shuts his eyes, “do you really think Ladybug would let me go free?”

Panthera humours his change in topic. “I don’t see how she could stop you if you just… stopped. It’s not like she knows who you are but if you wanted me to be a courier of those Miraculous then yes, I believe she would. I’d make her if I had to.”

“How on earth would you make her?”

Her warm laughter is a sound he’d like to hear for the rest of his life. Like maybe she could provide the warmth this house has been lacking for so long. That maybe he could hear Adrien’s too.

“That’s a secret.”

“What if I offered you a secret in return?”

“That’s a deal I might take.”

“Mayura.”

There’s a pause. “What about Mayura?”

“She’s dying and it’s my fault.”

She gasps. “My butterfly,”

“Don’t. It is. Her Miraculous was broken. And I think I’ve almost decided to not make the Wish. You know that. And she wants me to. But now every time I see her I think I’ve killed her. I, I struggle to look at her and I know that hurts her.”

He needs no Miraculous to tell him that. Nathalie, in her own way, has always valued being able to be there for him, to anticipate his needs before he knows she has them, and now he’s taking that from her too the way he’s taken everything else.

“You don’t know she’s dying.”

“I do. I’ve seen it before.”

“No I mean. I’ve, um,” he wishes he could see her face, see the truth of her feelings in from of him, see her white teeth dig into her dark lips like Nathalie does when she’s nervous. “spoken to Ladybug. I thought that might be it from what you’ve said before. What you wanted the Wish for.”

Ridiculously he sits up as if he’s going to go running to the two of them and sort this all out right now, “You can do something?”

“Not for. She can’t just back from the dead. But for Mayura, she’s not the first person in history to use a broken Miraculous.”

Yes, Gabriel knows that all too well.

Panthera continues, “and I don’t know about a cure but even Plagg’s quite capable of stopping the damage. She doesn’t have to die.”

“She doesn’t,” he marvels even as he can’t understand why he doesn’t feel the triumph of he should at this.

“Shadow Moth?” Panthera must have got worried at his silence.

Everything in this world has a price. “And what would you ask for for this boon?”

“For this? Nothing.”

“Nothing?” That can’t be real. Panthera is enormously giving, is too kind, in exactly the same way Nathalie is without it making either of them soft or without an acerbic wit.

“It’s more of a gift to her than to you really. I wouldn’t want to be as heartless as to let the friend of someone I care for die if I can help it.”

That brings a smirk back to his face. “You care for me?”

Not that he’d been totally unware of that but he doesn’t feel her emotions with the same intensity when they’re talking through their Miraculous as he does when she’s right there, and besides with a woman like her he knows he’s an achievement to get her to say it out loud.

“Ladybug’s got a crush on Chat Noir.” She says in a total non-sequitur.

“That was a very strange answer darling.”

“A secret for a secret. You offered yours. Here’s mine.”

“She’s not though. Not that I haven’t considered driving a wedge between them as a way to divide them, and frankly she’s not as good with you as she is with him. No offense.”

“None taken.” She sounds amused.

“But she’s also made it pretty damn clear she’s not interested in a relationship with the boy despite his overtures.”

“Urgh, don’t remind me. I’m been cringing about his approach ever since I discovered who Chat Noir is.”

He tilts his head as if she could see him. “But you like it when I act like that.”

“I would not say that.”

“Come on, where does Chat Noir get his love of puns from if not you?”

“His father,” she says tightly.

“Oh.” Once again he feels awkward, like he’s overstepped.

“Quite.”

“But you do like your bait and switch. I’m feeling a little robbed. Ladybug isn’t interested in Chat Noir that way, even if I doubt she’d take it well where he to show interest in someone else.”

“Hmm, Ladybug isn’t interested in Chat Noir, but she does have a crush on him. It’d be kind of funny if I didn’t feel sorry for them both.”

Gabriel laughs as it hits him, “I’m not. That’s hilarious. You mean she’s admitted to you she likes whoever he is behind the mask? That does make explain how you were going to get her to go along with you. Clever.”

“I don’t feel good about it.”

“But he idolises her. It’s hardly as if you’d be forcing him to date someone against his will. I’d jump at the option in his situation. Hell, so would my son. He could certainly do worse.”

That DJ boy comes to mind. Any teenager ready to throw secret parties that endangers the house’s power supply and that keeping Emilie in stasis has an automatic black mark against them.

“Umph,” she makes the strangest noise, “but wouldn’t you feel bad? For using him?”

“In the circumstances I think he’d volunteer.” Adrien’s even been worried enough about Nathalie to ask him about it. And unfortunately his son is also a fan of Ladybug.

“Well that does make me feel a little better.” Her voice sounds odd though.

“About my a-, Mayura though,” he decides to put the question of Adrien dating well aside, “if I wanted you to help her how would I do that? How would she contact you?”

“Um.” He can feel her thinking, “I suppose you could give her the Peacock Miraculous.”

“I’m sorry?”

“I mean. It’s fixed isn’t it? So she and I could sort something out? I’m sorry but much as I will help you I don’t think Ladybug would be happy if I gave you my Miraculous.”

“Probably not.” He agrees but that old fear at the idea of letting Nathalie near his Miraculous raises it’s head, “But you’re sure this won’t hurt her more?”

“I’m sure.” He doesn’t think she’s lying. “Will you do it?”

“You’ll hear from her if I do.” He doesn’t quite commit. It feels like an easy answer. Too easy. If he’s given up then it doesn’t matter if he has both Miraculous or neither. It feels like a trick but he doesn’t think Panthera is tricking him.

And he doesn’t think he can do Nathalie out of any chance of a cure.

Panthera is either oblivious to or ignoring his doubts. “Hmm, Shadow Moth?”

“Yes?”

“Look at her.”

He tries to make sense of that and gives out. “What?”

“Mayura,” she clarifies, “I know you’ll think I have no reason to say but but…as a woman, you’re hurting her treating her like you’re preparing for her being gone. Now you know she’s not just. Show that?”

“You’re right. I should.”

The slightest hint of swallow can be hear. “And I know this is overstepping. And you don’t have to answer but, what is she to you? Mayura? You said it wasn’t romantic but the way you’re bringing her up now…”

“Are you saying I can’t not want my best friend not to die?”

“No. No I didn’t mean. I’m sorry.” She blurts back.

“She is my best friend you know.” He stops her.

“I see.”

“Anything more is your fault you know?”

He can’t fault Panthera for her incredulous. “My fault?”

“You remind me of her sometimes. The two of you are oddly similar. It gets confusing. So yes. Your fault.”

Partially Adrien’s fault too to give credit where it’s due but sometimes he can’t help but look at Panthera but see Nathalie and vice versa.

“You make things confusing for me too you know.” She tells me, “so I think we’re even there.”

“I shouldn’t say this but I think that in another world out there without any Miraculous then maybe we could have been happy together you know.” He admits to her the secrets he’s not yet admitted to himself.

“I shouldn’t say this either but I agree.” Her answer is tired. Wistful with might-have-beens.

Gabriel shakes his head as if that will clear it of never-will-be fantasies. “I will go to Mayura now. You’re right. I’ve been cruel to her. “

“She’ll appreciate it I know.”

“Any suggestions? You know.” He can’t help the slight dig, “As a woman.”

“I don’t think you need any help from me there.” He imagines her saying it with a smile on her face, “Goodnight my not-so-little butterfly.”

“Goodnight my darling cat.”

Chapter 14: Nathalie V

Everyone tells her she’s doing the right thing. Plagg. Nooroo. Even Adrien and Ladybug would be delighted if Shadow Moth just disappeared. So why does she feel so selfish?

It shouldn’t. She knows that. Even if she somehow pulls off convincing him to give up without letting the truth of everything slip that hardly means Gabriel is going to be ready to move on with anybody anytime soon whatever he might say to Panthera. Because she's not stupid, she knows the very impossibility of a relationship with Panthera is why he says that to the heroine.

And though that’s no option, because despite the trust that’s grown between their other identities even as the understanding they’d shared as Gabriel and Nathalie has diminished he’s hardly as misled by lust as to consider sharing who’s behind his mask with her, there’s still no reason he’d pick Nathalie to move on with instead.

Unless that line about her and Panthera confusing him had meant something.

She fiddles with the Miraculous on her finger.

Perhaps Gabriel could plausibly chose her afterwards, because he’d think that she unlike the heroine is someone he could be honest with. Ironically enough. But it’s one thing to play second fiddle to Emilie when if she’s more than an assistant she’s still only a friend.

It would be quite another to be with him knowing that he really wants the more interesting version of her, but if he was to discover that was her he'd never want to be with either of them.

Because that’s the thing. Even if all the rest of it happened the way it does in the dreams she doesn’t acknowledge when she’s awake this will always be there between them. His secrets bound them. Her secrets will forever keep them apart.

What she has with him as Panthera is all she’s ever going to have with him, and acknowledging that makes her hate that they only call each other these days; that she’s too scared to throw caution to the wind and greedily take whatever he so freely offers her.

She swallows the lump in her throat and tries to tell herself this is a good thing, that this proves that however it might feel she’s not in fact being selfish. That’s she’s doing this because it’s the right thing to do, that it’s for Adrien, and not because it has any benefit to her.

But as she gives up trying to work on her own laptop and watches the boy doing homework further down the table as Plagg curls over his shoulder she can’t quite manage to smother her guilt.

“Adrien,” she calls.

His head jerks up. Eager, but shy about it. “Yes?”

But now he’s looking at her she feels stupid. What is she supposed to ask him? Is he ok with her taking away his only chance to see his mother again? Even though he is the very reason she can’t trust herself or Gabriel to make the Wish?

“Did you need any help with your homework?” she tries to ask as if that was all she'd ever meant to say.

From how his eyes narrow she's clearly not carried it off.

“No. But. Do you need help with anything?”

Nathalie can feel her expression tightening. She hadn’t realised her dilemma was so visible to Adrien and she can’t see how he thinks he can help with it. “Like what?”

He looks up and her, and god she has to save this boy. “I know it’s not easy being the Black Cat...”

She shrugs, she doubts the difficult parts of it for her have anything in common with the difficult parts for Adrien. After all, “there haven’t been many Akuma attacks.”

Gabriel spends most of his time transformed speaking to her these days. She guesses that could be seen as making her some sort of hero even if thinking of it in those terms does nothing but make her feel vaguely unclean.

“No,” Adrien’s face looks sour, “but you’re sure you don’t need help with Shadow Moth?”

She does her very best to mute her reaction to that. “I’m not sure that’s something you can exactly help with.”

From Adrien’s scowl she's clearly not quite succeeded but then his expression is hardly giving unpolitical brand ambassador either. So there’s that.

“That's what I'm worried about,” he mutters but audibly enough that she can hear him so easily she wonders if she was meant to.

If she was. Meant to. That is. It would be quite an Emilie little flourish. Nathalie hadn’t known her well, but she’d known her well enough to know she’d liked to do that sort of thing. Gabriel had used to hate it. He’s more of a blunt communicator.

“Would it be so bad?” She asks, “if he listens to me? He’s not a bad man Adrien.”

“Not a bad man?! Nathalie! Have you seen what he’s done? How he’s hurt my friends?”

She stiffens, “I’ve fought him too. And Ladybug’s power fixes everything.”

“The physical stuff yeah, but it doesn’t fix the rest of it.”

“The rest of it?”

Adrien looks every image the orator Gabriel wants him to be, “can you imagine what it’s like being akumatised?”

She doesn’t have to. She knows what that’s like actually.

Shivers go down her spine at the memory of the sensation of Hawk Moth’s voice inside her mind, and his fingers against hers and how he’d asked for and she’d given the loyalty that thrums through her veins and in return he’d reciprocated with trust, esteem, and power.

How as he’d empowered her it had felt like the boundaries between Gabriel and Nathalie were gone, that they were one, tied together by a purity of purpose because they weren’t Gabriel and Nathalie, they were HawkMothandCatalyst and they had one goal. She existed to fulfil him, and he couldn’t do it without her.

How for once she’d not felt insufficient in the slightest, that she was able to be exactly what she was needed to be, that she was wanted, that she was essential.

Akumatisation had been an expression of love for Nathalie, because what is loyalty if not a type of love?

And when the man she was loyal to, the man she loved, had needed a different solution to Catalyst then she’d broken her own akuma, broken the bond between them, as easily as a change of hair colour.

She’s aware her experience is likely pretty unrepresentative.

Not that no one else has ever said yes, give me the power I need to strike back, or even that no one else has volunteered, has called out to Hawk Moth asking to make their pain someone else’s problem.
