But Nathalie is the only one who was asked by Gabriel, not Hawk Moth, who know exactly what she was getting into, who suggested the entire idea of akumatising an ally in the first place.

So she knows what it’s like be akumatised.

But she doesn’t know how most people feel about being akumatised, and Adrien’s fierce scowl only supports that.

“He comes to people in their weakest moment and drags out their innermost emotions and makes them public, he takes away their chance to sort out things themselves and forces them to hurt their friends and people they love and do things they’d never do themselves.”

The room suddenly feels over-warm and if the brace she’s wearing, more to appease Gabriel these days than because she really needs it, didn’t hold her jacket in place she’d shrug it off.

Because she’s not Shadow Moth, but she was his partner, and while as Mayura she’d used her own emotions or Gabriel’s more than she’d reached out to anyone else’s heart and pulled out their hopes and fears to be the clay for her creations it’s not that she never has, even if she’d generally piggybacked on Gabriel’s akumatisations then.

“I thought the general opinion was that people weren’t to blame for akumatisations?” She suggests gently.

It’s easy to get to caught out in her betrayal of Gabriel, even if it’s for his own good, that she’s forgotten her betrayal of Adrien and the secrets she’s keeping from him even if it’s for his own good.

She doesn’t want to be Adrien’s enemy, has always disliked her role as a force keeping him from having a more normal life despite seeing the necessity of it. She doesn’t want him to hate her.

“They’re not,” he almost snaps, “Shadow Moth is. He’s a terrible person Nathalie, how can you look past that?”

“I’m an adult.”

“What does that mean?”

“It means maybe I’ve done things I’m not proud of too Adrien. I know people make mistakes, and…the world is a lot more complicated than it seems to someone your age.”

His face droops, is the only description she can give of it, “I’d say my life is pretty complicated.”

She sighs. “I didn’t mean to say it wasn’t.” Because even without him being Chat Noir she can’t pretend anything in his house is simple, “just that, maybe I’ve less room be judgemental.”

“What do you mean? You’re nothing like him.” Self-righteousness animates every line of his body and she would almost falter back in the face of it but the exoskeleton holds her in place.

“But I am,” come on Nathalie, she pushes herself, take the jump. “Shadow Moth isn’t just doing this for power’s sake you know?”

“You’re that close to him that he told you that?!”

“More or less.” She shrugs, that it was Gabriel and not Shadow Moth who told her isn’t the point. The point is, “yes, he’s doing the wrong thing. From what Ladybug and Plagg have told me about how these powers work I know that but…”

“You’ve talked to Ladybug about this? She’s ok with this?”

“Ladybug just wants all of this to be over as far as I can tell,” she forces herself to move closer to Adrien and puts a hand on his shoulder, “it’s a tough life for the both of you. You’re both too young for this.”

To her surprise Adrien looks away, “but the people Shadow Moth akumatises aren’t? Do you know how many Akuma victims I know? How many of my classmate he’s targeted? If you think it’s unfair Master Fu asked us to be heroes, isn’t it more unfair that Hawk Moth made my friend’s villains?”

She lets go of him on instinct but because it does hurt like a stab between her ribs not because Adrien is wrong but because this is Adrien talking to her, and it had all been fine when they were going to remake the whole world but now they’re not.

Everything Hawk Moth and Mayura did will last. And she’s not sure Adrien will forgive either of them.

But despite the salt burning at corners of her eyes Nathalie’s dealt with other’s hatred before; in her youth from strangers and then from her family, when Gabriel had snapped and blamed her for finding the Miraculous for them before his contrite apology, when people whisper loud enough to be heard about whether Emilie’s disappearance was a promotion for her, and if maybe maybe Gabriel’s jealous little mistress had had something to do with said disappearance.

Which she had, if not in the way they’d meant.

It feels worse now, looking Adrien in the eye as he expounds his hatred of his own father, of her, but it’s not unprecedented and so she’s able to stuff all those feelings down and gentle her voice like she does with his father when he’s down but rarely does with Adrien from a fear of overstepping. Perhaps everyone thinking her Chat Noir’s mother is having an impact.

“Teenagers are…volatile. It makes them easy targets. And.” She has to concentrate a little not to heave, and forces her thoughts down a different route, “it think it’s location based,”

“Wait. Shadow Moth lives around here?”

Damn. She really isn’t in good control of herself. If only she hadn’t let herself get so attached to both Adrien and Gabriel. “You know that,” she stonewalls, “the same way you know that Ladybug is too. I mean what’s the chances he attacks somewhere you’re both able to get to fairly quickly?”

“Oh my god.” Adrien’s eyes are wide and he’s staring somewhere into the middle distance, “Ladybug lives near me?! Do you think I know her? That I’ve met her as not-Ladybug? I mean, she’s around my age right?”

Bingo. Mentioning his Lady was a stroke of brilliance to distract his attention.

“I mean I did threaten to tell you she was in lyceé but given her response to that I’m pretty sure she’s in collége, so yeah she’s around your age I’m pretty sure.”

His whole mouth contorts, “she’s not younger than me right? That’s just, urgh, no she can’t be right? She’s so responsible and actually I can kind of see her being in lyceé and that would be alright right? Because everyone at the parties my parents took me to said I’m so good with adults and-“

“You’re around the same age kid,” Plagg drawls.

“Wha-?”

“Oh hey! I can say that.” Their kwami sounds delighted.

“Probably because I was going to,” Nathalie decides to stop him because she’d forgotten once again that Plagg was here and he could have made this so uncomfortable had he wanted to.

Adrien looks between her and Plagg and then seems to settle back on his inquisition of her. “So she’s my age?”

“I’d guess,” it’s an honest enough answer. Nathalie doesn’t actually know but she’s pretty sure that superheroine is in that ballpark and much as she has very much hoped she could maybe push Adrien in a different direction with someone who isn’t Ladybug she can’t deny that getting Adrien together with Ladybug might be useful if Ladybug goes along with Shadow Moth now giving up.

“Then,” she winces as she sees Adrien’s fingernails grasp along his jeans the same way Emilie used to grip at the handles of her handbags, “I guess it would be. Good for her. If Hawkmoth gave up. Before we get to more important years at school”

Despite his words nothing from his countenance to his posture to how he won’t met her eyes is in the slightest bit positive.

And maybe Adrien, not her son by blood or by any legal tie, is a little bit hers after all because there’s an answering ache in her heart as it thuds.

Cautiously, ready at any moment for him to jump out like she’s a hunter laying a trap, she clasp his upper arm like she might, in of course a purely friendly way, for his father.

“It would be,” she imparts firmly, “should you struggle at school then we’d ensure you had the tutors to make that up but we don’t know that she has that freedom, and it’s quite likely she doesn’t.”

“Yeah, I know,“ He jerks up, “and what if the freedom she wanted was to date someone, do you think she has that?”

Nathalie steadfastly shoves all her own issues to the side, “no, I’d suppose that so long as she’d Ladybug she doesn’t have that, even if,” she skates dangerously close to the truth but compared to all the other truths Adrien might learn this is a good thing and much as she hates it she might have to rely upon it, “she actually quite likes Adrien Agreste, and maybe,” and she’s not at all sure but some gut instinct is yelling at her, “she might have even meet him it’s not like she’d feel free to do anything until,”

“Shadow Moth was defeated,” Adrien interrupts.

“Shadow Moth is no longer an issue,” she corrects.

“But,” and her boy looks so weak and uncertain it pains her because he was never supposed to look like that. Gabriel’s rules were strict but they were certain, were supposed to be easy to obey, that Adrien never had to wonder what was the right way of living that wouldn’t earn his admonishment, “isn’t that kind of weak? Like choosing the easy route? Isn’t it our duty to put him in prison?”

“Your duty,” it’s easy to scoff a little, perhaps less to moderate it, “I think I missed when you signed up to be an officer of the Republic.”

“I’m supposed to be Chat Noir,” and suddenly he’s sniffling.

“Hey,” and it’s surprisingly easy to fold Adrien inside her arms, to hold him safely against her chest like he’s the precious thing he is, “this isn’t your responsibility anymore right? I’m dealing with it, and if I could convince Ladybug to let me meet her parents they’d be dealing with it too. And anyway which crime they’d convict Shadow Moth for is a total mess. It’s ok to just let him go and take his Miraculous. To let Paris forget all of this.”

“But,” Adrien sniffles against her arm, “I wouldn’t be Chat Noir then would I? There wouldn’t be a need for him, and Ladybug might take my Miraculous back and-”

She recognises the spiral from Gabriel and interrupts, “Hmm, I think Plagg would disagree there.”

And for once her prayers are answered, “Sorry.” The Kwami humphs, “But you’re stuck with me kid. No broken foot,”

“Ankle,” Adrien corrects.

“Whatever,” Plagg seems ridiculously unbothered when she knows just how much he values Adrien, “is going to take me away from a holder I chose. Not Master Fu. Me. Do you know how many boys there were with your boyband cutesy hairstyle in Paris?”

“Uh, much as multiple focus groups have decided this is the best option for me what does my hairstyle have to do with you choosing me?”

“Um,” Plagg suddenly looks uncertain in a way that doesn’t fit with her usually laconic Kwami, “I think that was important? Because he…I don’t know he could have known. Ask, actually don’t you’re my kitten I’m not sharing you with whoever comes around but look, I guess you’ve wielded Sass before. He knows enough to point you to Fluff if the timeline changed.”

In a nervous tic she uses the arm Adrien isn’t currently clinging to to run a hand over her hair, pushing any loss strands back into her bun, pushing herself back into Nathalie Sancoeur, executive assistant, “Couldn’t that be a good thing? If the timeline changed?”

“It could be,” Plagg nods, “or it could be a catastrophe.”

“And what do you, oh manifestation of destruction, think it is?”

Plagg hold out an arm.

“Are you seriously asking me for cheese?”

Stubbornly he remains with an arm out, and Nathalie has to abandon Adrien, though she leaves him with a kiss on the crown of his head, to go in search of something.

“Have some Fourme d’Ambert and shut up,” she slams the cheese down on the table.

Adrien’s eyes move between them as quickly as if he’s watching a tennis match, “can you just decide that’s an option? Surely if things have changed that’s not a good thing?”

“Eh,” Plagg takes the most nonchalant bite of cheese possible.

“Yes, I think we can. It’s our world, we’re the ones living in it.” Nathalie decides firmly, “and as to the rest of it, are you really that worried about me ending up with Shadow Moth? Or are you just worried about your father choosing someone else as a possible new stepmother for you?”

“Someone else?!” Adrien yelps but then he flushes and shame colours his voice when he says, “I just want Father to be happy. To be like we used to be. But. I thought maybe that was going to be with you? And that wouldn’t be forgetting my maman and It wouldn’t be re- drawing all my life. You’ve been here since I can remember and if you and father could reform around that gap in all our lives my mother has left and be happy then maybe I could be happy,”

“Adrien,” she can’t do more than hold her arms out, she’s not his mother and she’s not meant to be his mother, and she knows that but she also knows that his mother can’t come back so maybe a little bit of comfort is ok and, “I’ll stay with you,” forever but that’s not a promise she can make because she’s not his mother, “as long as I can. I promise you that. And I will always love you.”

“Even if father found someone else?”

“Even then.”

His eyes are so devastatingly large, she thinks he’s deliberately exaggerating but that doesn’t mean it doesn’t work on her. “Even if she was like some horrible fairy tale evil stepmother? Would you rescue me?”

Nathalie blinks away the utterly wrong impossible image of Gabriel with another woman and focuses on what she knows is true. “Your father loves you more than that Adrien.”

“I think those fathers loved their children once too,” his eyes when she looks into them are tired and full of wisdom and she hate hate hates that she’s been part of putting that there. “And I’m not sure he remembers how to love me anymore. And it’s hardly like I can go off an run away with you and Shadow Moth.”

“Even in the extraordinarily unlikely event Shadow Moth was any to offer me a real option, a real life with him, I wouldn’t say yes.” Not least because she couldn’t because the truth would mean the end of anything between her and Shadow Moth, but that’s not the point, the point is, “you’re more important to me than any romantic relationship.”

“So you’d never chose him?”

She’s regretting this whole conversation, “Like I said, he’s not a real choice.”

“But,” Adrien presses, “you would if he was? You meant it when you said you and father really aren’t…”

Ignoring the desire to massage at her temples she instead of answering that she replies, “Shadow Moth wants to save someone. And I’m not him because I know about the consequences about making a Wish with your ring and Ladybug’s earrings, so I know that’s not an option but. His devotion means I can’t help but wonder if I should do more.”

“Do more?”

Her nails bite into the palms of her hands, she’ll be surprised if she’s not bleeding later. “if I manage to get Shadow Moth to surrender then you and Ladybug are going to have a lot of time on your hands, along with access to a lot of magical power.”

“Nathalie?”

“I just think, should I ask her if anything could be done for your mother?”

His voice cracks, in a sign how he’s aging in Emilie’s absence. “What?”

“Maybe terrorising the city is wrong, but if Ladybug could save her and I don’t ask her to isn’t that wrong too?”

Adrien just stares at her gaping, and thank goodness they’d built up to this.

“I…don’t think that’s how it works.” He says eventually.

“What?”

“It’s, it’s not your job to find Maman, and it’s not Ladybug’s either and. I’m not even sure she could do anything. She can open a portal to anywhere but she can’t do that if she doesn’t know where to, and we’re not supposed to meddle with time and…wouldn’t it be wrong?”

“Wrong? To help someone?”

“To expect her to help us just because Plagg chose me? I’m not the only person who’s lost someone. I’m not even the only person in my family who has, would you expect her to help Félix get his dad back?”

“That’s not the same.”

“Why? Just because we don’t know what’s happened to my mother? You’re right. It is different. I would be easier if I could know. But I do know she’s gone. She’s not coming back,” his voice increases in volume, “I cried myself to sleep for nights after we lost her, I spent every day alone waiting for her to walk in the door while my father hid himself in his atelier. AND I CAN’T KEEP LIVING LIKE THAT!”

“Adrien,” she stands but falters under Adrien’s fury, and the sudden sensation of having lost the ground beneath she as everything assumption she’s had about what Adrien needs and how to fix it all is turned upside down.

“No, I know she’s not coming back and I’ve accepted that because that’s the only way I can live and you and father, you two just refuse to even consider that and do you know how hard that makes things for me? Having to know that father’s not going to get better, having that little bit of hope instead me keep getting stoked for nothing because you’re supposed to be able to trust you parent’s with things like this? And now you’re dangling it in front of me again, possibly endangering my relationship with one of the only good things in my life, just because you want to go off with Shadow Moth and not feel guilty about it?”

“That’s not why,” she finds herself snapping back, and hates herself for it at Adrien’s wince. “I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to,”

“I’m sorry,” Adrien says, “that wasn’t fair of me, and its more father anyway. I know you just do what you have to. And I know you were just trying to help. But do me a favour?”

“If I can.”

“Try not get inspired by Shadow Moth. If you of you has to have an influence on the other then it needs to be you on him.“

She smiles the fakest weakest smile she’s ever mustered, because it’s a little too late for that.

“That’s what I’m trying.”

But despite all Adrien’s words, all his deeply felt feelings he’s entrusted her with, she still does think she needs to talk to Ladybug.

Especially with the glances Gabriel keeps casting on her when he thinks she’s not looking. Asking him to see her might have been a mistake.

Chapter 15: Duusu I

Nooroo has a secret and he’s not sharing.

It’s not fair.

Duusu too is stuck here as the prisoner of Gabriel Agreste, and without even the distinction of being first Kwami. And it’s so boring.

The only excitement, the only hope, around had been the growing relationship between their so-called master and Miss Nathalie who makes sure that both he and they are looked after and fed and sleep, and are able to keep fighting.

Splintered memories, like looking through broken glass, suggest Miss Nathalie had been his holder before. Duusu thinks that would have been better. The woman might also be a villain but at least she’s nice.

And her emotions were so much more comfortable to be around. Guilt is hardly one of Duusu’s favourites but in the circumstances it’s right that it should be felt and it’s certainly better than Gabriel’s righteous self-justification that tries to ignore it. At least until it pokes through when Miss Nathalie coughs and when something happens to make him realise, before he forgets again, the danger he’s placing his son in.

He likes to try not be feel most of his emotions actually. Anger he lets himself feel and devastation when he loses but anything else he stifles in a way that almost makes it feel like Duusu’s the one being smothered with a blanket cutting of any sense of the world.

Miss Nathalie hides her emotions from the people around her but she doesn’t try not to feel them. Duusu feels her little bursts of irritation, and sparks of excitement, and nervous hope followed by troughs of disappointment, and then below it all her love.

Constant unyielding devoted love.

It’s always been one of Duusu’s favourite emotions to feel; it offers so maybe different possibilities, so many ways it could play out.

Duusu could create something beautiful from Miss Nathalie’s love, it calls out almost asking the Kwami to do so, for some acknowledgement when Gabriel won’t give it.

But it’s dangerous to create anything without a wielder and moreso from an emotion like this. Even if Shadow Moth or Mayura were to the ones to make it that steadfast devotion could be forged into a sentimonster that even they might have cause to regret.

Love makes people do terrible things after all.

And Miss Nathalie’s has done terrible things for her love.

But it makes her do good things too, like standing up to their master about his son, and doing things that he wouldn’t approve of but which would make all of their lives better, and giving him hugs when he’s down.

The last one doesn’t sound like much but Gabriel Agreste is a volatile man who yearns not to feel and the worse his grief and self-hatred and anger gets the more likely he is to turn his anger out on the whole world.

Without Miss Nathalie what he forces Duusu and Nooroo to create would be worse. He wouldn’t be satisfied with the highly-felt but often low stakes conflicts of teenagers. No, he’d look for the worst emotions around even if they were further again, and fainter, and not really helpful to his apparent cause. Because really he’d be fighting as much to make everyone feel the way he had after losing his wife as he would be to bring her back.

He must have loved the woman in the casket once. Otherwise he wouldn’t feel the sadness that threatens to smoother Duusu whenever he stands in front of her, or the anger that almost burns.

Duusu doesn’t really remember Emilie Agreste. Trying to only brings a fuzzy sense of warmth and determination.

Nooroo can’t offer much either. Apparently he hadn’t been called upon much before.

The, yes romantic, love that is hidden deep inside Gabriel Agreste’s chest now isn’t for his wife. It’s a confused little thing, and Duusu can’t help not pout at how he feels it surge after they’ve transformed him.

Nathalie was here first, and Duusu doesn’t even know Panthera even if the lack of akuma attacks recently, and how for the first time in forever Mr Gabriel seems to be considering giving up suggest hers is a good influence.

Even if that last part was because of Miss Nathalie.

So illogical as it might be Duusu can’t help but beam on feeling Mr Gabriel come to a decision and stand up.

His hand goes to his cravat, pressing against the Miraculous beneath, and then the Kwamis follow him down the corridor to Nathalie’s room.

For some reason nervousness emanates from him but there’s no sign of wavering, no worry he’s making the wrong choice, and Duusu can’t wait to see what he’s going to do.

There’s a flare of panic on opening the door, and Duusu doesn’t get it until the empty bed comes into view but then it disappears as Gabriel spots Nathalie curled up in one of the chairs by the unused fireplace.

“There you are,” he smiles.

Nathalie looks up, “oh, sir. Were you looking for me?”

From their expressions you’d never know how similar their emotions in this moment are.

Save for the bit of disappointment from the man at her form of address for him.

“We need to talk, my dear Nathalie.”

Nathalie leans forward, uncurling her feet from beneath her to sit more professionally. “That sounds serious, should I be concerned?”

There’s an upturn to the corner of her mouth like she’s joking but beneath it she’s clearly actually very much concerned already. That little leap her heart makes whenever she sees Mr Gabriel abruptly halting.

He sits down.

Exhales.

Leans forward and places his hand on Miss Nathalie’s cheek, and Nooroo feels the affection/guilt/want there too from his abrupt turn of his head to meet Duusu’s eyes.

“Shh,” Nooroo whispers as if expecting Duusu to let out a happy shriek of excitement, like he’s the only one who’s learnt the best way to act around these two.

The man just stares at Miss Nathalie. Looking into her eyes, as if he could read something there that Duusu and Nooroo aren’t already giving him the power to tell.

Her breathing speeds up, and the faintest flush starts to spread over her face.

She’s terrified. She’s exhilarated. And she’s also very very confused.

“Are you alright?” He softly murmurs, concern leaking from him like water through a sieve.

Nathalie looks around as If someone can save her from this conversation. Like he’d listen to any of them.

“It’s got very warm in here?”

“Has it?” His hand leaves her cheek to press the back against her forehead, “You don’t think you’re ill?”

“No more than usual,”

That makes him release her with a flinch of guilt, and combined with Nathalie’s sudden harsh self-recrimination Duusu is overwhelmed enough to float closer to Nooroo for an anchor in how awful it all feels.

“You were never meant to get hurt,” he shakes his head, “but it’s not forever Nathalie. I am going to save you.”

“I know that you,”

“No,” he interrupts and for once he isn’t guilty about her condition, “you don’t understand. I’ve found a way. Regardless of what we do rather than the Wish you’re going to be OK.”

Duusu’s felt hope from Miss Nathalie before but nothing like what thrums through her sharp, “Rather than Wish?”

“You were right. I can’t chance it. I don’t know that I can just abandon Emilie but,”

His words are cut off as Nathalie throws herself at him, her arms closing over his shoulders in an embrace.

Duusu isn’t so surprised. If it’s possible Miss Nathalie’s just fallen more in love with him. She seems t happier than…

Well happier than she’s ever been the entire time Duusu remembers knowing her, and honestly Duusu would be willing to bet that she’s happier than she even was while the Peacock Miraculous was broken as well.

Gabriel Agreste on the other hand is both completely unsurprised and wholly unprepared, though Duusu doesn’t really know why. He knew Nathalie thought this the best course, and it’s not as if the two of them aren’t always finding excuses to touch anyway.

His hand hovers awkwardly over her back, but, Duusu’s eyes narrow, he’s certainly not distressed by the situation.

“Thank you, thank you, thank you,” Miss Nathalie looks up from where her face is buried in his chest, “I knew you’d make the right decision Gabriel.”

Her face shines with a sincerity that needs no Miraculous to back It up. In fact with one her deep all-consuming relief could suggest the opposite of her words.

But as his hand settles heavily on her back despite having pinned almost under her face two Miraculous brooches which could tell him back Gabriel doesn’t seem to notice that contradiction at all. Even ignoring it as he often does anything he’s not ready to deal with quite yet, or which he deems unimportant, or distracted by his own guilt as he hides his head in her hair.

“I’m still not sure what I’m going to do,” he mutters into it.

“That’s alright,” despite the softness of her voice it’s also firm, “we’ll work that out. Together.”

“Hmm,” the sit in silences as that guilt seeps away to be replaced by a pride that almost make’s Duusu’s eyes roll.

Their jailer always has been good at letting Miss Nathalie’s approval scrub away the few remaining qualms he have.

And he’s utterly pleased with himself as he says, “you know, I haven’t even told you how I’m going to heal you yet.”

Suddenly it’s Nathalie whose guilt Duusu can feel and it’s followed a millisecond later by panic.

It’s a very strange reaction to being told you’re not longer going to be made ill by magic.

“You told me you were going to, and you said you’d given up the Wish so you must have found a way. The details aren’t so important.”

He expels half a laugh, “oh Nathalie. What would I do without your belief in me? Though maybe I’m a little disappointed not to see what you’re next enthusiastic response would have been.”

Miss Nathalie’s embarrassment isn’t as strong as her guilt but her voice wavers as she says, “what exactly were you hoping for?”

A swallow does nothing to stop the desire shooting through him, nor the surprise that follows, and then the oddest temptation to shock Nathalie and Duusu halfway expects Nooroo to starting pulling her out the room. He can get prudish about this sort of thing.

But then a sharp prick of pain seems to burst that little bubble and, “Sorry,”

He’s clumsily tries to reach for his Miraculous under Miss Nathalie and rather annoyed Duusu realises that it must have pricked him.

Of all the times!

Why couldn’t it be during an akuma attack or sometime like that!

And then the room disappears and everything goes blue.

Oh.

Renunciation.

But before Duusu can even start to think about the fact that Shadow Moth has just given up the Peacock Miraculous the familiar surroundings of Miss Nathalie’s room comes back.

And Miss Nathalie, no longer pressed against Mr Gabriel, who know seems to be kneeling in front of her chair is smiling at her.

“Hello Duusu,” she says, and her bright excitement is infectious, “I’m going to be your Mistress again. For a little bit anyway.”

Her old master nods, backing up her statement.

“I thought we weren’t going after Ladybug and Panthera again.”

And ooh there’s a fun response from Mr Gabriel at her name, as he must remember the superheroine was the last one he’d held the way he’d just been holding Miss Nathalie and that his response had been much the same then.

Well he deserves to feel bad about it so Duusu’s quite satisfied by the discomfort and confusion he’s currently trying to feel.

Miss Nathalie’s smile only seems to grow wider, she’s so so pleased. “Mayura’s not going to be helping Hawk Moth with attacks. Gabriel’s given this to me so I can contact Panthera.”

Putting on the best scary judgemental face Duusu flies up to Mr Gabriel, “and why would she want to do that?”

He stares right back like Duusu’s a bug he wants to squish, “because she has the power to heal Nathalie. And because she’s agreed to heal Mayura.”

“She has?” oh Duusu gets why his Miss Nathalie is so pleased, because she’s so pleased too. This is so so great. Even though it’s hardly the fault of the Kwami if their wielder breaks the Miraculous Duusu has never liked seeing the damage the Peacock Miraculous had left on Miss Nathalie. “Oh that’s so much better. Miss Nathalie Is going to be ok.”

It sort of feels like maybe if Duusu had been able to say something different to Emilie Agreste then she could have been convinced not to do any of this.

And even if that wasn’t the case, if Duusu had never been able to stop what happened then it didn’t change that there was still a faint link between the damage being done and where the energy that had done the damage had come from in the first place.

Duusu.

It’s not how the magic is supposed to work, and seeing Miss Nathalie suffer had always felt wrong, and made Duusu feel sad even when she’d been cross at her for helping Mr Gabriel.

So this is so fantastic that Duusu can almost almost forgive Mr Gabriel. A little bit. Not totally.

“Yes,” the man in question nods, and he’s so fond Duusu thinks Miss Nathalie must feel it too, “she’s going to be ok.”

Maybe Miss Nathalie does because her smilie changes to a different one and she ducks her head shyly, “Gabriel…would you mind if I tried it now? Contacting Panthera?”

“Of course not,” concern makes him frown, “should I stay while you transform? We could go together. I know she thinks you’ll be fine but I’m not sure it’s a risk we should take.”

“Together,” huh, Miss Nathalie very much does not like that idea and Duusu does not get it, “no that’s alright. In fact,” she reaches for her phone, “Adrien has a photoshoot coming up. And I know this totally isn’t how anything is supposed to work but could you possibly take him.”

He stares back at her confused, “Adrien has a fracture. I know he’s much more mobile with the boot but how can he has a photoshoot?”

“It’s for touch-up shots of his face from the last one. You didn’t like the expressions remember?”

“Did I not?” and yep, Duusu is still enjoying it when he’s annoyed with himself, “well I suppose I can go but I still don’t like you going alone.”

“Maybe I’ll just contact her with the Miraculous then,” Miss Nathalie says.

“You, you know how to do that?”

“I know it can be done.” Miss Nathalie is amused, “I am aware of your conversations with her sir.”

“Oh.” And he’s embarrassed again, “I’d best be going then.”

“Good luck,” Miss Nathalie almost does a little wave as he leaves.

She stares dreamily at the door, and fingers her Miraculous.

“Oh yeah,” a very familiar voice that Duusu hasn’t heard in years, “he totally doesn’t have feelings for you. Urgh the two of you are worse than-“

“PLAGGGY!” Duusu flies at the Black Cat Kwami, “What are you doing here? This is so cool, oh my gosh you need to help me save Nooroo he’s stuck with Mr Gabriel and he’s terrible,”

Miss Nathalie snorts.

“Sorry Miss Nathalie, but to everyone except you he is, and anyway Plagg hasn’t answered me, how are you here? When did you find out where Nooroo and I were? Do you know Miss Nathalie? Did you know she’s going to be OK? Did you hear Shadow,” but oooh he doesn’t have Duusu anymore, “Hawk Moth is going to stop trying to steal your Miraculous?”

“I am suddenly wondering why I wanted to help you.” Plagg groans.

“Duusu,” Miss Nathalie is doing her responsible voice, “this is a secret and as the holder of your Miraculous I’m commanding you that you can’t tell anyone.”

The sensation of the command tightening around is no fun at all, “not even Nooroo?”

“Nooroo knows. But you can’t talk to him about it where Mr Gabriel might overhear.”

“Oh! Are you secretly working for Ladybug? I knew you were nice,” Duusu beams. It’s so cool, and so romantic. Her and Hawk Moth as star-crossed lovers with Miss Nathalie trying to bring him back out of being villainy without him discovering her allegiance and ruining the mutual bond between them.

“I’m working for the Agrestes.” Nathalie throws cold water on that idea, “I’m going to make sure they come out of this the absolute best way possible.”

But actually that’s still kind of beautiful and could make the best story now they’re not going after the Wish and Duusu can cheer Gabriel and Nathalie on wholeheartedly and…

“But what’s Plagg doing here?”

Nathalie shrugs one shoulder, “Plagg, claws out.”

And there’s Panthera in her place. Dusuu’s not seen her in the flesh before. Usually too busy creating Shadow Moth’s transformation, but the heroine is easily recognisable from photos and the blurry recognition from Shadow Moth’s interactions with her.

“You’re Panthera! And he doesn’t know!”

“I am. And he doesn’t. And we don’t have time for this because I need to go see Ladybug.” She types something in her staff.

Duusu frowns. The woman in front of her is stressed, and seems ready for a fight but she’s also oddly….hopeful seems the best word for it.

Probably another secret Duusu will discover soon.

“Aren’t you going to unify me with him?”

“Not yet,” Panthera holds her hand out for Duusu as she had as Miss Nathalie, “I need you to be very very quiet, and very serious and pay lots of attention to what Ladybug’s feeling OK?”

Need isn’t an exaggeration from her point of view at least.

“It’s important?”

“Very important. If Ladybug seems ready to turn on us I have to know. Give me a nudge or something. And if it all goes well then…I need you to tell me if I can trust she’s telling the truth.”

“I can do that,” it doesn’t even sound that bad. “What are you going to ask Ladybug?”

“Something very very important.”

…

Ladybug is annoyed when she arrives. “What’s this all about?”

Panthera folds her arms, as If she wasn’t the one to summon Ladybug to this rooftop. “Shadow Moth.”

It’s a simple clever answer but then Duusu had known Panthera was clever.

“Alright. Go on then.”

“If someone dies from using a broken Miraculous can they be saved?”

Ladybug’s eyes widen, “so you were right about him wanting to save someone.”

“Yes.”

“But,” Ladybug’s suspicious again now and her hand curls over her yo-yo but Duusu doesn’t judge it time to alert her new-old holder yet, “The Butterfly and the Peacock are the only ones missing from the Miracle Box and he’s used both of them. How can the problem be a broken Miraculous? Are you sure he’s not lying to you to try to make it our problem?”

“He fixed the Peacock with the information he took from the previous Guardian.”

“Stole from the previous Guardian,” Ladybug corrects but despite her sharp tone, Duusu can feel the consideration turning over through Ladybug’s mind. If she let herself be taken over by her every emotion then she’d have been akumatised long ago, or a sentimonster created from her so it makes sense that despite her distrust she’d listen.

“Stole then.”

Disgust fills Ladybug, “so that’s why he started wearing it after that. What a prick. That does actually make perfect sense but, wait,” now it’s horror, “are you telling me Mayura’s dead? And that still doesn’t explain why he wanted our Miraculous before so-“

“Mayura isn’t dead,” Panthera doesn’t lie, “The Miraculous made her very sick, but Plagg is able to heal her and I’ve agreed with Hawk Moth to do so.”

“What?!” Oh she is very very cross, and Duusu debates making her move, on Ladybug’s rather high pitched, “Without asking me? Why would you do that?”

“In exchange for the Peacock Miraculous,” Panthera says evenly. “and there wasn’t exactly time to ask you.”

“You really believe he’s going to give you that?” Ladybug scoffs but doesn’t seem quite ready to attack.

“He already has,” Panthera bends down her unzipped collar to show it pinned there.

Ladybug reaches up but Panthera shakes her head. “Not yet. Should things go wrong….” Which seems to be a true worry, “I might yet need it.”

“So you don’t totally trust him?”

“Mayura isn’t the reason he started fighting.”

“He wanted a Wish to raise the dead.” Ladybug realises, “but then, he should never have let Mayura use it?”

“Give Mayura some credit, she chose to use that Miraculous.”

“I feel like I should give her the opposite of credit for that.”

Panthera tilts her head, but she doesn’t feel offended to Duusu, “Maybe.”

“So...” Ladybug frowns, “you want to know if there’s another way to save this person Hawk Moth’s fighting for?”

“Yes.”

Ladybug’s sadness must be as obvious to Miss Nathalie as Duusu as her whole face falls, “I can’t raise the dead. No Miraculous can. Even combing mine and Chat Noir’s would take an equal price.”

Panthera seems oddly reluctant to ask. “You bring people back after akuma attacks, don’t you?”

“What? Ladybug shakes her head, “no I….” for a moment Duusu is frightened because what Ladybug’s feeling right now is exactly the sort of thing Hawk Moth would akumatise, “that’s not the same.”

“Why?”

“That’s tied to the akuma, and it’s only, it’s taking away damage magic has made. Fixing something that never should have happened.”

“Isn’t a broken Miraculous that?”

“There wouldn’t be any dark intention to cleanse, I can’t. I can’t work from nothing, I can’t cure someone ill, or save someone in a car crash, or bring back my friend’s mother. It’s that’s all part of life and my Miraculous doesn’t work against that.”

“Because it’s part of it too,” Panthera finishes for her and she, she’s relieved, she wants to give up.

“What Shadow Moth’s doing, it’s fighting against the inevitable.” Ladybug says.

“So, it wouldn’t be selfish to give up?”

“Why would it be selfish, it would be selfish to— Oh.”

“Yeah,” Panthera admits.

“So um. You and Shadow Moth? It’s um, serious then.”

“I don’t know.” Panthera says, “but I’m starting to wonder if I’m wrong about it being impossible.”

Chapter 16: Gabriel VI

The picnic blanket is in place, wine and cheese placed carefully central. He’d considered candles but decided against it in the end. Too much that could go accidentally wrong. This time there will be no embarrassing mistakes.

At least Hawk Moth’s outfit will not betray him the way Shadow Moth’s did.

And now he’s given up the Peacock Miraculous he can finally admit that he has always thought it had a more flattering cut too.

Not that he’s trying to seduce Panthera this time.

But still.

Appearances are important.

“Hi,” her voice startles him from behind, he’d had thought he’d have heard her enter through a window.

He forces himself to turn slowly, into of twisting in shock. “Hello. Silent as a cat indeed.”

“I try,” there’s amusement playing over her lips but her eyes are wide as she looks around, “you set all this up?”

Gabriel looks around the otherwise empty room, “well, I could hardly take you out to a restaurant. You’d be surprised at how many empty buildings there are in Paris when you care to look.”

Even ones he himself owns but that goes seem like it might be a little too close to giving the game away. Still it had been easy enough for Nathalie to find a suitable meeting place even if he’d taken over the rest of the preparations himself.

It had seemed…wrong to expect her to help even if this is after all in many ways because of her.

Panthera elegantly settles down onto the rug, “my Kwami will be jealous you know. He’s a maniac for cheese.”

“Mine’s rather fond of wine,” he settles his legs down next to her.

“Hawk Moth,” then she wrinkles her nose, “it’s odd calling you that again.”

“Not a downgrade I hope?”

“No, not a downgrade at all.” Her shy smile reminds him of the new little ones that Nathalie’s started sporting, and for once he’s totally at ease as he reaches for the glasses.

But then she stops him, “before you pour anything. What is this about? I know you said you weren’t going to make the Wish but,”

“It’s not that.” He releases his hold on the stems and takes her hands in his instead, “this is me saying thank you.”

“Oh.”

“For helping me decide against the Wish,”

“I don’t know that that was me,”

“Without you I don’t know I could have made the decision with what it meant for Mayura and-,” my son and my wife, but he doesn’t want to go into that, still isn’t quite ready to admit why he’d been doing this while he was flirting with her and how close he’d come to being ready to sacrifice his son when she’s such a fierce protector of hers.

“And,”

“I can’t thank you enough for what you’ve done for Mayura. Seeing her now-“

It hadn’t been obvious while she’d been sat in her chair but then when she’d left to go fetch Adrien for lunch his ears had caught on the sound of her shoes against the Atelier floor. How it had sounded different to what he’d become used to.

And then when the door had let her back in the difference had been obvious.

“You’re not wearing your exoskeleton.” He’d realised.

“I’m not,” and then he’d seen the first of those smiles. Not the smirks of their planning, or the cool pleasure she sometimes shows as she, so politely they can’t complain, makes it clear he won’t be talking to someone, but pure joy and unlike its descendants this first smile hadn’t been small at all.

It had been beautiful on Nathalie’s face.

Nathalie had been beautiful.

She’d seemed to glow almost and he’d been about to ask if she’d tried a new foundation or something, when he’d realised what he was seeing was the healthy flush of vitality.

It had seemed like a travesty that he’d ever allowed her to become anything less than she was then; beautiful, happy, and well.

Panthera cocks her head, waiting.

“I don't know how to describe it.” He finishes weakly, “she’s... she’s so much better.”

“That it would heal her was the aim.”

“I know but,” and now he reaches for the wine and pours it, before passing her her glass, “cheers.”

“Cheers,” she echoes still seeming a little bemused.

He muses, swirling the glass around, “it’s more than just her health. She seems so much... lighter now. I suppose it’s how much easier things are for her now which makes me feel terrible but,”

“You chose her over fighting with her Miraculous. That's saving her too,” she lifts her glass to her mouth, burgundy wine against the deeper red of her lips, “and maybe it's not just her health making things easier for her these days.”

“What else could it be?”

Her eyes go all soft and comforting, and there’s nothing he wants more than to see that expression on her real eyes. Not these false green feline eyes. Human blue eyes creasing round their corners with affection despite a weary sadness shiny in them.

Not that he knows they are blue but it’s hard to imagine anything else now he’s got used to imagining them that way. Not a less saturated shade of it like his own grey-blue but something brighter, though not as true a blue as Ladybug’s since he refuses to ruin the image with that. No Something more like the aquamarine of Nathalie’s and if that feels a little too right he can probably blame it on Adrien’s confusing ideas.

“She was the one who told you about the Wish wasn’t she? It must be a relief that you’ve chosen not to chance it.”

“Yes, it must have been.”

He’s thankful for the mask hiding the flush he can feel on his face as he remembers Nathalie’s joyous reaction. The phantom warmth of her body feels heavy against his chest, and he can almost smell the scent of her hair, and the knowledge won’t leave him of how different it had felt to have her in his arms then to when he’d carried her during her illness.

“I’d have been. I mean I was, when you told me.”

“Because you are supposed to be keeping that ring safe from me.”

“Hmm, I can’t deny less akuma attacks would leave me for more time for the rest of my life. But honestly, it was on Chat Noir’s behalf I was most relieved.”

“That makes sense.” That kinship he feels with her raises it’s head again. “I’m relieved I didn’t do it for my son’s sake too.”

“You know. I was relieved for you too.”

Gabriel tries to resist the urge to frown. He doesn’t want to seem dismissive. “For me?” “You’ve done so much to get these jewels for something deeply important to you, and despite my best attempts you’ve found your way into my affection. I’d have hated for you to think you’d won only to lose something else.”

It’s a good thing Gabriel’s not the one wielding the power of destruction right now or the shiver that went down his spine at the suggestion of Nathalie or Adrien crumpling to the floor dead at his hand as Emilie rose up out of his casket might have sparked a cataclysm.

Instead he tries to grasp for something else to destroy the image and finds it in Panthera’s words.

“I’ve managed to wriggle my way into your affections have I?”

“Oh hush you. You know that. Or do you think I spend hours on the phone to anyone?”

“Hmm,” he gives into the impulse to stroke just behind the ears of Panthera’s mask to see if it gains him a reaction, “but I don’t know what place I have in them.”

She eyes flutter, and she makes a noise as close to a purr as a human woman can, “Hawk Moth,”

“What? Do I give you butterflies in your stomach?”

Her eyes immediately open and she moves her head out of the curve of his hand, “you had to go and ruin it didn’t you?”

“I thought it was clever.”

“Please for the sake of my sanity never give Chat Noir lessons on dating.”

He almost smirks. “I always thought the boy’s major issue was being focused on the wrong girl.”

“You’re not exactly wrong there. Although I’m not sure that she is exactly the wrong girl.”

“Ah.” The heroes had never been at the forefront of his mind when he speaks to Panthera but he recalls, “You said she had a crush on his identity under the mask.”

“Another set of people you might be making happy. I assume their mutual unmasking will be saccharine enough to make our teeth hurt.”

“Probably. Though I don’t really think I’ll be the one making them happy. I’d hate to lose your goodwill by kidnapping and unmasking Chat Noir and his possible future girlfriend.”

“What?” She frowns, and looked away again, “I’m sorry. I guess I got ahead of myself.”

“How?”

She shrugs, “I guess I got so caught up in the excitement of you choosing not to make the Wish I forgot that you haven’t actually said you giving up completely.”

“Oh. That’s alright, you’re not the only one.”

Gabriel almost had himself the other day. He’d been watching Nathalie, his heart beating off a happy little beat at how clear it was death from the Miraculous had been avoided for her, when she’d placed a cup of coffee down next to him.

And he’d wanted nothing more in that moment than for this not just to be something that happened during work hours. He’d wanted her to make him coffee first thing in the morning, and to do the same for her. To have Nathalie and her support all the time truly, not just because he paid her and because circumstances made it practical to stay here.

He’d grabbed her hand to say, he doesn’t know what, and he thinks Nathalie had forgotten too with how her lips had parted and she’d made no move to separate herself from him.

But then then she’d gone from as seemingly unable to look away as he was to apparently catching sight of something behind him and she’d gone stiff.

He’d almost thought Adrien had stuck in behind him or something and had been ready to turn and confront his son when he'd remembered: Emilie’s portrait.

The visual representation of the woman lying below them waiting for him to bring them back.

It had been liked a rush of cold water and he’d let go of Nathalie.

“Have you thought sir, what you’re going to do?”

They'd both known exactly what she was referring to. There’s no reason to be circumspect in the Atelier; if they could be caught for incriminating themselves there then they’d already be in court under the unimpressed eyes of a judge. Yet she hadn’t needed to say anything more and it had felt wrong to do so.

As if doing do would bring the spectre of Emilie up from her resting place to judge them.

She’d be doing rather a lot of judging him recently.

And aware of that all he could offer was a cowardly, “Not yet.”

Panthera draws her glass to her lips and takes a long draw and then says, “do you... think you will try to find another way?”

“Why? Are you offering to negotiate with Ladybug for me.”

She sags a little, “she'll never agree a trade of any Miraculous.”

It's easy to shake his head, “none of the others have the power for what I need.”

There’s the Rabbit but what can be done with that can be just as easily undone, and there's so much tied up in his past he's reticent to chance changing, to risk losing with an over early change to the timeline. Never mind the paradox it could cause. Nathalie might know if it could be done but he doesn’t know if he wants to ask her.

“If,” Panthera bits her lower lip, “there’s anything else I could do to help...”

“There isn’t.”

“I’m sorry.”

“No I'm sorry. I shouldn't have snapped. You, my darling Cat have been a light in all this darkness to me.”

Her mask doesn’t quite cover enough of her face to hide her blush. The strawberry stain of it creeping past the edge of her mask.

Embarrassment and desire and despite his turn away from villainy, guilt warring with hope tickle against his Miraculous making his mouth dry.

She gulps, as his eyes drawn to the motion of her throat, “I wish you wouldn't say things like that to me.”

“There I thought that was your favourite thing about me.”

“No, that would be how far you've go for the people you love.”

“Sometimes now I think that’s either too far or not far enough.”

“I think I know that feeling.”

Sympathy rings through him even if her mistakes are nothing compared to his. “Still feel you need to confiscate Chat Noir’s Miraculous permanently for sneaking out and being a superhero?”

“Humph, something like that.”

“I meant it you know. When I said I was thankful for all you’ve done. If it wasn’t for you, and for Mayura then I don’t know where I’d be now.”

“Probably somewhere worse,” she concedes with a tilt of her head, “though I’m not sure that was really me.”

He can’t help but disagree, “Mayura gave me the courage to continue after the worst part of my life. You gave me the courage to change.”

“I think maybe you just see me as you want me to be.”

“You’re a very tough woman to compliment you know.”

“Hawk Moth, do you...”

“Do I what?”

“Do you think you maybe have the issue you thought Chat Noir had? That maybe you’re…”

“Focused on the wrong woman?”

She means herself. He’s not blind to that. Not with how clear she’s always been that nothing can happen between them despite the want he always feels from her in his presence.

But it’s not Panthera that the words on his tongue conjures up in his mind.

No, it’s Emilie that he lets himself taste the possibility of letting go when he adds, “perhaps.”

Ever he decided to give up the wish-

No.

Ever since that moment Nathalie had come to him with what she’d found it’s all felt different.

It had all seemed so simple before.

Emilie was dead if not for his intervention. She was his wife and he loved her, of course he’d do anything for her. And their family, he hadn’t known how to be happy without her, and he certainly hadn’t known what to do with Adrien when he’d always let Emilie handle most things about their son, and he’d thought saving Emilie was the only way to put their family back together. That with her missing there wasn’t a family.

But Panthera and Chat Noir are clearly a family whatever their connection.

And Nathalie and Adrien only seem to get closer day by day even as he’s tried to put that fact out of his mind as much as possible.

And it seems like perhaps his heart isn’t the shrivelled dry thing incapable of love he’d thought it was without Emilie.

So that insidious whisper in his head keeps up its irritating buzz. Asking that if it took killing someone else he loved to bring Emilie back wasn’t she actually already dead after all whatever her casket might do to keep her suspended in time?

Funny how it seems different when he’s the one losing regardless.

He’d told himself before that what did mattered if he ruined someone else’s life like his had been ruined?

He was willing to fight against the physical laws of the universe, to terrorise an entire city, nay the whole world, to gain the primal forces of creation and destruction he needed to do so. If whoever he doomed had someone who truly loved them then they could do the same as Gabriel did.

But it wouldn’t someone else. Nathalie had told him it would be him again.

And Panthera has a way of making him feel guilt.

And he wasn’t sure Emilie would like the man he’d become.

But others looked at him now and all his foibles and still believed in him. One of them even knowing all of him. How he’d only briefly attempted to reach out to Adrien telling himself it would all be fixed so there was no great need to. And now it won’t be. And he’s going to need help.

“Did Mayura say much about me? When you’ve been healing her?” He frowns, “how does that work actually? Do you just wave your arms over her?”

“Which of those questions did you actually want me to answer? Actually I’m stopping you right there, what happens between myself and Mayura stays between myself and Mayura.”

“That’s no fun,” although now he’s trying to work out what it would look like if Nathalie had to wear the Cat Miraculous to heal but all he keeps coming up with is Panthera with Nathalie’s bun. Similarly imagining Panthera with the Peacock should they swap just gives him Mayura with Panthera’s messy waves.

That probably says something about his psyche.

“Did you want me to tell Mayura the details of all our conversations?”

“She probably guesses much of them. She’s a remarkably perceptive woman. But no. Still. What did you think of her?”

“What did I think of her?”

“Did you like her?”

Unfortunately he’s caught her in the middle of a sip, and she chokes on it launching into a coughing fit that makes his own throat close up in horrified sympathy. Latent instincts from Nathalie’s illness have him moving immediately.

Thankfully she shakes her head, as he’s about to offer to help, but she doesn’t shrug his hands off her shoulders this time once the coughing subsides.

“Sorry, went down the wrong way.”

“You’re alright?”

“Yeah OK.”

“So dare I ask you that question again?”

Her face freezes in exactly the same way Nathalie’s had when he’d trying pressing her on her opinion of Panthera before she’d told him she was very grateful for the healing offered her, and what the heroine had been able to offer him but she didn’t think her opinion on Panthera was particularly relevant in the circumstances.

“She’d very devoted to you you know,” Panthera at least defers the question more gracefully but that only makes him more fascinated by what exactly had gone down between them because it doesn’t suggest any enmity between them the way Nathalie’s might.

“I do know,” he admits, “I’m not sure I deserve it.”

“I don’t know that it’s about deserving it. But you did justify her trust in you didn’t you?”

“I suppose I did.”

“Hawk Moth,” Panthera’s warm voice is uncertain again, “do you have feelings for Mayura?”

“Good thing this isn’t a date or I’d be failing desperately at it.” He still feels embarrassed. But maybe not as much as he shoud.

“You don’t have to answer.”

“You told me you loved Chat Noir’s father. So.” He exhales, “I am thinking of giving up. And if I do I think I might have to deal with the fact you remind me of her and all the implications of that.”

The corner of her lips starts to turn up, and he can’t help but go, “what?!”

“I like it when you’re brave.”

“Was that brave?”

“I think so.”

“You’re a strange one,” it’s easy enough to understand how Panthera can like him and be attracted to him but still stand certain on her walls of not letting anything happen between them, but how she can seem honestly happy with no hint of jealousy at all when he talks of his feelings for Nathalie he can’t understand at all.

But then if Nathalie has feelings for him, which he doesn’t know, and maybe it’s Adrien giving him unearned confidence here, but he certainly feels they could be nurtured in her from her reactions to him, she’s developed them while helping him try to get Emilie back so maybe it’s some feminine mystery Gabriel will never be able to understand.

“You wouldn’t like me otherwise.”

“Panthera,” he can’t help but lean in closer, “if this is our last time meeting,”

“I really shouldn’t let you kiss me,” but he can feel her breath against his lips and she closes her eyes.

If any man could resist that it’s definitely not him. It’s the work of seconds to close those last few millimetres, and then the softness of her lips are under his. They’re more textured than he’d expected and he wonders if she worries at them, before his mind loses the ability to think at all as her mouth opens, and she drags him down to the floor.

Distantly there’s a clang, and part of him registers that that was either a glass or a bottle and Nathalie is not going to be impressed about getting the rug cleaned.

Then he remembers Nathalie and suddenly he’s not all running on the impulse to try to close any slightest bit of air between himself and Panthera, to mould himself to her, to bury his reservations in the sensations of her.

Torn between desire that makes him as a dizzy as a teenage boy, and an awkward dissatisfaction that this isn’t quite right; that the hair his hands are lost in is too short, that that leather should be soft crimson wool he manages to make himself roll off her.

It’s not fair to Panthera to kiss her like this if he’s not focused on her.

“I’m sorry,”

“No I get it I think.”

“That wasn’t actually what I meant to give you you know.”

She turns her head to look at him, and once again he feels younger than his years, but back in his courtship of Emilie this time.

He reaches into the pocket he’d made clear to Nooroo needed to be included in his outfit and digs out a little jewellery box.

Panthera almost seems frightened but takes it from him and he can feel her relief as she opens it to discover a set of ruby earrings.

“It’s hard when I don’t know what you actually look like.” He smiles, “I was going to go green. You know for your Miraculous, but then I remembered you saying you’d carry off red better. And well. It’s my favourite colour too.”

It had also been easier when Adrien had realised that on his screens wasn’t designs, but a catalogue. His son had assumed he was picking something for Nathalie and pushed him away from the green he’d been looking at when Gabriel had remembered Panthera’s words.

To cover his tracks, what with his son having noticed Nathalie’s study of Shadow Moth, he’d pointed out to his son that Nathalie had taken to wearing what appeared to be a peridot ring, that she’d said she’d accidentally found after looking for it for ages when he’d asked if it was new, recently so she clearly did like green.

Adrien had countered that she wore all different colours of jewellery but they knew she liked red enough to put it in her hair so he still thought it was a better bet giving him an excuse to give in without suspicion. Nathalie had said he should try to listen to what his son said more.

From the look on Panthera’s face he made the right choice.

“Aren’t you afraid of giving away your identity?”

“Anyone could buy this.”

“Anyone with the money to,” she corrected, turning them to catch the light, “but thank you.”

Chapter 17: Nathalie VI

Nathalie is a sensible adult woman who’s been successfully hiding her secret identity as Panthera from the man she lives with, works for, and is in love with. Never mind hiding his, and her own as Mayura from the entire world including his own son.

So there’s absolutely no reason why she should keep teetering on the boundaries of giving it away by letting herself get distracted by the memory of that kiss. By the heat of his mouth, and he pressure of his weight against her.

Maybe it’s just that it’s been a long time since the last time someone kissed her and it definitely hadn’t felt like that then.

Either time’s just dulled her recollection or it’s turns out that love, even unknown to him requited love, makes the difference.

“Nathalie?” Gabriel prompts again.

It takes all her power not to blush, “Sir?”

The kiss had been such a mistake, and she’d known it was a mistake but she’d got selfish and greedy and he’d sounded like he was choosing her and she’s been running on restraint for so long while he actively flirted with her.

But it was definitely a mistake because now she knows exactly what it’s like to kiss him, knows how his hands grip her like he never wants to let go, and he doesn’t know she knows all while she still has to work with him.

She really needs Gabriel to say half to what he’d said about Nathalie to Panthera to Nathalie so she has an excuse to initiate another kiss and then at least she won’t have to hide how affected she is by his presence as Gabriel anymore.

“When is Clara Nightingale supposed to come in for her consultation again?”

“11 o’clock but that’s not until tomorrow.”

“I know. But I was thinking of having it in the larger atelier.”

“You were thinking of leaving the house?”

“Hmm. It seems silly to have the dress shipped here away from the team actually working on it, and making them come over.”

“It’s how you’re been working for the last few years sir,” Nathalie tries to just about fall on the side of polite reminder rather than reprimand.”

“I know. But I think I’m going to be making some changes to my life. Of course if I was designing something for you that would be quite a different matter. It would be stupid to leave here for any sort of fittings.”

It almost feels like he’s trying to fluster her, and it’s almost a retaliation to say, “are you expecting to be making me anything?”

“If certain things play out there might be a need for it.”

“Would certain things include-“

A knock on the Atelier door breaks things up, the clock on the corner of her screen suggests it’s likely Adrien back home for lunch.

“Adrien,” she lets Gabriel know, given his ability to lose track of time, “I’ll let him in, or are you too busy?”

“He’s already distracted us. Might as well see what he needs now.”

“Yes sir,” she gets up at goes to the door, still not quite used to the fact that she can mostly do without the exoskeleton now and that she no longer has to worry about Gabriel noticing that much at least.

Adrien looks at her oddly as he comes in, and she surreptitiously checks her reflection in the her screen as he asks his father if he could go to cheer on his fencing team at their tournament even though he can’t compete.

She interjects then, reminding Gabriel that Adrien had been originally expected to attend as a competitor and thus there’s room in his schedule before going back to her inspection of herself.

Her hair is perfectly in place. Her red streak a little grown out but not in need of a touch up yet. There’s nothing on her face, or obvious smudge on her glasses. Her lipstick and eyeshadow seem perfect and not in need of reapplication. Her usual scarab stud earrings sit safely in her ears.

She’s wearing much the same outfit as she always does working. What Adrien thinks is so odd isn’t readily apparent to her.

Oh god.

She hopes that her excitement from the other day isn’t that obvious.

Gabriel, apparently aware there’s no good reason not to, gives Adrien permission though he asks Nathalie to accompany him which urgh, Adrien aside teenagers are not her favourites but she can deal even if it means deliberately ignoring when he and his friends talk about things she doesn’t want to and shouldn’t hear.

When Adrien turns to let her lead him out the door he’s still staring at her strangely, as if looking for something.

Unconsciously she brushes her fringe back, “is there something wrong with my appearance Adrien?”

“Oh. No,” he holds up as hands in surrender, “you look fine. I mean. Not fine, um good. I just thought maybe you’d be wearing the earrings Father gave you.”

Nathalie freezes.

But Adrien is looking far too innocent and frankly unbothered for this to be his announcement that he’s discovered Shadow Moth is his father, and by the way he and/or Ladybug were spying on her that night.

He might be Emilie’s son but he’s not that good.

So either Gabriel bought something both for Nathalie and Panthera and…somehow apparently involved Adrien in that decision which feels beyond implausible or, what for some reason asked his son’s opinions on jewellery for Panthera pretending it was for her while having no idea Adrien knew Panthera?

That makes no sense.

But now Gabriel is looking back up at them to after having returned to his screen immediately after granted Adrien permission and all Nathalie can do is offer a warning, “your father hasn’t given me any earrings Adrien.”

Only Nathalie has apparently made yet another horrific mistake in spending more time with and letting Adrien talk his mind to her as he frowns and looks away from her and at his father.

“Father!” Adrien hands fling out at his side in annoyance, “did you just put them in Nathalie’s room without telling her they were from you.”

Gabriel has turned positively glacial, but she can see the shake in his hands as he promptly hides them behind his back.

“I have no idea what you’re talking about Adrien.”

Adrien’s eyes dart between them, “I don’t understand why you’d both try and keep this a secret from me. You both know I’d be happy for you to get together, and Father you know I saw you chose them, and Nathalie you know they were in your room yesterday when we were playing chess.”

Apparently Adrien has the subtlety to say “playing chess” rather than “checking in with you and the Kwamis” but not enough to realise that even if this wasn’t the tangled mess it is it still wouldn’t be helpful of him to stick his oar in like this. School with other people was supposed to help his interpersonal skills but it clearly isn’t working in this arena damnit.

She had planned to hide the damned things, and then try to find a way to secretly get rid of them. She’s sure plenty of other people would value them, but Adrien had come in too quickly and she’d never suspected this particular threat because she hadn’t thought he’d have any reason to recognise them.

“My new ones?” Her voice isn’t coming out anywhere near as calmly as she’d hoped. All she can do is pray Gabriel just blames it on her being embarrassed by the misunderstanding, “Adrien, they’re not from your father.” Then the stop his next question, she adds to her lie, “I bought those myself.”

From his awkward reach to the back of his neck Adrien seems to accept us, “I guess I should apologise for ruining the surprise? But at least Father didn’t accidentally give you something you already had.”

She swallows with as much subtlety as she can, “that’s a testament to how well he knows my taste I suppose.”

“I’m not sure it implies that at all,” and all Nathalie’s muscles tense at the rage reined in behind the sharp bite of Gabriel’s voice, “Nathalie could you see Adrien off to school.”

Confusion fills Adrien’s face, “but I’ve only just got home.”

“Have the chef pack up your lunch for you, and go eat it with those friends of yours you’re always claiming you want more time with.”

“I don’t-“

Gabriel looms over both of them. “I think it’s time Nathalie and I had a discussion. In private.”

The sudden excitement in Adrien’s eyes cuts her to the quick.

“Come on,” Nathalie places a hand on Adrien’s shoulder and guides him out of the atelier.

The boy seems to glow, radiant in a way Nathalie didn’t really think a person could be, like a beam of sunlight as he smiles once he’s out of the door.

“I’m sorry for making things awkward but maybe,”

“Adrien.” She takes up her wallet and pulls out all the cash in it and after a moments thought gives him her debit card too. It should take Gabriel a while to think of tracking that rather than the card Adrien has access to, or any of her Gabriel account ones. “Go back to school now and just buy lunch somewhere. Your bodyguard will take you. And….don’t come home until I text you.”

The light drains from his face with every word from her mouth, eyes widening in wounded confusion. “Nathalie?”

“And take this,” she slips off the Black Cat Miraculous watching it change shape before eyes as she slips it back onto Adrien’s finger instead.

“What’s happening?”

Instead of meeting his eyes she finds Plagg’s as the kwami appears behind Adrien. “It might be a good idea if you gave it back to Ladybug.”

“What?!”

She pushes Adrien out of the door, “and remember. If your father asks, you know nothing about any Miraculous.”

“I’d say that anyway but,”

“It’s important Adrien.”

“OK but what’s going on, what has this got to do with the earrings? Is this something related to Shadow Moth? Is it my fault for telling Father you had a crush on him?”

“It’s not your fault.” It’s her’s, it might be Gabriel’s, hell there’s even an argument for it all being due to Emilie and her single-minded determination blinding her to what she was doing. “But that doesn’t matter. Just go.”

She’d shove him if she wasn’t concerned about rebreaking his ankle. His hard boot gives him decent movement but he still moves down the stairs in front of the house too slowly for her liking until thankfully his bodyguard sees her distress and with a nod picks Adrien up and carries him to the car.

Nathalie knew she liked that man.

Then with a deep breath she turns and goes back to confront Gabriel.

It would be easier just to leave now but if Nathalie always did the easy thing in life then she wouldn’t be in this situation to start with.

So she doesn’t even pause by the door. There’s no benefit to putting this off. Gabriel’s mood will only get worse as he waits.

He’s stood leaning against her desk but he lets go of it as she enters.

“Was it all a lie Panthera?”

“Sir,”

“I never did pay you enough attention did I? I took your loyalty for granted, I gave you your,” he spits the word out, “privacy. But you can’t hide now Nathalie. I know exactly how you feel about me now. Tell me, how long have you been conspiring against me? Trying to find a way to make me give up on my wife so you could have me for yourself.”

“That’s not what I was doing.”

“Isn’t it? It was clever of you you know. To approach me twice and make it seem like the choice was Nathalie or Panthera, instead of my wife or an wannabe homewrecker like you. So much for your apparent love for Chat Noir’s father.”

It should hurt. Should feel worse than any blow Shadow Moth had offered her. But instead she it’s anger that rises in her, harsh and cruel, wanting to harm Gabriel the same way he wants to harm her for all his has a right to be cross, a right to feel betrayed.

Because afterall, “seduction was your idea not mine. If you remember I told you your plans with Panthera were a bad idea.”

“And yet you didn’t shut them down as Panthera.”

“You didn’t exactly give me a chance. And by the way, I wasn’t lying about the Wish.”

“Of course you’d say that,” he yells slamming his fist on the table almost making her jump, “but how can I trust anything you say now? You were supposed to be my ally Nathalie. Panthera was supposed to be my friend. And instead you’ve been lying the whole time.”

“I didn’t have a choice.”

“You could have told me who Chat Noir was when he gave you that ring, you could have not told me and just given me the ring if you were so set on protecting the boy, god knows why,”

“I couldn’t give you the ring or-“

“You risked dying because you’d found your way into my affections under false pretences,”

She risks extending a hand, “Gabriel,”

But he slaps it aside, and it’s more then shock than the pain that makes her flinch away but the air in here suddenly feels far too close and maybe she belatedly understands how it feels to be one of Shadow Moth’s victims.

“It was always what was right for Nathalie Sancoeur wasn’t it? Never mind what you said about wanting to help this family, wanting to help Adri-“

“Don’t you dare question my love for Adrien. That’s why I didn’t give you the ring, that’s why I told you about the Wish because that is the truth Gabriel, push me aside if you want to believe that. Don’t risk it. Don’t risk him.”

His eyes narrow and then his hand is on her chin forcing her face up to look at him him, “you do believe that don’t you?”

“Because it’s the-“

“Shut up!” He snaps, but then his voice softens again, “how did they get to you Nathalie? How did they convince you? What did Chat Noir offer you to make you forget myself and Adrien? Or were we what he offered? Did he tell you that you could be enough for us? Did he tell you you were saving us? Well I guess I could just ask your Kwami couldn’t I? Give me-“

Gabriel suddenly releases her. “Where’s the ring?”

“Gone.”

“Even now,” he looks mad, and she wants to draw him back to sanity but he’s out of her reach and she doesn’t know how, “even now you betray me. You used the fact I wanted to protect my son from all of this as a way to get rid of what I needed to save his mother.”

“No. No I took what could doom Adrien out of your reach. I can’t let you keep doing this?”

“Let me? My dear Nathalie. You gave Panthera’s Miraculous away. How exactly do you think you’ve going to stop me?”

“Don’t,”

“I’m sorry,” Gabriel says, “I really wish I didn’t have to do this. Wish you really were the woman I thought you were. Nooroo, Dark Wings Rise.”

Hawk Moth stands in front of her, and something at the back of her mind registers with surprise that she’s not any more frightened than a moment ago. Despite Hawk Moth’s increased height and presence what scares her is that this is Gabriel, and he’s never looked at her like this before.

“I’ve done all of this for you.” She begs him, tries to remind him, “You’ve felt my emotions, how can you think I was working against you?”

Something flickers in his eyes, “maybe you really did think you were doing the best thing for me. I’d like to believe that.”

“Do then, because it’s the truth.”

“I’m not sure that’s a concept you’ve familiar with.” His hand is surprisingly soft as it closes on her arm, “it would have been nice if it all had been true. I’d…had ideas for how I’d tell you about my feelings for you, introduce you to the press, and now instead I’m going to have to imprison you.”

“What? What the hell are you talking about?”

“I can’t have you running to the heroes. Or the police. Oh it won’t look good for a second woman in my life to disappear but it can be handled.”

“You can’t be serious.”

“You’ll be in complete comfort. I’ll have your bed installed on the lair. Have the chef make all your favourite foods to bring up.”

“Please stop and think about what you’re saying. If I wanted to betray you to the heroes I could have done that at any time already. I’m not going to-“

“That was back when you still thought the two of us could have a happily ever after together. My exposure would have hindered that rather, wouldn’t it?”

“Can’t we still have that? This doesn’t change anything.”

“It changes everything. It-“

And then the window next to them crumpled into nothing

And there Chat Noir stood like some avenging angel, a dark silhouette seeming out of place against the blue sky and the white walls separating the mansion from the road.

Her throat goes tight. He wasn’t supposed to be here, she should never have given him the ring back. She shouldn’t

“Get away from her.” Adrien commands his father like the hero he’s supposed to be and not the scared teenage boy he must be.

Though the question remains as to whether Adrien knows Hawk Moth is his father; just how long he’s been stood outside listening.

Worse still despite how obvious Nathalie feels like she’s managed to make it Gabriel very clearly has not realised that Chat Noir is Adrien, and as he turns towards Chat Noir there’s nothing but hatred in his eyes.

She prays in that moment that some small affection he had for Panthera remains and will remind him that they’d talked about Chat Noir, and how he’s just a boy.

“You.” His hand clenches on the hilt of his cane, “you’re the one who turned her against me. Or perhaps your father did. Either way I’ll enjoy this.”

“Don’t assume that old man,” Chat Noir quips back, repositioning his staff in his hands to attack but Nathalie’s eyes are drawn to the brace Plagg must constructed about Adrien’s leg.

“Hawk Moth don’t,” she tries to get between them, “you don’t need to do this. You’re better than this.”

“Am I?”

“He’s really not,” Adrien huffs.

Nathalie turns, “and you. I told you not to come back.”

“Nathalie, I’m here to save you.”

“Urgh,” she could tear her hair out, “why do you two have to be like this?!”

“Um,” Adrien and Gabriel’s response in concert would almost be funny if she wasn’t trying to ensure Gabriel doesn’t unknowingly fight his own son again.

“I’m asking both of you to stop this.”

“I can’t leave you” Adrien gasps.

“You now I can’t,” Gabriel sounds at least a little apologetic.

“Then I’ll have to make you,” she reaches for the brooch pinned to her turtle neck below her suit jacket, letting this secret free is the best of a bunch of only bad options, “Duusu, spread my feathers.”

“What?” Heartbreak echoes through Adrien’s voice, “no, no Nathalie you can’t be.”

“I’m sorry, but just trust me? I kept your Miraculous sa-“ she unable to finish her sentence as Gabriel yanks her back.

“Take off that brooch now.”

Trying to wriggle out of his grip, she bristles, “no.”

Gabriel tries to reach for her Miraculous, grabs her chest instead, and lets go like he’s burnt, “that’s my wife’s brooch you’re using to try to use to kill her,”

“She’s already dead! You admitted that yourself.”

Adrien reminds them of his presence, “What are you even talking about?”

“Don’t,” worry about it she means to finish but coughing swallows her next words.

“Oh I’m not falling for that again.” Gabriel says, “how long is it since you’ve actually been sick Nathalie?”

She tries to pull a feather for her fan, searching for something anything to keep Gabriel and Adrien apart while she can’t but her hand shakes as she does so and she can’t get a grip,

Then she’s falling to the floor, and Adrien’s screaming her name, and this isn’t right, Plagg was supposed to have stabilised her, but now her throat hurts worse than ever, and Gabriel’s just standing there staring at her.
