Her throat convulses again as more air pushes itself out of her lungs, even as she tries to cover her mouth like it matters if she spits saliva everywhere now. As it pushes it’s way through her mouth it’s oddly warm and salty, and when her hand leaves her mouth there’s blood on it.

Black spots take over her vision and her chest hurts the way it usually does after a transformation not during when the raw power of the Miraculous keeps her body going until it doesn’t.

But all she can do is keep coughing, and even Gabriel seems to realise that. Kneeling down and putting a hand on her shoulder as if they hadn’t been fighting moments ago.

“Mayura, Nathalie, breathe. Please. We can sort this out afterwards. Nathalie just keep breathing for me alright? We’re going to get you help?”

But she can’t do anything but fall into a warm shroud of darkness.

Chapter End Notes

Needless to say this developed a little from it's initial conception in Snippets

Chapter 18: Adrien II

“We should call a doctor,” Hawk Moth says to him, as he carefully lays Nathalie down on her bed. “Usually she recovers of her own accord but I’d prefer to have a second opinion. Especially since your cure,” his face twisted on the word. “didn’t work.”

The supervillain turns from the bed apparently looking for something, leaving his back open for attack if Adrien had the presence of mind to do so.

But Adrien was still distracted by how familiarly Hawk Moth had held Nathalie, how practised the action of laying her down had been, how he’d not questioned where Nathalie’s room was.

Especially as Hawk Moth pulls various monitors out from her cupboard, and starts to plug them in and then attach them to Nathalie. Gently pulling off her blazer, and to Adrien’s horror putting his hands under her top to attach electrodes to her skin.

Which did all make a sort of sense if Nathalie was Mayura and Hawk Moth had been by her side throughout her illness except no, that made no sense at all; because then why on earth would she have pretended to be on Adrien’s side as Panthera instead of just giving Hawk Moth the ring?

His heart jumps as Nathalie takes a gasp of air in below then but she doesn’t rouse from her unconscious state.

“She’s stable at least,” Hawk Moth says looking at the machines around her, “I’d thought… but we just need to wait now.”

“Plagg said he was helping,” even to himself his voice sounds lifeless, “what’s wrong with her?”

“Her Miraculous.” Hawk Moth said. Then as he picked up Nathalie’s tablet he clarified, “the Peacock Miraculous. It was broken. It,” he jabs harshly at the screen, “hurt her to wear it. Still hurts her apparently, which makes no sense- it’s supposed to be fixed, and apparently she was able to wear the Black Cat Miraculous just fine.”

Adrenaline has Adrien’s muscles contracting, ready to leap forward into battle once more at the villain’s admission that all this time it had been him hurting Nathalie.

The Miraculous might have done the damage but clearly Nathalie had worn it to help Hawk Moth. Or even if they somehow had some shared goal they both wanted he’d still let her wear it. Got himself cornered so she had to save him and unfortunately it hit a little too close to home with how Nathalie had worn his Miraculous despite her own illness, so he could easily see her choosing to continue. Claiming the damage was done.

“So this is your fault.”

“I thought I’d agreed with Panthera to give up that Miraculous once Nathalie no longer needed it to keep her identity secret in return for healing for Mayura. Only it seems that wasn’t the situation at all. So I’d suggest it’s you she risked her life for even though I can’t understand why.”

Hawk Moth looks down at him like Adrien’s a bug he’d like to squash. He supposes it’s nothing new. It’s pretty much how he imagines Hawk Moth does think of everyone except Mayura, who is apparently Nathalie, and it’s not dissimilar at all to what that copy of Hawk Moth had looked down at him as he was ready to drive his sword through Adrien’s shoulder.

It’s not how his father looks at him, even when he’s furious, or when he ignores him, and honestly Adrien had thought it was. Thought he was a useful asset at best, or a burden at worse and maybe his father, though he did love him, didn’t know how to see him as anything other than that. Only maybe that’s not true and he doesn’t know how to deal with that right now.

It feels like there was a rock in his throat blocking his airways, “shouldn’t we get on calling that doctor?”

Hawk Moth holds up the Ipad, “already done.”

“Right,” and he wasn’t going to cry, he couldn’t cry, not now. Ladybug needed him to be strong. Nathalie needed him to be strong, “I’m guessing you are Gabriel Agreste then.”

Hawk Moth stills, his attention tugged from Nathalie to Adrien, Adrien stays equally frozen caught in the stare of a predator, “tread carefully boy. Or you won’t be leaving this house.”

He looks the man who either is his father or has already done away with him. “Are you threatening to kill me?”

Hawk Moth seems startled into movement, “am I, am I threatening to kill you?! Good lord what is wrong with you?”

“I don’t know! What am I supposed to think of a supervillain who’s OK with his girlfriend hurting herself to help him?”

“Don’t you dare.” The man hisses, “Nathalie and the fact she apparently cares for you is the exactly what’s staying my hand.”

“Really? It didn’t look like you cared for Nathalie’s opinion down there.”

Hawk Moth reaches out, and strokes Nathalie’s forehead. “I’d had a shock. You wouldn’t know what it’s like to see such a betrayal from someone you trusted.”

“Don’t I?” Adrien can feel his lip curl, his voice goes harsh in an echo of the supervillain in front of him and he's carefully not thinking of the implications of that, “how do you think I felt when I saw her turn into Mayura?”

“Satisfied at how you’d turned my only ally against me I suppose.”

“How self-centred are you? Do you really think I was acting being shocked down there?”

“Nathalie is apparently able to a much higher level of deception than I thought,” Hawk Moth says, “how can I believe anything is true now?”

Adrien reaches out to touch Nathalie’s hand, his hopes falling as she doesn’t responds to it, “this isn’t a pretence.”

“No,” Hawk Moth’s eyes softens around the edges, “it isn’t. Was her recovery do you think?”

“I don’t know. I thought she was getting better.”

“So did I,” the supervillain suddenly looks a little less threatening, “she really lied to you to?”

“Yeah.”

Hawk Moth looks down at the woman in the bed, “Nathalie, Nathalie. What on earth were you up to?”

“I think she was trying to protect me,” Adrien swallows, “which is why I need to protect her,”

“Protect her?”

He stood out as straight as he could. “Give me you Miraculous.”

In other circumstances he’d laugh at the irony.

Hawk Moth’s hand goes to his cane. “And here I thought we were getting on so well.”

Adrien crosses his arms, and tries to stare down the supervillain and possibly his father, “We need to speak to my Kwami. He’ll know about Nathalie’s condition and I am not transforming in front of you.”

“And you think I’m going to let you discover my identity while letting your preserve your own?”

“It’s a little late for that isn’t it? So. Because either you’re Gabriel Agreste, or you’ve somehow done away with him but,” Adrien glanced down at Nathalie again, and how everything had started to go wrong played over in his head, “I’m pretty sure it’s the former.”

Much as he’d like to believe his father and Nathalie had sent him out of the room because they’d noticed something he hadn’t telling them Hawk Moth was coming he can’t convince himself of that. Because it had been his father who’d reacted about Nathalie’s earrings. And Adrien couldn’t see Nathalie appealing to Hawk Moth like she had if he had done something to him.

But Nathalie helping his father as Mayura. That, though he hated it, made a certain type of sense.

And it would, though he also hated, explain a lot about her reaction to Shadow Moth that was his father and she’d known that.

Hawk Moth’s eyes moved to the Peacock Miraculous lying glinting on Nathalie’s bedroom table.

“Don’t even think of creating a sentimonster copy of him to confuse me.”

“You’re cleverer than I thought.” Hawk Moth muses, “I always thought Ladybug was the brains of the operation.”

“That’s ironic,” Adrien retorts, “I always thought Mayura was that for you. Not that either of you were exactly distinguishing yourselves getting beaten by teenagers all the time.”

Hawk Moth’s nostrils flare, then he says, “and yet Nathalie apparently deceived you too.”

“Because I trusted her! You’re supposed to be able to trust the people looking after you, supposed to be able to trust your-“

He covers his mouth with his hand but Hawk Moth is looking at him with wide eyes. Horror scrawled across them.

“Adrien?”

“Nice try. But no.”

But Hawk Moth shakes his head, “god to think I thought I could use you to make money off my enemies image.”

Dismay at his father being Hawk Moth should have already rendered him numb but, “That’s your response?!”

It hurts. It hurts a lot actually. That his father doesn’t care for the fact he’s attacked him, that he could have killed him. That instead he’s just annoyed his little joke at Chat Noir’s expense actually had him as the butt of it. That both his identities are just money-making schemes for Gabriel Agreste.

“I considered it was you but I dismissed it. I thought I had proof it wasn’t you.”

“Did you listen to when I said it wasn’t me?” Adrien forces himself to say even while hoping that maybe his’s misjudged his father, that maybe-

“To think your Miraculous was under my roof all this time.”

And no. Apparently Hawk Moth’s only distressed he’s missed his chance to steal his Miraculous.

“To think I could have got your mother back for us, that we could have all been a happy family again, but instead I’ve been fighting you for nothing. That when I’ve been-“ “Wait.” Adrien held out a hand to stop him, “what do you mean about getting m- Adrien’s mother back?”

“She’d hate me if she knew what I’d done, to think all this time I’ve been attacking our son to save her.”

“To save,” it felt like everything started to fall into place, “that’s what you wanted the Wish for? You were going to bring her back?”

“Yes.”

“But. That, that would have meant…” bile burns at the back of his throat, “who were you going to sacrifice? Me? Wait. Nathalie? Was that why you acted like that with Panthera?”

“No,” his father reaches for him but Adrien can’t help but flinch back, “son it was never meant to you. Never meant to Nathalie. I-, I didn’t know.” Then he repeated more quietly, “I didn’t know.”

“You kind of sound like you know what I’m talking about for someone who didn’t know.”

“Panthera,” he grimaced, “Nathalie told me about it. She told me that she’d found it in her research but now I assume that it was your Kwami at work there, so I don’t even know if I can trust her information.”

“Nathalie wouldn’t lie about that.”

“Nathalie would lie about a lot of things. But no, it’s your Kwami I don’t trust.”

Adrien snorts, “I guess it’s not like it makes a difference either way. You didn’t give up when she told you did you?”

“I was going to,” his father says, “after agreeing to give up the Peacock I’d realised. Well. I was going to give up because it didn’t seem worth it. Going to start a new life with you and Nathalie. Those earrings were supposed to be both a goodbye and a thank you to Panthera for helping me make that choice. Instead….”

“They became what made you turn back into a dick.”

“Adrien Alexander Anselm Augustin Athanase Agreste how dare you speak to me like that. If this is what public school does to you then I am pulling you out the moment we get confirmation Nathalie is going to alright.”

“How can you lecture me on good behaviour when you are a supervillain, and a-“ it suddenly hit Adrien, “a, a cheater!”

“Be careful boy,”

“No! You said that you were doing this for my mother but then you were flirting with Panthera all the time. And you were, you know, weird with Nathalie too. You’re a two-timer. Three-timer? I don’t even know. Father how could you!”

“It’s more complicated than that,” but his father still looks away from him in shame, “Panthera…was supposed to be just a ruse to get her Miraculous. But she made me realise that your mother is lost to us.”

“And yet, you’re not willing to give up your Miraculous to save her.”

“I can’t see why I need to. Keeping our identities secret is a moot point at this stage.”

“And you’ve just shown me I can’t trust you not to go after my Miraculous if I drop my transformation first.”

“You, urgh,” his father made it almost a growl, “dark wings fall. Happy now Adrien? Now can we please talk to your damned kwami.”

Adrien had known Hawk Moth was his father, they’d just had a whole conversation on that basis. And yet it’s still hard to actually see his purple be replaced by cream and red. A nervous looking butterfly kwami hovering over his father’s shoulder.

“Claws in.” Adrien sighs

“Cat Kwami.” His father snaps, “why did wearing the Peacock Miraculous do this to Nathalie?”

“My name. is. Plagg.” The harshness of Plagg’s tone is new to Adrien. All the laziness habitual to his Kwami is gone, “and I’m starting to think it’s worth chancing making France the new Atlantis if it meant I could cataclysm you.”

“Plagg don’t,” Adrien

Plagg’s teeth are bared, “you’re right. I wouldn’t want to catch you and Nathalie in the crossfire. He’s done enough to both of you.”

“I didn’t do anything to Nathalie.” His father scowls.

“Why’d she put on that Miraculous then?”

“That’s irrelevant,” his father splutters. “And not what we’re talking about. Can you heal her or not?”

Plagg goes and floats over Nathalie, “sheesh. You should have yanked that thing off her immediately.”

“Trust me I tried,” his father sneers.

Adrien interrupts, “Will she be alright?”

“I can’t heal her kid,” Plagg saggs.

His father’s scowl got deeper, “You lying bastard, telling her that-“

“I was helping her.” Plagg interrupts, “I collapsed all the damaged magical channels that were leaching off her life support. Only someone apparently made it so she needed to wear Duusu’s Miraculous,”

“Which was fixed.”

“Which opened them all back up again. Any other one she could have used safely. She could have used mine but instead-“

“She sent it away,” Adrien thought he was hallucinating at the wetness he saw in the corners of his father’s eyes, “because she worried I’d hurt Adrien. Or win and wipe him out of existence.”

“Exactly,” Plagg bristles. “And now she’s paying for it. Because it’s never you that needs to suffer. Is that what you did to Adrien’s mother too?”

“I never threatened Emilie. How do you-“

“Just judging from the behaviour I’ve seen. It’s not like you held back from attacking her son is it? Or spent any time with her? How much could you love her when you treat the last part of her you have like that?”

“None of it was supposed to matter. I was going to,”

“Remake the world blah blah blah. They all say that. And I’ve liked some of them more than I like you. To think I thought you could love her and give my kid a family.”

“But he does,” a timid voice surprises them all.

Adrien looks past his father and sees his Kwami.

Plagg winces, “Nooroo I know you like to see the best in people but. Really?”

The other kwami seemed to puff himself up, “but he does. I can feel it. And we can still save Nathalie.”

Plagg shook his head, “I can’t. If I try now I’d risk taking her organs out too.”

“Not you. Tikki. And her wielder. She’s a guardian isn’t she.”

“Eh,” Adrien did feel he hard to point out, “in terms of being responsible for the Miraculous yes, in terms of knowing everything a Guardian should know um. She’s figuring it out?”

“Fantastic,” his father pinches at the bridge of his nose.

“I believe she could do it.” Nooroo says.

Plagg looks considering. “He has a point. Sugercube, her powers could point us in the right direction at least.”

Adrien tries not to let the hope in his chest unfurl. His wishes seem to not come true. Or do so in the worst way with a sting in the tail. “Do you really think so?”

“Yeah.”

His father picked up Nathalie’s tablet, looked at something on the screen, and then sighed. “Would she though?”

“She’s Ladybug,” Adrien reminds him.

“And we’d be asking her to cure Mayura,” his father replies, “Panthera she might still feel some...responsibility to, goodness know they were hardly friends, but Mayura? Me?”

“Have you a better suggestion?”

His father looks him up and down. “We could try using you I suppose.”

“Me?”

“Ladybug has a crush on you.”

Adrien blinks. And then tries to remember if either he or Nathalie had hit his father on the head during the fight.

“She, what? No she doesn’t. If she did we’re be dating right now.” This he’s not falling for. He might have been oblivious to a lot of things, and he might still think that Ladybug could return his interest in the future because they were best friends, how she’d stayed in contact with him during his injury when he’d worried she might just move on showed that, so maybe she just needed more time but he wasn’t deluded.

Ladybug did not have a crush on him.

“Not on Chat Noir,” his father dismissed, “on Adrien Agreste.”

“How would you even know that?”

“Nathalie told me. Ladybug has a crush on Chat Noir’s alter ego and doesn’t know. So unless you and Félix have been taking turns with that Miraculous she has a thing for you.”

Adrien tries to imagine Ladybug telling Panthera that and draws a blank. “Why were Ladybug and Nathalie talking about that sort of thing?”

“How should I know? Some sort of female conversation I suppose.”

Still weird but, “you think that will be enough?”

His father seemed to think it over then sighed, “No. Perhaps if- no. It will be helpful for the rest of it but…I think we’re going to need something more.”

“Something more?”

His father shuts his eyes. “I left your mother kill herself on my watch.”

Wait what?

Adrien could have fallen over in shock. What did that mean? What had happened to her?

But his father continues talking, given him no time to assemble a whole picture from the clues he’s been given. “I won’t let it happen again. Nooroo I renounce you.”

“Father?”

He pulls the Miraculous out from under his cravat, and holds it out to Adrien.

“Here. Take this and the Peacock to your lady right now and bring her back to save Nathalie.”

Chapter End Notes

Show I have kept this chapter back a few days as I won't have time to write next weekend? Probably.

Chapter 19: Gabriel VII

Time seems to drag on and on and on. The sun doesn’t seem to move across the sky, the shadows cast by Nathalie’s windows doesn’t seem to move inch by inch across the room.

But Nathalie’s struggling breaths, audible now in the silence left by Adrien’s absence, announce time’s passage nonetheless.

Gabriel wonders how many times her chest will rise and fall before Adrien returns with Ladybug. Whether Adrien will return with Ladybug. Whether he’ll return with Ladybug alone.

He believes his son wants to help Nathalie. He even believes he might feel some measure of guilt for having to be the one to finally pronounce his mother beyond saving. He wants to believe that his son still respects, cares for, him.

But this might not be Adrien’s choice.

Ladybug’s suspicious by nature from what he’s seen and what he’s heard from Panthera. She also has access to all the other Miraculous and people she apparently trusts to wield them.

Gabriel meanwhile has traded away the only method he has to fight back, has sent his son into the heroine’s den while still injured, his only real ally lives dying in front of him and as for the only other person he’d consider trusting with a Miraculous, well, Tomoe’s not one to join a losing side.

Something he’d always admired in her really. He’d thought himself cut of quite the same fabric except apparently he isn’t. He’d been unable to see that once Emilie could only be frozen on the verge of death that she was effectively dead. Her heart doesn’t beat in that casket. Her soul…Gabriel doesn’t know where that is. Doesn’t know whether had he won he would have traded back his son or Nathalie for some sort of zombie rather than the real Emilie. An automaton in reanimated flesh. An abomination.

The phantom splinters ache in the back of his thighs. Gabriel is the irreligious son of parents to whom church was more a cultural matter than one of faith but right now the crucifix he’d stared at on those high holy days they’d taken him with them to church seems to blur with the one that had stood above the altar at his and Emilie’s ceremonial wedding. Both suffering for humanity where Gabriel has made humanity suffer for him. Both sacrificing where Gabriel has, he wants to say he’d never asked for it and he hadn’t at first and maybe never would have done but once she’d made that first choice herself he had, asked for sacrifice from Nathalie.

Nathalie herself looks quite on her way to being able to haunt him for his failure and Gabriel hates that the only weapon he was able to arm his son with was the flimsy fact of Ladybug’s crush on Adrien Agreste.

Nathalie had seemed to think it ammunition but then she had clearly had a well-conceived plan of her own built on the secrets she’d been keeping from them all where Gabriel’s been desperately grasping for a solution since the truth of her secrets came out.

Funny really how even her betrayal had turned not on morality or anger at him but as it turns out, now he knows the truth of the Wish he would have made and of Chat Noir’s identity, from a desperate attempt to save him and the family he’d had left.

A devotion he struggles to understand the root of when she’d clearly not trusted him enough to just tell him the truth. Whatever she might have pretended more recently when she’d declared her faith in him as she’d looked into his eyes with her arms around him.

Even now it’s difficult to accept that was all false. Panthera and Nathalie were one and the same and that makes sense and it shouldn’t and it doesn’t and it does and Gabriel still can’t reconcile his desire and his sense of betrayal and how his heart freezes in terror at the idea he can’t save her.

And he can’t help question if Plagg had misinformed both her and Adrien. But he has to ignore that question. Has to assume he’s made the only choice possible to save the woman he-

The woman he can actually save.

That this is no renunciation of Emilie but merely a recognition of reality. That all he’s done for Nathalie now is but returning the favour she’d done him in saving him from killing his own son unwittingly.

A hitch in Nathalie’s rhythmic breathing has him startled upright with adrenaline but then she continues on steady as ever. He lets out the air caught in his own throat in response.

Hesitantly as if the atmosphere around them is suddenly viscous he reaches out and takes Nathalie’s hand.

The tension in her face seems to ease a little as her fingers weakly close around his.

“It’s alright my dear,” he belatedly tries to reassure her, “Adrien’s gone to get Ladybug and she’s going to make you better. I know you and she, and I and her, have had disagreements but Shadow Moth’s been defeated. That has to count for something doesn’t it?”

It’s too much a question in the still empty room.

“It has to count for something,” he forces himself to declare to correct that. “Besides I don’t,” he hastily corrects, “can’t think she’ll want to alienate Adrien by not helping you. Do you?”

Nathalie remains unconscious, not uttering a word. Her countenance neither shifts nor relaxes any further.

His tongue feels stiff and awkward and useless in his mouth.

So he returns to his long silent vigil, hand in Nathalie’s, until Adrien returns.

Time must move on because at long last he hears footsteps on the landing. Mutterings that could almost be Adrien and a schoolfriend attempting to sneak past the atelier. Only this is instead is Ladybug and Chat Noir bringing hopefully at least one salvation and perhaps another doom.

The door slowly eases open admitting two teenagers; his son and his arch-enemy.

The girl following Chat Noir into the room looks tense. The white over her torso breaking up her familiar red and streaked through her hair coupled with the dark glasses over her eyes declares her intention to flee should any sign of a threat emerge.

Rather a worrying indictment of her trust in Chat Noir. Especially when he’s just brought her victory in the form of the two Miraculous that had empowered her opponents.

“Ladybug,” he greets, as if manners have any place here, “or should I call you something else dressed like….that.”

Her chin juts out in defiance, “Pegabug right now. You’ll pardon me if I wanted to be sure of a quick way out in the circumstances M.Agreste. Or should I call you Shadow Moth? I’m a little unsure you see, about whether you’re the man I’ve been fighting, or whether you’re just the man Shadow Moth reminded Panthera of. I can see Shadow Moth fleeing when he knows I’m going to be here after all.”

Gabriel hates that she’s caught his interest, “Shadow Moth reminded Panthera of someone else?” But that’s him, and why on earth would Nathalie have told Ladybug that unless it was someone different entirely but in that case, “who?”

“Chat Noir’s father,” Ladybug is no less bold, “which then,” suddenly she seems to take in Nathalie on the bed, “would mean that maybe Panthera isn’t Chat Noir’s mother, and then that means that,” her voice goes extraordinarily high-pitched, “Chat Noir is your son.”

She spins back to Adrien who looks exceedingly sheepish and Gabriel wishes wishes wishes he knew what his son had said to get her here.

But still maybe there is a way around this, maybe they can suggest that Shadow Moth destroyed by his guilt in leaving Panthera like this had stepped aside. Had yielded the battle for Panthera’s heart to Gabriel and that he has of course never been the villain that Ladybug probably wants to throw in prison.

But then Adrien gives an awkward wave, “um hi.”

“Adrien?! No.” Gabriel is rather discomforted to see his some of his own internal reactions to Panthera’s and Chat Noir’s identities voiced aloud by her, “that’s not possible. You. I know Adrien. Or I thought I knew Adrien maybe I don’t know Adrien? But you’re so different. But Chat Noir wanted me to heal Panthera, uh, Miss Nathalie, which if she’s Panthera then uh, you are Adrien and I just want to disappear right now. And oh my gosh I had that conversation with-“ she puts her hands over her mouth.

“Yes Nathalie told us about your feelings for Adrien,” it’s not a helpful interjection, he knows it the moment the words leave his mouth but then if Gabriel’s impulsiveness wasn’t baked into him then they wouldn’t be here right now.

The girl makes a noise disconcertingly like a kettle, while simultaneously going almost as red as her uniform.

But then she tears her hands from her mouth, balls them into fists by her sides, takes a determined breath and says, “Panthera,” then she corrects, “Nathalie thought she actually had a chance of a serious relationship with Shadow Moth when he traded the Peacock Miraculous for Mayura’s health. And she wanted one. She hoped he might actually be able to give up trying for the Wish. Which he has now. So I guess I’ll be able to work out whether you are him or not by whether or not the two of you are together once she’s better.”

Well.

That.

That certainly answers some questions about exactly how much of Nathalie and Panthera’s behaviours and emotions had been false. Not that all of them could be. Nathalie couldn’t have lied to his Miraculous; as Nathalie she was spared because he didn’t look and as Panthera he didn’t understand the implications.

Other questions lie unanswered, and cannot be answered by anyone other than Nathalie. Ladybug and Adrien will of course back her up about the Wish. It’s Nathalie he needs to pin down and know if all of that had been for him or for her.

About if the idea of them had only come to her prompted by his own behaviour to Panthera, who he had apparently succeeded rather well in seducing, and then fanned into an idea by his choice to renounce the Wish.

Which he’s now definitively done while she can’t see it and he doesn’t quite know what to make of his feelings.

What to make of this new Nathalie he’s discovered will act independently of his goals, in defiance of him, which yes isn’t exactly unprecedented not with Mayura but he’d never expected her to keep secrets from him like this.

“Um, talking about that,” Adrien interrupts, “can you save Nathalie?”

Pegabug doesn’t quite let her eyes leaves him as she looks down at Nathalie putting her hands out to hover above her.

“What happened to her?”

Adrien’s hand goes to the back of his neck, “So you know Nathalie apparently which I am very carefully not thinking about right now.”

“That’s probably a good idea. Right now.” Pegabug nods.

Gabriel is overcome by the very strong desire to pinch at the bridge of his nose. His own mistake perhaps in bringing up the whole entanglement between Ladybug and Adrien and letting her let slip she knows Adrien which just makes the fact he never got their Miraculous even more infuriating even if he knows it is technically a good thing.

“And you know now that she’s Panthera.”

“I do. Given you asked me to heal Panthera and brought me to Nathalie.”

“Yeah. Well. So. This is also Mayura?”

“What.” Her answer is flat, and she looks likes she just wants to teleport out of this whole thing and for once Gabriel might have found something in his enemy he can’t even fault her for.

“But then…” She frowns trying to make sense of it.

“It’s why she’s like this,” Gabriel decides to explain, “she told you about how the Peacock Miraculous was broken?”

“Yes. And about how she’d agreed with,” the bug’s eyes narrow, “you because it has to be you doesn’t it? Adrien says she lives with you so you’d have noticed if she kept disappearing to be Mayura or Panthera. Except you didn’t. Because you were disappearing too. Which is why you never noticed Adrien disappearing either.”

“Clever.” Gabriel concedes, “but how do you explain your parents never noticing your disappearance? Don’t tell me you were honest with them. Would you have it they were secretly fighting you too?”

“My parents don’t try to monitor every second of my life and actually let me leave the house. But for you or her not to notice Adrien’s absence that’s inexplicable. Unless you were absent in a more literal sense than your normal too.”

“And what do you mean by that?”

“You’re a terrible father!” Bump her up a few years and she’d pass for a student political leader with all her righteousness, “you might control where Adrien is all the time, and what he can do, which by the way is terrible parenting. He’s 14 not 10 he deserves some freedom. But either way you’re never there when he needs you. Have you ever thought about how Adrien feels at all? About how he might not want to spend all his time being a model for you company? About how he might actually just want a father not an employer?”

Oh he’d be a good target for his own akuma right now. “Do you expect me to let my son, whose mother I have very recently lost, and who is extremely well-known wander around Paris freely like a target for abduction? Do you have any idea what might happen to him? How tempting a ransom might be for some people?”

“And who made him famous?” the brat crosses her arms, “Oh yes. You. Because you care more about what he can do for you than for him himself.”

“My lady,” Adrien tries to interrupt but she just holds up her hand.

“Chaton, he needs to hear it.”

“But,”

“Adrien,” Gabriel stops him, “it’s fine. I’m quite capable of defending myself. And,” he focuses back on the heroine, “how dare you say I don’t love my son. Don’t love my family. Why do you think I’ve been doing all this? Did Panthera tell you that? Do you think I don’t know I’m distant? That I don’t know I’m busy? That I don’t know I don’t know that I don’t understand my son and he’s too unlike me for me ever to do so? Did it not cross your tiny little brain that maybe it was for both of us that I wanted to save his mother? Or are your family so,” his lip curls, “hands-off that it’s easy for you to imagine a life without yours?”

She gasps, “his mother.”

“What did you think?”

“That’s why…”

He snaps, “why what?”

“Why Panthera asked me if I could save someone who’d died from using the Peacock Miraculous. Why she asked if it would be selfish of you to give up, but she didn’t mean you really did she she meant,”

“Herself,” Gabriel says quietly, and he’s still not sure what to do with that, “but perhaps she meant me too.”

He’d wanted her after all. Wanted Panthera and then wanted Nathalie when letting go of Emilie had become a possibility.

“It wouldn’t be selfish. You know. To give up.” Ladybug says with a horrible kindness in her voice, “I can’t imagine what it was like to lose your wife, or what it was like for Adrien to lose his mother but no one would expect you to do what you’d done to try to save her. No one could expect you to do that.”

“Emilie would have.”

Adrien looked startled at the mention of his mother, and oh god that’s another conversation that Gabriel really doesn’t want to have that now seems inevitable.

“But that’s. That’s sort of selfish I mean,” a sudden wince heralds the realisation this is her partner’s mother she’s talking about, and then she’s blushing again, “I’m not calling her selfish, I guess she didn’t know about the cost and-“

“Enough,” Gabriel puts a long-overdue halt to this embarrassment. “You don’t need to convince me to give up. I’ve already done so. Now can you actually do something for Nathalie, or have I just summoned you here to embarrass yourself and my son?”

Pegabug looks down at Nathalie again, “she said she had to way to help Mayura. But if she was Mayura then….”

“Apparently Plagg did have a way of helping her. The Peacock Miraculous had left broken magical pathways was how he explained it and he was destroying those. So she would have been fine even if he couldn’t make her like she used to be. Only then she wore the Peacock again and. Now she’s like this.” Adrien finishes in a hurry.

“Right,” she frowns again but it does seem to be in thought rather than in anger. “Why’d she put it on then?”

“I’m not sure her Kwami had mentioned the danger,” Gabriel answers before Adrien can.

But his son glares up at him, “because father frightened her. She thought she had to. To protect me.”

Gabriel takes a deep breath, and corrects his son. “To protect us. She didn’t want me to do something I’d later regret.”

“Huh.” Pegabug says.

Irritated he looks away from Adrien and at her. “What?”

“I couldn’t reconcile her being Mayura and her not telling you when she got the ring. Even if she discovered the cost of the Wish. To me and Chat Noir the two of you always seemed so, you know, close. And that seemed even stranger once I worked out who you really were. I mean why would she do all this for you when you’re not even a couple and then lie about Chat Noir. Because she’d have to believe in you to support you right? But I guess you’ve just given me my answer.”

“Talk clearly for once.”

“She might have loved you but she clearly didn’t trust you.”

Rage held in abeyance by Nathalie’s collapse earlier makes a sudden resurgence up his gullet, burning behind his heart, “Get-“

In the absence of his cane his hands find each other behind his back, nails digging into his palm while he forces himself to calm down, to remember he needs this blasted bug for Nathalie’s sake. Because maybe it’s all a mess and maybe he despises Ladybug and is furious with Adrien and even moreso with Nathalie but he needs her alive to have that conversation with her.

Even if he’s not sure what it is.

“Get to healing her,” he finishes gruffly and from the looks the teenagers are sending him he hasn’t fooled anyone.

“Then you get out.” The girl says.

Adrien looks between them uncertainly, “my lady?”

“I need to talk to Plagg and Tikki and I’m not doing that with you here.”

“Couldn’t you,” Adrien makes a strange movement with his hands, apparently some sort of code between the heroes.

Or perhaps not with the furrow between her eyes it gets in answer, “couldn’t I?”

“How did you two always defeat me.” He wonders aloud.

“Maybe you should think about what that says about you,” Pegabug says, “and yes Adrien I could just ask Tikki to stop transforming me and leave Kaalki protecting my identity. Which I’m going to have to do given,”

“You don’t trust me either. Even if you like me.” Adrien echoes her words back to her and making Gabriel prouder of him than he was the last time he aced his piano exam.

“It’s not that. It’s your father. And your,” she paused looking unsure, “Nathalie. It’s not fair of me to expect you to lie to them.”

“Sure.” Adrien mutters.

It’s somewhat annoying to have confirmation of the fractures between the heroes now when he can’t use it, and when it actually would be in his favour to have everything as smooth as possible.

Gabriel sighs internally. He needs this all over with. “Adrien?”

“Yes father.”

“Make sure she helps Nathalie.” He takes a step forward, “I do know what it is to need privacy while pulling off a masterpiece after all.

Chapter 20: Nathalie VII

Red surrounds her, a deep featureless crimson in all directions. Though the longer she looks the more that isn’t quite true. Darker lines criss-cross her horizon, black spots hover in front of her, and even hints of blue rim flash off.

Sunlight shining through closed eyelids her mind belatedly provides.

She’s not dying then.

She thinks anyway.

With awareness comes a bone-deep weary ache. Shouldn’t she lose that if she was slipping away from life?

Perhaps not. People do sometimes die in pain don’t they?

Gabriel surely wouldn’t deprive her of painkillers to ease her passing though. She’d haunt him to distraction if he did. But no, despite how she can feel her heart beat hard against her ribs at the memory of Gabriel’s anger he’d been frightened too at the end.

That she can remember.

Him begging her to come back.

It almost feels like she’s hearing him right now.

Maybe she is hearing him right now?

The words seem slightly different from before. There’s no Miraculous on her breast to broadcast his emotions to her but still this doesn’t sound like the panic before though her ears are being sluggish and she can’t make sense of the words.

But hmm, that’s different, he sounds different, like he’s asking her maybe, instead of commanding her. Different.

Maybe Nathalie could like different?

If she wakes up that is.

There’s a rough warmness brushing across her, across her, her hands? She thinks that’s her hands being touched. She tries to make out where that sensation move lies but she doesn’t think she succeeds. If she does then she can’t feel that but neither can she feel any response so she’ll guess not.

Instead Nathalie tries to attempt opening her eyes but the damned things feel welded shut.

It would be so much easier to let go. To just sleep.

But there’s Gabriel. And Adrien. And she doesn’t know what’s happening.

She can’t stop everything going wrong won’t have a chance to clear everything up unless she can just wake up.

Only even as more sensations begin to return, that ache getting worse, and actual shots of sharp pain in her chest she still can’t more.

The voices change sometimes. Once she even thinks she recognises Ladybug and if Ladybug is in the house then things have all gone so wrong and she’s stuck here unable to fix things.

She feels a little more relaxed when after that she feels that weight on her hands, and those flutters against her forehead, that she’s tentatively named Gabriel even though he speaks little these days.

But then it’s at Adrien’s voice that her sticky crusted eyelids finally manage to open.

“Nathalie?” her boy beams like sunlight, “Nathalie stay awake,” his head turns towards the door shielding her from the force of her yell, “FATHER,” then he’s back looking at her, “stay with me. It’s going to be alright.”

But she eyes are just so heavy and with all the willpower in the world she can’t.

Gabriel’s there the next time she wakes. Her mouth is dry when she tries to speak, and nothing comes out but she manages to squeeze her hand around his, and he looks at her like she’s a miracle.

And then it becomes more and more often; her body responds to her, she’s awake for longer, she manages to sit up, to ask what happened and listen while Adrien gives her a clearly edited take.

She holds him against her chest when one day he breaks down in tears and babbles about losing her too, and why did she do it, and she should have told him.

Her state is a convenient excuse for why she just holds him and doesn’t explain anything more than just the necessary reassurances. Her throat is sandpaper, and she just doesn’t have the energy but really it’s cowardice.

Nurses come, paid for their discretion, and help her get out of bed and clean herself.

One day a physio even comes but Nathalie can feel the power thrumming through her veins, however she’s been healed it’s no feat of modern medicine or natural recovery of the body. Her limbs are sluggish from lack of use but she can feel the strength recovering in them.

Nathalie hasn’t seen Gabriel since she’s been able enough to follow, if not hold, a conversation.

She sits in front of her unlit fire, hair damp and dressed an oversized hoodie and leggings she hasn’t worn for years. It’s fitting really. She feels younger like all her defences have been taken away from her; she’s too weak to use her martial training, underdressed without the formality of her work suits, and her veneer of unbothered competence has been stripped away entirely when her body failed her.

The chess board sits on the table by her. Pieces still lying in place from a game she and Gabriel had started and never finished.

Maybe it’s a sign.

“Nathalie,” Adrien calls as she opens the door, and that’s one of the downsides to being an invalid. People forget you might want privacy, or make up reasons it’s unimportant.

Perhaps Adrien is his father’s son after all. Gabriel had certainly never had any issues ignoring boundaries and taking what he wanted.

She reproaches herself the moment she has the thought. Out of the two Agrestes she’s betrayed only Adrien has come to her each day to help her through her recovery. The difference between father and son is stark.

“Good morning Adrien,” she forces the irritation out of her voice only to end up with a monotone more suited to an executive assistant ready to put up with a bunch of aggravation on her boss’ behalf.

“Afternoon actually,” she hates that she can see the pretence in the lightness of his answer; that’s she hurt him again, “I brought lunch up. I thought we could eat together.”

“Oh,” she shifts her chair a little to make room, “that was nice of you.”

An uneasy silence reigns as he lifts the lids to reveal their plates and sets things out on the trolley.

“How’s school going?” Nathalie asks eventually in an awkward attempt to break the silence.

“Oh you know,” it’s a sharp turnaround from the boy who’d been so delighted to go to school, “it’s going.”

She leans forward, she’s not quite sure what her place is in relation to Adrien now, what he’s willing to grant her, never mind that it’s probably definitely not her job anymore but she can’t help but worry.

“Is something wrong? Do I need to talk to your teacher? Or the administration?”

He holds up his hands to stop her, “no, no, no, it’s nothing like that. I like school,” but his flat voice doesn’t support his words, “so please don’t tell Father any of this.”

“That’s not a problem,” for her to tell Gabriel she’d have to see Gabriel.

A shadow crosses Adrien’s face, “Nathalie. Father, he,”

“No.” She stops him firmly, “we’re talking about whatever your problem with school is.” “But,”

“We can talk about him after.”

“I have to go back to school after.”

“So we’ll talk about it after school.”

His lips set mulishly but he huffs, “fine.”

Nathalie waits patiently. It’s easier when there’s something to wait for.

“I hate lying to my friends.”

She tilts her head.

“I know I had to keep Chat Noir a secret too but…that was different. That felt like a nice secret, like I had this other life where I could be me and,”

“You don’t think Chat Noir is you surely.”

His mouth opens with an apologetic tilt forward of his head and Nathalie expects some mixed spiel of justification and apology to come, but then he suddenly squares his shoulders and looks for a second remarkably like a younger version of his father.

Give him glasses and the illusion would be surprisingly perfect.

But then a viridian flame in his eyes makes it quite clear this is Emilie’s son.

“Chat Noir is when I get to be free. To not have to be Father’s perfect son, or the obedient brand ambassador, or Paris’ favourite poor little rich boy.”

“Adrien,”

“They, Ladybug, everyone, they look at Chat Noir without preconceptions and he can do whatever he wants.”

“I’d hardly say that,” she means it to come out more sympathetic than it comes out. “Chat Noir is expected by Paris to be the hero, to be Ladybug’s support. Do you think you’d be allowed to break out of that structure? That you’d be allowed to do anything with that ring? Ladybug even objected to you giving it to me.”

“But…but you two kept on working as a team. And I mean, I did give it to Mayura so,”

“So you managed to stop Hawk Moth.” Nathalie says firmly. If Ladybug wants to blame Chat Noir for giving his Miraculous to a villain she’d better remember that too.

Adrien goes still.

“Your father gave up the Miraculous didn’t he?”

“I’m not supposed to be stressing you out?”

“There’s no other way I’m sitting here healed by magic and he’s not in prison. And besides he’d pretty much agreed to do so anyway.”

“He…had?”

Nathalie can’t help her frown. “Do you and Ladybug not talk? But yes, he had. Do you really think I’d let him go and make that Wish knowing what the cost was?”

“You helped him before.”

Oh this conversation has gone driving off in a direction Nathalie neither wants nor is ready for, “I didn’t what the price of winning would be, I didn’t know I was fighting you. Adrien the moment I found out that changed everything. I know that I’ve often been the tool your father has used to control your life, and I recognise that you haven’t always been happy about that and that I’ve done things I regret. But I have never ever wanted to hurt you. I promise you that.”

“I know, you always looked like you felt bad about it.”

Somehow that doesn’t feel like enough, she reaches up over the table to take his hands, “I know I only work for your father, and that I’ve deceived you so many times, but to me you’ve become more than that. You’re the closest thing to family I have.”

“Nathalie,” his voice breaks, “you have to know you’re family to me right?”

“I hoped so,” she smiles but she can feel a treacherous trickle at the corner of her eyes.

Adrien knocks the air out of her as he traps her into her hug at a run.

“It’s alright,” she tries to sooth him as she rubs his back, “I’m alright and we…we have time to work this out. It’s all going to be fine Adrien and we’re all here, and even when I’m not you’ll know that I love you OK? If something’s wrong you just ring me.”

She’s telling herself this as much as she’s telling Adrien.

“Why would you not be here?”

Nathalie blames the time unconscious for how she’s lost hold of everything, “well, who knows what the future might bring,” she says airily, and then adds hopefully more convincingly, “but anyway I won’t be there at school. Or when you’re Chat Noir.”

“I don’t have a reason to be Chat Noir anymore.”

“Hmm. Don’t you and Ladybug have things to talk about? I doubt she wants to be photographed with Adrien Agreste much as that might delight your father.”

“I’m not sure Father would like that.”

“Oh he’d be horrified. But also unable to resist the PR opportunity. And then horrified again that his brand would owe something to his enemy. It would be great fun to watch.”

Adrien’s nose wrinkles as he looks up at her, “is that something Panthera would say?”

“Probably not. Panthera was trying to get your father to listen to her because Panthera was me.”

“But don’t you see Panthera and Shadow Moth they could do things you and Father couldn’t let yourselves. Even if you were constrained as an apparent hero and villain. That’s what being Chat Noir is like for me!”

“Panthera could say things I couldn’t but that was…that was very much in reference to your Father and what I could get away with. I never had any real different personality- that would have felt like far more effort than I had the energy to do.”

Adrien lets go of her and mounts a very unimpressed face, “right so you want me to believe all that, he starts to flutter his eyelashes and his voice goes to a higher pitch ‘oh Shadow Moth I should stay away from you but I’m just so drawn to you’ was you?”

“Firstly I didn’t sound like that. And secondly given that we’ve just established you’re the closest thing I have to son I am not going to discuss my romantic life with you.”

“Don’t you have to if it’s with my Father? Given that would kind of involve me.”

She looks at his Cheshire ca-

Huh.

“Now that face is Chat Noir behaviour,” she sighs, “you and your father. You’re not like me. I was…pragmatic as Panthera. My goals were to get what I needed done. The two of you are different you do take your alter egos as a chance to do things you can’t as Gabriel and Adrien Agreste. Maybe even things you didn’t even realise you wanted as yourselves. But though your way of doing things might have been healthier than your father’s and maybe it’s something you needed I don’t think Chat Noir is any more your true self than Shadow Moth is your father’s.”

Downcast he does an uncharacteristic pout, “I don’t want to believe Shadow Moth is what my father truly is but…”

“Tell me, does Chat Noir have time to be sad?”

“He’s there to fight help Ladybug fight, there’s not really, we can’t talk about personal stuff.”

“Hmm. And you’d go up to, I don’t know, Kagami and flirt the way Chat Noir does?”

Adrien’s nose wrinkles, “I don’t think Kagami would like that.”

“Alright,” Nathalie casts her mind around Adrien’s various other friends and classmates looking for one hopefully Ladybug-esque and receptive, “what about Marinette?”

“Marinette’s just a friend.” His answer is a little too hurried.

Which. Interesting but not what Nathalie is actually going for right now.

“So?” She says instead of prodding. “Chat Noir turned his joking-so-it’s-not-threatening flirting on some of the temporary heroines.”

“That was just being charming,”

Oh Adrien definitely is Gabriel’s son. How did she never see this before. “Right.”

“It wasn’t flirting.”

“Mmmhmm.”

“It didn’t mean anything,” Adrien throws his hands in the air, “it was just a kiss on the hand. Father does that to Chloé’s mother and that doesn’t mean he likes her.”

“No he doesn’t. But I wouldn’t describe it as not flirting. Your father does that to appease Audrey because she likes to feel men are interested in her, and it’s a sop to her pride when he decides to remind her of favours she owes him rather than vice versa. It’s complicated.”

“Ew.” Adrien’s gone somewhat pale, “Why would you tell me that?”

Because at least it’s not about her. Which. Very much not dealing with that right now. “You brought it up.”

“Not like that.”

“We’re getting off topic. My point is, yes, you have more freedom as Chat Noir but he’s a persona you’ve created as much as the one you present to the media.”

“Father created that one.”

She shrugs, she won’t argue that.

“And maman.” He admits of his own accord, looking away.

She reaches out and ruffles his hair. “And you can remake it once you’re 18.”

“I don’t think Father would like that.” Adrien says as he tries to force his hair back into submission.

“I’d like to think I’d tutored you well enough you could get into a good university, and then a good job. Plus with your inheritance from your mother and your earnings as a model you’d hardly be without resources.”

He frowns, and part of Nathalie melts at his confusion. Oh way can’t she just hold him safe here forever instead of dealing with all this difficulty?

“Are you trying to tell me I should break away from my father the moment I’m 18?” “No. I hope very much that you and your father can work things out. He does love you you know, you’re why he gave up.”

“Uh,” now his forehead wrinkles even more, “he gave up for you.”

“He only did that because he’d already given up on the Wish. Because he couldn’t risk it taking you. If he’d still been planning to fight for it then he might have given you one, he’d already done that for me once. But he wouldn’t have given up both, given up his chance.”

“Nathale he loves you.”

She pushes her hair back into an non-existent ponytail, “your father did,” god she can’t say it, “care for me. But that was before he discovered I betrayed him.”

“You betrayed me too and I don’t suddenly hate you.”

Nathalie studies Adrien but she can’t make him out, “are you angry with me?”

“Not really. I think it all sort of drained out while we were waiting for you to wake up,” he shrugs, “and you know I am angry at Father and that’s…easier. Plus I’ve been talking to Plagg a lot and I mean, you did try and protect me the whole time and pretty much from when I gave you the ring you did do the right thing. Just not the honest one.”

“I’m sorry.” She offers. “I was trying to protect you but I should have been honest with you. And,” she sighs, but it has to be said, “I know we spoke a bit about this before you knew the whole of it but, I’m sorry that I thought your father was right. Not entirely. I always wished I could get him to spend more time with you, let you have some more freedom but I honestly did think that saving your mother was the best thing for both of you.”

“You should be sorry.” Adrien retorts but then he softens, “but it’s easier to believe that you honestly believed it than Father.”

“Adrien,” Nathalie tries.

He ignores her intervention. “When you think it it’s not selfish. Father…it was what he wanted so of course he thought it was the best thing.”

“It was hard for him, watching your mother die.” It had been hard for Nathalie and she hadn’t even been particularly close to Emilie. If she hadn’t seen the fading of such a vibrant woman in front of her eyes, and the effect it had had on Gabriel and Adrien then Nathalie would never have let guilt coax her into continuing to try to help the Agrestes find the solution after it was, as she knows now, too late.

“I was hard for me too.” Adrien mumbles, and she winces as she sees him picking at his fingers.

Then he looks up. “Nathalie?”

“Hmm?”

“How did you justify it? I know you wanted to help my family but how could you think what you did to the rest of Paris was OK?”

She shuts her eyes and lies back, “oh I had lots of justifications. They just all seemed to fall away when I discovered I was fighting you. It wasn’t supposed to go on this long; we never meant for a reign of terror just to swipe those Miraculous, so you know really it was Ladybug and Chat Noir’s fault-“

Adrien’s looks unconvinced, “Uh,”

She holds up her hand, telling him to let her continue, “and Ladybug fixed anything so there was no damage done, and it’s not like either of us met people to be forced with the truth there of what we were doing. And I mean there was nothing stopping anyone from fighting back if they wanted to protect their families the way we did ours. And the big one of course: we could make it so none of this ever happened with the Wish.”

Adrien’s eyes narrow. “Mayura really is part of you isn’t she?”

“Just as Chat Noir is part of you. Adrien there’s nothing stopping you being a little bit more like him with your friends. If he truly was you why don’t you revert to being like him with them in private?”

“Because…because I don’t know if they’d like him.” He’s fiddling with his hands again.

She forces herself not to stop him. It probably won’t help. “I don’t think that’s it. But maybe you could try?”

“Try to be a little more Chat Noir with my friends? Is that even possible?”

Nathalie wants nothing more than to bring up how different Emilie had been able to be in different environments, with different people but she’s not sure Emilie’s son is ready for that yet.

“Only you can say that. But. I know your parents have put you in the public eye and that makes it harder but everyone has to bend themselves in little ways to not annoy people and be someone they like to be around. Unless of course you become the personal assistant of a reclusive billionaire who pays you to be a dick to people on his behalf.” Sometimes Nathalie loves her job.

“Yeah I am getting how you were a supervillain more now. But I don’t want to offend people.”

“See, that’s you. That’s not just what I’ve taught you or what your father demanded from you or what your mother modelled for you. You’re a good boy Adrien. And maybe you’re just a good boy who happens to have a terrible sense of humour.”

Adrien’s jaw drops and now he looks betrayed.

“I’ve had to deal with your Father’s. Trust me. I can say that.”

“Father has, wait, do you think Ladybug doesn’t really mind either?”

“I think maybe that’s something you need to talk to Ladybug about that. And maybe you should talk to her,” and she does not want to say this but urgh the girl has healed her and she has a decent head on her shoulders and Adrien needs someone more than her, “about other things too? We’ve given you a lot to deal with. You don’t have to bear it alone.”

“Father would said I need to. For this family’s sake.”

“Your father hasn’t gone through anything alone since at least when he married your mother,” she catches the clock by her bed out of the corner of her eye, “and you need to get back to school.”

Adrien moves slowly, “Can we talk more? Later?”

“Yeah, sure.” Nathalie promises tiredly as if that could be enough to fix it all.

She watches Adrien leave the room only for Plagg to fly back moments later.

“You’re going to need to tell our kid what you’re doing now."

Nathalie stiffens, “I’ll tell him when it’s time.”

Chapter 21: Adrien III

Chapter Summary

I'm not happy with this chapter but in the interests of you guys not waiting another week have it :)

“You’re leaving?!”

Whatever composure his parents have built into him, whatever deference his father has demanded from him, whatever obedience Nathalie has asked for with a mixture of command and apology all crumble in the face of her awkward explanation.

“Adrien,” she sags back down onto the bed like someone who very much should not be leaving the people who can help her when she’s ill.

“No, don’t try that Nathalie. This is stupid! You can’t leave. Why would you even think that’s a good idea?”

“Because it’s necessary.”

He can’t help but cross his arms, “and how exactly is it necessary? Explain that to me.”

She stiffens, “don’t try to channel your father to me Adrien, it’s not going to work.”

“I’m not trying to,” he stumbles over his words, he doesn’t want to be his father, doesn’t want to force what he wants on the world, “be like him I just…do I have to lose you after,”

Oh and that choking hard rock in his throat is a surprise.

He’d thought that he’d accepted that his mother was gone a long time ago, but that was before he’d discovered his father had been fighting to bring her all back and Adrien had been the one stopping him.

And the stupid thing is that it hasn’t even made him want her back more. He loves his mother but he doesn’t want to die for her. Doesn’t want Nathalie to die for her. Doesn’t even want his father to die for her which doesn’t really make sense when she was always with him, and he never was.

Adrien knows it’s a good thing he stopped his father winning and he wants to have stopped his father winning, and he’s glad he won but it still…hurts.

Hurts to imagine her trapped underground in glass when his maman had been so full of energy and had chafed at the cage his father’s fame had created for them.

And yet, a horrible little part of him whispers, she’d strengthened those bars for him hadn’t she? Had encouraged his modelling career. Had been happy to be his main tutor while Nathalie had relinquished power and risked her positon by lending her voice to the cause of him going to school.

But that didn’t mean she didn’t love him, that had never been in any doubt, and maybe he’d played a little at pretending what Paris believed was true, that Panthera was Chat Noir’s mother and had wished Nathalie would show that love to him to but…

He’d never wanted to replace her.

Never wanted her dead.

Never wanted to lose her.

He’d accepted it, but that’s quite a different matter and everything recently; discovering the truth, nearly losing Nathalie too, has scraped open what he’d thought was a healed wound.

And now that he apparently is losing Nathalie it feels like something’s digging into it.

“You are not losing me,” Nathalie reaches out and puts her hand on his shoulder, “I’ll be right there at the other end of the phone. And…this isn’t going to be something permanent Adrien. We’ll work something out. Maybe I can go back to being your chaperone for shoots.”

“You know that’s not the same.”

“I don’t want to hurt you Adrien. But I can’t stay here. You must understand that.” Her voice is kind but that isn’t enough.

He knows he sounds like a prick when he huffs, “I don’t actually.”

She exhales, “you know I did what I thought was best.”

“I know.” He admits. That much he doesn’t doubt. Not at all really.

“And I don’t regret it. I’d do it again in a heartbeat.”

“I kind of know that too.”

“But I lied to you and your father to do so.”

“There seem to have been a lot of lies in this household.”

“That’s true but,” she repeats that odd tic he’d never seen before which she’d seemed to have picked up recently of pulling her hair back. “Look. How would you feel if Ladybug had kept that sort of secret from you?”

“Angry,” he admits honestly, “maybe…not surprised exactly but…angry.”

Nathalie frowns, “what do you mean you wouldn’t be surprised? I know Ladybug takes on a leadership role between the two of you, and she.” She shuts her mouth deliberately, “I mean I’m very thankful to her for healing me. I know she didn’t have to.”

“She’s not the sort of person who could stand by knowing she could help,” Adrien explains, “and she does feel grateful to you you know, she knows Father wouldn’t have given up if not for you and that.” He doesn’t like thinking about it but, “you did have my Miraculous and you could have stolen hers after you got close to her. Ladybug knows she’s lucky you didn’t.”

Plagg looked up from the wheel of cheese he’d been taking slices from, which Nathalie had apparently left out in anticipation in an attempt to distract the Kwami, “and I might have talked to Tikki. Given what you did for Adrien and all.”

Nathalie’s smile is tight, “I appreciate it.”

Things had been off between her and Plagg since…since she’d almost woken up really. Adrien guesses it’s to do with these plans of hers to leave but he wonders too if Plagg resents how she’d chosen to deal with his father finding out.

He’d have approved of getting Adrien out of it, and getting his Miraculous away, but it must be hard to transform someone and then have them throw themselves into danger without you.

Adrien wonders how many Chat Noirs or Pantheras or whichever names they used had done that to Plagg.

Whatever the reason it’s automatic to try to dispel the tension saying, “anyway you know Ladybug’s’s sympathetic to you, both of you, about why you were doing it,” maybe more than he is upside down as that seems but, “she’s a good person you know.”

“Umhmm,” infuriatingly Nathalie doesn’t volunteer anything more.

He can tell she’s waiting for him to continue giving him the silence to fill and yet it still works.

Adrien blurts. “She is. She just. She doesn’t trust me. And she’s right isn’t she? I have missed attacks and my father was Hawk Moth, and so how could she tell me her identity?”

“He’s not lying when he says he decided not to make the Wish.” Nathalie says. “You father… he’s toyed with giving up before but those times were different. He’s different. You don’t have to worry about him trying to steal the earrings of any girlfriend you bring home.”

“How? How was it different.”

“He thought he couldn’t win. Or that he couldn’t do what he needed to to win, couldn’t take the necessary risks,” she’s not meeting his eyes as she says it.

“He seemed pretty okay with risking me.” Adrien couldn’t help but point out and that that had been one of the hardest things to deal with. He’d known his father couldn’t, didn’t, seem to care about how he felt but he had thought he at least cared about his physical status. As a brand asset if nothing else.

“He’d vacillate.” Nathalie’s mouth pulls downwards at the corner.

“I don’t feel like fathers should vacillate about whether or not to endanger their sons.”

“No.” She sighs, “it was all supposed to be alright after but…”

Adrien let her trail off but he had to know, “Do you regret not doing something sooner?”

“All the time. Especially with how it turned out. You know he used to go back and forth about risking me too.”

That didn’t sound right, but then there was Mayura, but then, “he loves you though.”

“And he loves you.”

“I guess.” Don’t leave me with him please. They’d spoken more while Nathalie had been out of it and Adrien had been experiencing the weirdly dizzying effect of being treated like a peer by his father for once, having been his enemy apparently granting him a respect he didn’t get as his son, but since then his father had been going back to his old hiding trick.

He couldn’t live in this mansion alone.

“And he’s no interest in continuing,” Nathalie firmly declares, “he knows now that winning won’t get him what he wanted. So he won’t attempt that, you don’t have to worry about him trying to steal the earrings off any girlfriend you bring home.”

Adrien fought the urge to blush, “Nathalie.”

That was a totally unfair thing for her to say when he knew Ladybug wasn’t going to date him anytime soon.

“I can tell her that if you want.”

“Do that and I’ll tell Father that…that,” but he found himself rather short of equivalent ammunition. Telling his father about the conversations he and Nathalie had had before the reveal didn’t really feel equal.

“You father already knows everything I’d rather he didn’t,” Nathalie’s lips are pursed, “there’s nothing you can tell him to change anything.”

“I’m sorry for telling him you had a crush on Shadow Noir.”

“That’s alright Adrien. I’m the one who fastened the noose around my neck there.”

“You can’t really be leaving just because you’re embarrassed though?”

“It’s not that simple. I’m supposed to work with your father all day that’s, look it’s just not going to work anymore.”

“That doesn’t mean you have to leave. He wouldn’t want you to do that.”

“Oh he does. You know your father, he doesn’t like complicated. And I’m very complicated right now.” She reaches out and ruffles his hair, “I am sorry Adrien. If I thought I could bear it I would stay for you. But I can’t keep dealing with this, I’ll go mad.”

Adrien tries to get his hair to fall back into place. He appreciated the affection but his father hatted to see it in a mess.

“You get used to it. Him avoiding you.” The words sound weak even as he says them.

She shakes her head, “I really have failed you haven’t I?”

“He’ll get over it. I mean he traded with Ladybug for you.”

“That’s the problem.” Nathalie exhales, “I betrayed him, for his own good and he knows that but that probably makes things worse, and in doing so I forced him to give up his Miraculous to save my life.”

“You said he’d given up on the Wish. And that he’d given up the Peacock.” The little bit of suspsicion he couldn’t stifle raised it’s head.

“He had. But so long as he had the Butterfly he still had the chance that maybe he could find another way. Or maybe he wouldn’t and he’d put it aside when he was happy with the life he had. And I took both those choices from him. And your father isn’t a forgiving man.”

Adrien frowns, “that’s stupid.”

“I’ve been telling her that kid but she’s not listening.” Plagg interjects, “and neither has your idiot of a father.”

“I told you not to talk to Gabriel,” Nathalie said sharply.

“Oh you can’t imagine how much I’ve been waiting to say to that man.”

Suddenly she pinches at the bridge of her nose, “It doesn’t matter. I’m leaving and that’s it.”

“Where are you going to go? Have you even got another job?”

“I’ve savings enough to support me until I work that out.”

“You shouldn’t have to.”

“You know life isn’t fair Adrien. I could have put a stop to this much earlier. Should have stopped your father from,” her words come to a sudden halt, “let’s just say I’d do things differently.”

“Is this about what happened between Shadow Moth and Panthera? Is that actually why you’re leaving?”

Nathalie’s pallor gains something of a flush. “I’m not going to talk about that with you Adrien. Hell, I don’t even know why I’m telling you even half of this.”

“It’s nice getting the truth from one of you for once actually.”

“I guess it is.” She sounds chagrined, “I did tell him to tell you, you know.”

“Tell me, wait, you wanted Father to tell me he was Hawk Moth?”

“I didn’t realise you’d be so opposed to it.”

“How?” He yelps, sometimes for all the connection he feels with Nathalie he doesn’t get her at all.

“Your mother,” she says simply, “and I didn’t know you were Chat Noir. I thought it would help you to know what he wasn’t just ignoring you for work. That he was fighting to get your family back.”

“My family is right here. And it seems like ignoring people is his default when anything actually emotional is involved.”

Weirdly Nathalie’s face goes all fond, and the hint of a smile plays over her lips, “he needs time to work through things. He’s never been good with sudden shocks. But. He will get through it. Eventually. And Plagg will make sure he doesn’t forget you while he does.”

“I thought you didn’t want Plagg to talk to Father.”

Nathalie shrugs, “not where I don’t think it’ll be useful.”

Adrien looked up at her as if a silent plea would move her when his words couldn’t but whatever she saw in her eyes Nathalie remained stubborn and implacable.

“I’m going to miss you,” he gave it a last ditch attempt.

“I’m going to miss you too,” her hand reaches out for him, pauses, but then she pulls him into a hug.

Adrien lets her hold him a long time.

“I can’t believe you’re just letting her leave.” Adrien almost spits the words at his father, as they watch Nathalie get into a taxi, his bodyguard carrying her cases.

“You’re still my son Adrien,” his father doesn’t look away from the window, “be careful how you speak to me.”

“I’m the reason you’re not in prison right now. Or are you deluded enough to think Ladybug wouldn’t have exposed you if not for me and Nathalie.”

It’s something he doesn’t like to think about. Yet another reason for Ladybug to be uncomfortable around him. It’s much easier to focus on the other reasons she has, the one he’d told Nathalie.

He wants Ladybug to like him and to care about his opinion but on this one particular thing?

Yeah he’d prefer it more to be about Nathalie and forgiveness than because of anything she does or doesn’t think of him. He doesn’t want this to be a reason for her to resent her down the line.

But when it comes to fighting Shadow Moth you have to fight dirty and do things you’re not quite comfortable with, and this is as vital a fight as any Chat Noir’s ever thought.

“I have lawyers. And contingency plans,” his father scoffs.

“Sure.” Adrien agrees, “I’m sure that even if you managed to avoid prison the brand would get through this just fine. That Nathalie wouldn’t get harassed on the street every time you sent her out in your place. That my aunt would let you keep custody of me. Or my mother.”

That gains a stiffening and a slow turn of his head towards Adrien.

“Adrien,” there’s an obvious warning in his growl.

“Are you going to bury her? Properly? Somewhere I can visit? Or are you planning to make me lose every woman who’s helped raise me?”

He hadn’t meant to bring this up now, but in the moment it feels like the most natural thing possible.

“No one is stopping you visiting your mother,” his father snaps, “and despite whatever idea you have in your head I don’t plan to stop you seeing Nathalie either.”

“No. You’re just pushing Nathalie away, and leaving my mother somewhere I can’t go without being reminded that you were a supervillain who almost killed me multiple times, and that’s after I have to walk through the Atelier while you glare at me for being there.”

“Adrien,” and the change from anger to, what, uncertainty maybe, is stark in his voice, “I never. I didn’t know it bothered you so much. And I never meant to hurt you.”

“Yeah Nathalie said. But you didn’t exactly try and keep me safe did you. Adrien I mean, not Chat Noir.”

His father shut his eyes, “I should never has let any of that happen to you. I let myself get blinded by the power of the Wish, by the promise of none of this having happened.”

“Yeah, Nathalie said that too.”

“You too seem to have been speaking a lot.” There’s annoyance there yes, but also consideration in his eyes, like he’s assessing Adrien.

“Someone has to.”

His father looks away from him again, “I’ll have a more suitable resting place found for your mother.”

“Thank you.” He’s not sure he feels better. Not sure he will until he’s by her grave for real. But it’s something. “And Nathalie?”

“What about Nathalie?”

“Are you seriously going to let her think you want her to leave?”

The tightening in his father’s hands would have been imperceptible behind his back, but stood at his side as Adrien was he could see it all. “My and Nathalie’s relationship isn’t any of your business.”

“Are you really trying that with me? Even if your relationship was just, I don’t know, a work thing do you really think it’s fair to let her leave thinking you’re incapable of forgiving her for saving my life?

“I understand why Nathalie did what she did.” He says tightly, like the words hurt to utter.

“Well she doesn’t think you do. Seeing as you’re refusing to talk to her.”

“You have a very odd conception of giving someone space.”

Adrien’s whole brain froze at that, “what?”

“Nathalie’s condition was fragile. You know that. The last thing her recovery needed was the man who almost killed her in the first place bothering her.”

“You,” oh my god his father cannot be this stupid. On the other hand he did think it was a sensible idea to become Hawk Moth so, “you think you’re staying away for Nathalie’s sake?”

“You’ve said it yourself Adrien. I don’t know how to be there with someone. And I have been very angry with Nathalie even if her actions were justified. If I was to go to her…a confrontation would help no one.”

“Maybe it would actually get you talking. Father she thinks you don’t want to see her. That’s why she’s leaving.”

“She’s leaving because she wants to be away from me. Because it’s for the best if she’s away from me.”

“She loves you.”

“She loves you,” his father says it like it’s a correction, “that much of my conversations with Panthera were true enough. I failed to save your mother. I won’t keep you away from Nathalie. I know you’re important to each other.”

“That much?”

His father casts a considering gaze on him, “I have always wanted to protect you Adrien. But it seems I have failed there. So perhaps I should treat you as an adult here. Perhaps it will help you in your endeavours with Ladybug.”

“Me and Ladybug aren’t going to happen.”

“If you say so,” he says it so blithely as if it’s not true and he isn’t the reason it won’t happen, “but you must understand Adrien I’ve hurt Nathalie. A lot. Not least by not noticing her,”

“What?”

“I thought for a long time after what happened that she’d only given into my advances because of you, maybe even for your mother’s sake to spare her from what she’d come back to. I hated that. But I hated it more when I remembered what she said about your father once and realised that wasn’t the case at all. That she’d been holding back all this time.”

Adrien really really does not want to know any of this, and yes, he’d wanted his father and Nathalie together but not to know the details, and he definitely did not want to know what ‘given in’ meant but, “I don’t get it.”

“She loves your father you know. She told me that when there was no advantage to her saying it. She had other methods to keep me away.”

“You’re my father.”

“Yes.”

It feels like he’s fallen into some topsy-turvy universe. “How can Nathalie loving you be a bad thing?”

“You don’t approve of what we’d both done.” His father announces in an apparent non- sequitur.

“No. Of course not.” He says unthinkingly.

“I think perhaps that was why she did it.”

Not that on some level Adrien hadn’t known that, hadn’t known a simple employee wouldn’t have risked her life as Mayura but, “Oh.”

“Exactly. I am angry at Nathalie. I’m not sure I knew her at all after all. I don’t know if I can forgive her. But most importantly if I can then I think the best thing I can do for her is to let her go.”

“Are you going to let me go?”

“What on earth are you talking about?”

“Well, if you apparently ruin the lives of those around you, Maman, Nathalie, who knows who else, and you’re letting Nathalie go to avoid that then are you going to let me go? Or do you not love me as much as you love her?”

His father almost recoils, “That’s not the same. Adrien you’re a child. I’m not going to throw you out into the world.”

Adrien decided to ignore the fact that his father still seemed to think he was ten or something, “And Nathalie’s still sick.”

“She’ll have made arrangements.” His father tries to justify.

“You can’t be telling me this is what you wanted. That if I hadn’t blabbed about those earrings this is where you hoped things would go.”

“Of course it’s not.”

“So? Are you the man who was ready to remake the universe for Maman or not?”

“Adrien,”

Adrien ignores the warning. He’s not being grounded anytime soon. Not when he can jump out his window as Chat Noir and his father won’t dare to call the police. “Are you going to go out there and fight for Nathalie and that future you wanted or are you going to be a coward and make us both lose someone again?”

His father’s hands are now in front of him as he fiddles with adjusting his cuffs and it suddenly seems ridiculous that he’d thought him hard to read, “you think she won’t flee further?”

“I think you need to talk. What happens then is up to you.”

Chapter 22: Gabriel VIII

The door opens and all the air runs out of the room with it leaving Gabriel dizzy and dry- mouthed as he looks on the woman standing in the doorway.

Nathalie looks completely different to the image that haunts him of her lying pale and still on her bed halfway to a corpse in those black pyjamas that only made her skin look a more sickly shade of white.

Instead she stands there purely under her own power, in dark jeans and a scarlet jumper that echoes the warmth in her checks and her lips, with her hair falling free behind her neck.
