Longer and straighter than Panthera’s, and he’s surprised at how thrown he is at that difference, it has a healthy gleam to it.

Gabirel had known she’d been doing well. He’d read it from Adrien, read all the reports from her doctors, and then physiotherapists. Had she not been safe to leave the house he’d never have let her go in the first place.

But it’s different. Seeing it look this.

The edges of her ears are the first thing to move upon seeing him, and then her shoulders slump, her handbag strap falling from her arm to the crook of her elbow.

“Really?”

The strange atmosphere in the room breaks on her word. She might not look like his Nathalie, the one he’d known, but she sounds just the same.

It’s not exactly the reaction to seeing him he was hoping for, but it always could have been worse. He’d known that appearing unannounced in her hotel room was a gamble but this conversation is hardly one to have in public, and he’d not been sure she’d agree to meet from what Adrien had said.

“I’ve come to take you home,” he offers.

She throws her handbag down on the chair beside her bed and crosses her arms, “I don’t recall asking for a lift. Did Plagg and Adrien put you up to this?”

“They did both have rather a lot of words for me on the matter,” the damned kwami especially, “you seem to have wrapped that kwami around your finger. However do you manage it?”

He means it. First Duusu, now Plagg. Even Nooroo to some extent. The Kwamis seem to like Nathalie despite her forcing them to go along with her own plots as much as he does with his.

“I wouldn’t say that. Although he’s open to bribery with cheese. The more expensive the better.”

“I doubt that would work,” the blasted thing’s ongoing litany of the many many ways in Plagg’s estimation that Gabriel has apparently failed as a father, which is honestly quite rich coming from a fatherless and childless inhuman being whose slept most of his existence, suggests only a happy Adrien will appease him and that seems a long way off these days. “Apparently I’m a worse father than a villain and I’ve almost killed everyone I love.”

There’s faint wrinkles of disagreement on Nathalie’s forehead. “But. Adrien, you,”

“The episode when I akumatised his bodyguard I suppose. Though I suppose that’s only the most obvious, and there’s probably worse times if I consider when he was Chat Noir.”

It’s one of those things that Gabriel’s been trying not to think about. It’s so easy to do when Plagg provokes him and he can expel his anger out on someone he doesn’t care about hurting and think about nothing but that. It’s harder in the night when he dreams of all the various ways his son could have died at his hand.

Dreams of a soulless Emilie staring through him at their son’s portrait, of a woman who’s sometimes Emilie and sometimes Nathalie yelling invective at him for killing Adrien, of the smiling Emilie he might have created will the wish who looks at him in love and whose face creases in concern and confusion when he talks of a son and tells him they have no children.

“Oh,” there’s a pause then slowly he sees her indifferent mask dropping to showcase the softer Nathalie behind, “How are you doing with…all of that?”

His own shield of ice that sometimes feels like the only thing keeping him upright seems to fracture in response. That little bit of warmth from Nathalie enough to melt a crack wide enough for the whole thing to shatter.

For once he makes no attempt to hold anything back as he sighs, “How do you think?”

Nathalie brushes her hair back behind her ears. “I’m sitting down to have this conversation.”

Gabriel hovers awkwardly as she settles herself on the bed.

“Here,” she takes her handbag off the chair, “you should too.”

It’s not an offer to set next to her, definitely not an invite to her bed, but it’s something so Gabriel accepts the olive branch gratefully and sits down opposite her.

Nathalie curls her legs up beneath her in a move that doesn’t really fit with the Nathalie he’d known but does fit with Panthera, and then it hits him she’s not wearing her electronic supports.

She hadn’t before it all came out; he realises now that that was due to Plagg and not just because she’d been as fill of hope for the future as he was the way he’d egotistically thought. But even after Ladybug had intervened the Nathalie whose bed he’d sat by for so many hours had looked so close to death he’d feared she’d never leave that state.

“You’re doing alright?” He nods towards the absence of the prosthetics.

Her fingers tap against her legs, “whatever Ladybug did I seem to have too much energy these days. It’s unsettling. I keep waiting for the crash. But until then I don’t seem to be at risk of collapse. ”

He tries not to frown at the concern that stirs in him. He’s not here to reprimand Nathalie or scare her off but, “You should speak to her.”

“Have you?” She strikes back.

Gabriel looks down at his lap, “not since I gave up my Miraculous for you.”

He’s not sure why he feels so ashamed. Nathalie knows what type of man he is. That he’d stay in his lair and send out others to do his bidding and that’s the only reason he hasn’t struck his own son with his own hands much worse than anything his father did to him.

“I suppose I can’t blame you there.” Nathalie sighs, “I was tempted to run away after Adrien gave me his Miraculous and then Plagg told me about what would happen if we made the Wish in very quick succession.”

His eyes are drawn back to Nathalie, one of the few ineffective tools he has to try to gauge her emotions now that he no longer has the Butterfly Miraculous. Not that that helped him much.

Nathalie, he can’t help but think, must have felt a rush of relief when he’d confirmed that he didn’t use his Miraculous on her or Adrien. Gabriel now thinks it might have been easier if he had. Or maybe he hadn’t used it on Panthera. Things would be much simpler now then.

She sits there opposite him and waits patiently for his response. She’s always been patient with him.

“You didn’t though,” he points out slowly, the words delayed by the confused morass of emotion he’s drowning in, has been drowning in since he saw Nathalie again, perhaps since the moment she collapsed as Mayura.

“How could I? I had to somehow find a way out of this that didn’t leave someone I loved dead. Leaving you and Adrien to fight each other? That wasn’t an option.”

Loved. That last syllable dragging it into the past tense echoes around his skull.

“But it is now?”

Her movement is slight, someone else might not notice it at all but Nathalie is Gabriel’s entire focus right now and he’s known her for years. Felt her make the same preparations to attack when he’d held Panthera in his arms.

“I thought you didn’t have your Miraculous anymore,” Nathalie’s voice is tight.

“I don’t, I gave it to Ladybug to-“

“To save me.” She finishes for him. “Yes I know. So I can’t see there’s an issue anymore. Unless you were planning to try to take Adrien’s Miraculous as Gabriel.”

His nails dig into his palms. “Adrien and I am quite capable of fighting without it being a physical battle between magical superheroes and supervillains you know. Especially now he knows I was said supervillain, and that I almost got you killed trying to save his mother who’d died from the same thing which I’d help her acquire.”

“Oh my god,” Nathalie presses at her temples, “Gabriel Agreste did you seriously come here to try and get me come back just so I can referee things between you and your son?”

“Well he does like you more than me,” he tries to joke, despite the tightness in his chest her misunderstanding creates.

But Nathalie scowls instead of giving in and fear shoots down his spine.

Her chest heaves, as she takes a breath in. “Have I not done enough for you? I risked my life for you, I risked my freedom, even our friendship by lying to you. And after you made it quite clear that you didn’t want to see me, which fair enough I can understand that after how I deceived you, that fine. But when I try to make a clean cut of things and leave your house like you seem to want me to you turn up somehow in my hotel room, which I don’t know how you even got in and-“

“I told the receptionist I was your boyfriend.” He interrupts.

It seems to work to break her flow. “What?”

“I told the receptionist I’d just flown in last night and wanted to surprise my girlfriend, Nathalie Sancoeur, and asked for a key to your room.”

“That’s why the flowers?” Her eyes fly to the bouquet sitting on the table. “How the hell did that work? What if you’d been a stalker? Or just randomly targeting me? I should complain, or sue them or something.”

“That seems a little harsh. I did show her photos of the two of us together,”

“You have those?”

Gabriel decides to ignore that, “and admittedly I did bribe her. Quite a bit for someone like that I’d have thought actually.”

Her head falls into her hands, “I don’t even want to know.”

“And I hoped you’d be happy to see me.”

Her face reveals itself from behind her hands with a distinctly unimpressed expression, fire burning in her eyes as clear as in any akuma Gabriel’s ever created.

“You. Never. Came. To. See. Me. I was literally in your house, just down the corridor. But you didn’t, and you didn’t stop me leaving. And now you’re daring to come here because you’re hoping to push Adrien’s parenting off on me. While we, what? Just do our best to avoid each other in that house of yours?”

“I didn’t come here for Adrien.” He stands up. He’s let this misapprehension go on too long.

Nathalie shakes her head. “What?”

He kneels down in front of her and reaches for her hands to unload himself to you, “I was scared to talk to you Nathalie. You know I need you with me to give me courage and without you, without even Nooroo, I couldn’t work out what to say. I didn’t know what I felt except that I didn’t want to risk your health with another argument.”

Nathalie’s lips part, “So you stayed away.”

“So I stayed away,” she hasn’t shoved his hands off hers so he dares to close his fingers around her a little tighter, “Nathalie it would make my son very happy if you came home. And he would be slightly less angry with me, because he’s very cross at me for how I’ve treated you. But that’s not why I’m here. I’m here because I want you to come home with me.”

“You…do?” There’s a wobble to her voice.

“Don’t be so surprised. According to Plagg I’m very selfish you know.” He half-smiles up at her, trying to make his honesty clear in every way he can.

“I don’t know about that. You gave up Emilie for my and Adrien’s sake.”

“I gave up one woman I loved because I couldn’t bear to bring her back at the cost of one of the other two people I love. Or both had I kept on fighting my son and let you die that day.”

“You,” Nathalie swallows, “you love me?”

He’s never seen Nathalie look quite so flummoxed. Even that time he’d told her about his plan to seduce Panthera, to seduce her. It’s cute. And it’s an indictment of him.

Leaving just one hand on hers, Gabriel strokes her thankfully no longer hollow looking cheek, “I thought I’d told Panthera that.”

“You told me you had feelings for Nathalie. Me.” Her face screws up, and he’s glad he’s not the only one still struggling with how to describe those interactions now, “but those. I thought they were fairly new, that you wanted to try things, I wasn’t sure how strong they were.”

“Even after I risked my identity by asking Ladybug’s help to save you?”

“I thought you might have resented me for that whatever you used to feel. You did,”

“Pull a disappearing act.” He sighs, “I know.”

“It’s one of my least favourite of your traits you know.”

“That’s your reaction to me laying my heart out to you?”

“Well it’s not like you need me to reciprocate. You know how I feel.”

“No I don’t.”

“I told you. And you felt my emotions when I was Panthera.”

“Not the depth of them. I had no idea you’d risk me hating you all for my own sake. And I knew how you used to feel. Not how you feel now.”

Tears threaten in the corners of her eyes, but the line of her mouth is soft as she looks down at him. “Do you really think something like that can just change? I know you avoid things you don’t know how to handle. That wasn’t going to make me hate you.”

He wants to accept that, to luxuriate in the forgiveness she’s offering but he has to push, has to know there’s no caveats, not sting in the tail, “What about attacking you and refusing to listen when I discovered you were Panthera? Threatening to lock you up? And then almost fighting the son you’d been trying to protect from me? Would any of that be enough?”

“It wasn’t. Though…had you not stopped fighting Adrien knowing he was Adrien I think we’d be having a different conversation.”

Gabriel inhales. “If I’d do that then I don’t think you’d be here to have that conversation.”

“You’re a better man than that.”

“Only because you make you one.” He half-disagrees.

“I also helped you be Hawk Moth, I’m hardly some paragon of morality.”

This time he does have to argue with her. “You wanted to save someone completely unselfishly.”

“You did pay me to try and find a magical cure for Emilie and I failed. It didn’t feel right to leave you after that, not after how I saw you all suffer. And then well,”

The tightness in his chest relaxes at the reminder. “You fell in love with me?”

“Yes. And Adrien wriggled his way into my heart too. I might not have been doing it for me, but I was still willing to terrorise thousands to just make two people I loved happy. It never bothered me until I knew it was Adrien we were fighting.”

“Do you know I used to judge Ladybug and Chat Noir’s parents?” Gabriel muses, “I couldn’t believe anyone could let their teenager do that.”

“Once you figured they were teenagers.”

“Lots of people are very short. But yes, discovering who else they gave out a Miraculous did give the game away there.”

“Admittedly I do doubt Ladybug is going to grow that much more.” Nathalie presses her lips together, “should we have cared? That we fighting kids Adrien’s age?”

He considers it a little. “Even if the heroes were adults we’d still have endangered people of all ages. We put a lot of weight in that Wish to fix things.”

“We did.” She nods, but it looks heavy, like she’s weighed down by their mistakes.

That he understands. “God I wish I’d known earlier.”

“Would you have been ready to know?”

“Perhaps not.”

“I suppose. If you haven’t been speaking to her, and you’re here so she’s clearly not revealed who you are, then…is Ladybug just letting us both get away with it?”

“She’d probably pardon you regardless.”

“Adrien said that too.”

“But you were right about her affection for my son. She knows him. It’s not just a celebrity crush. I could tell that much before I gave her the Butterfly. I think she doesn’t want to deprive Adrien of his father after she wasn’t able to save his mother. Though admittedly I’m somewhat worried she might be just waiting until she works out who’ll get custody of Adrien if I go to jail.”

One manicured eyebrow raises, “And you still came after me? To bring me back to that?”

“Well I rather hoped that if I was going to spend the rest of my life behind bars that I’d get to kiss you first.”

“Oh,” and the slight part in her very pink lips is extremely tempting right now.

“May I?”

“Technically if it was only the one kiss you’ve already had it.” But there’s a hint of smirk in her expression, and she’s clearly ready to engage.

So he overs her the same buy-in she’s offering to him, offers a truth he’s yet to say out loud, “That was Panthera not Nathalie. And I wanted it to be Nathalie all along.”

“You did?”

She’s staring down at him as if worried he’s just an illusion despite his hands on her proving his existence.

“I told you.” He tries to remind her, “Panthera reminded me of Nathalie. Why do you think I liked her so much?”

“I thought it was the other way round. That because of your attraction to Panthera you were able to imagine a relationship. And there I was a real practical option that I’d accidentally seduced you in to seeing.”

It’s a risk, but “Were you trying to seduce me?”

“No! I had no idea you’d respond to me like that,” she pushes her lips together in that little pout again and her eyes flick down, “Plagg wanted me to though.”

“Ah,” a few more pieces fall in to place, “that change in your costume.”

“Quite.” Her tone is dry.

“So between me and your Kwami it sounds like you were actually putting in the effort not to seduce me.”

“It was harder than I expected, not giving in when you were trying to seduce me.”

“Oh god,” and yes he’d known it already, had been reminded of it by Plagg and damn had he missed Nooroo then in a way he’d never expected “I told you to your face that I planned to seduce you.”

“Now you know how I feel.”

“I don't know about that, you can't have felt quite as stupid as I do right now.”

“I felt a lot of things.” Her fingers catch his to entwine them, “have you really forgiven me?”

“I think my anger died while I was worried you were.”

“You were there weren't you?”

He frowns trying to grasp her meaning.

“By my bed,” she clarifies, “before I woke up. I wasn’t sure after. Thought maybe I’d hallucinated it. Or that maybe you only watched because you weren’t sure I wasn’t faking until you saw what improvement in me looked like.”

“Nathalie no. Adrien and I kept constant vigil until we were sure you’d survive. I just...”

“Didn’t know what to say once you knew I would.”

“It was only after that I realised,” or was reminded, “that I did know your feelings. That you’d acted out of love for both of us.”

“I’d be embarrassed I told you that if it wasn’t that, well, I don't think you'd be here otherwise.”

He smirks, “you’re glad I’m here.”

“Of course I am. I thought you despised me Gabriel.”

“I could never despise you. I’m a little hurt that you didn't tell me the truth but...”

“But,”

“But you wanted to tell me didn't you? When you admitted things you only could because I didn’t know who you were you always seemed,” he struggles to explain it, what he’d felt is hard to translate to words, “I don't know. A little less like you were balancing on some tightrope.”

“That's a good way of putting it. It was,” there’s a pause as if she too is unsure how to say it and yet he thinks he understands perfectly, “Freeing to be honest amongst all those lies. Even if I could only say them because of all the masks.”

He certainly does understand that. Shadow Moth had pursued Panthera when Gabriel had been unable to even accept he wanted to pursue Nathalie.

And there’s another thing he gets on an instinctual level.

“You know, it would be a little hypocritical of me to blame you for lying to someone you loved to protect them while you thought you were doing the right thing.”

The corners of her mouth turn up a little at that, and Gabriel wants to spark that glimmer of amusement in her eyes everyday.

“But I do think,” he continues, her love for him giving him confidence, “that if you’re going to come back to the house in your new role then we’re going to have to be promise to be honest with each other. No more going behind my back. And I’ll have to try to make it so you don’t need to.”

Nathalie’s eyes narrow. “A new role?”

“Well you can't very well come back as my assistant now.”

“I suppose not.” But her lips press together.

She has always hated being unable to do anything. Gabriel can only hope he can offer enough to make up for that. He likes to think he can. He’s almost caught her.

“The title would be up to you you know.”

“Hmm,” her consideration is clear, “really?”

“Girlfriend, lover, partner. Whatever you think is best.”

“Oh.” Nathalie tongue wets her lips, “I see. Can I think about that?”

“So long as you’re only thinking about the title. I can offer others you know. But if you’re thinking about whether to accept,” he does as much as a shrug he can without releasing Nathalie, “then I’m going to have to seduce you again. Without the masks this time.” “I.” Nathalie starts, “Gabriel,” stops again, “we need to talk.”

His heart drops, “What? You said,”

“I love you,” she stops him, “and I think I can believe you love me. It. It fits with everything you’re done. But I am, for a number of reasons, the reason you ended up letting Emilie go. Are you able to promise me you don’t resent me for that. That you’re not going to resent me for that? That the first time we fight as a couple you’re not going to throw that in my face?”

Chapter 23: Nathalie VIII

Her surname has never felt quite as untrue as in this moment when Gabriel looks up at her with his certainty shattered by her words, and indecision clashing in his eyes like the beginning of lightning in a storm cloud.

Inside her chest her heart beats so hard it feels like it wants to break out of her and she’s not sure if it’s for herself or for Gabriel that she feels this pain.

If he can say yes, then she’s exonerated. She has everything she’s ever wanted. A future with a Gabriel that loves her where she can attempt to rebuild things with an Adrien who seems open to that. None of her fears of everything being beyond fixing will have been true, they can move on from this, bruised and aware of their mistakes but towards a better life.

If he can’t then that mirage of happiness that’s been dangled in front of her is going to be ripped away again.

Which has already happened once.

But it would be worse this time. Worse when Gabriel knows the truth and is still proposing this because she’d known, even in her happiest moments with him before she was unmasked, she’d known that it could fall apart on him discovering the truth.

But she fears for Gabriel too. For a man so many people think cold he’s such an expressive face and it’s clear she’s hurt him. He’s put himself out there, opened up his soft inside to her, only for her to rebuff him.

For his own sake as well as hers but that doesn’t make what she’s asked of him any easier. Not with how it presses on all those tender spots he’d rather avoid: Emilie’s loss, losing her, admitting things his instincts are to hide.

His lower lip sticks out below his upper and he looks like nothing so much as a child whose teddy bear’s been taking away and Nathalie can’t help it. She frees her hand from his and pulls him closer to her.

Gabriel doesn’t fight her, falling between her legs and letting his head rest against her chest.

Nathalie runs her hand over his back, “if you can't answer now that's ok you know. We could still try being friends.”

“Friends?” He croaks against her, and something in her twists in sympathy with the brokenness in his voice, but his cheek moves up against her almost as if his mouth is turning up, “my dear Nathalie, do you think we’re really capable of being friends?”

“I-“ it’s tricky to imagine yes, their working relationship had transmuted into something else, something where loyalty went both ways and wasn’t motivated by money but no, that never had been friendship. Not on her side anyway as she’d slowly realised how far she’d go for Gabriel Agreste, and then realised why she would; that she’d lost her heart to him.

And perhaps it hadn’t been on his side either. Perhaps the Gabriel who had leapt to woo her behind a mask armed with an excuse, the man who would come to her bedside seeking consolation, who shied away from physical affection except for when he forced himself with those whose approval he needed but who sought it from her. Perhaps it had never been friendship when things had shifted for him either.

It should have been apparent earlier. What she and he had, it wasn’t the measured wary respect she saw between him and Tomoe Tsurugi, the only person she could maybe call a friend of his. Neither Audrey nor André with his pretended obsequiousness in public and derision in private would qualify. The closest she could think of otherwise is that he had people he preferred to work with.

Unhealthy perhaps but then Nathalie isn’t much better. Depressing as it is Plagg of all people might be the closest thing she has to a confidante. She deeply needs to fix that because now it’s not going to accidentally lead to the end of the world he’s quite capable of spilling something to Adrien if he thinks it funny.

She inhales and tries, “not living together, but if we’re not and I’m working elsewhere and maybe we don’t meet up too much and-“

“Nathalie,” Gabriel interrupts, “that’s not friends either. That’s acquaintances.”

“Maybe,” she lets her head droop so it lies on top of his, “but if you can’t answer…”

“I thought you said I had time to answer.”

“I did,”

His arms squeeze tighter around her, “it’ll be alright.”

“Will it?” She hates the damned quiver in her voice.

“You question…oh it was well chosen Nathalie. I don’t know that I can’t say I’d have said that in a fit of anger. I’d like to say we won’t fight but I deeply loved Emilie,”

“I know,” it’s an odd mix of guilt and jealously and guilt for being jealous that presses against the back of her tongue.

“And oh, the arguments we used to have. She used to disappear with Adrien for days afterwards.”

The world turns upside down at her words, “I didn’t know that.”

“We didn’t do it in public. And I suppose by the time you moved in she was too ill to have the energy. And even if I was angrier at her than ever, I couldn’t take it out on her. Not in that state.” Suddenly his chest hitches against her, “but I did that to you didn’t I? No wonder you don’t want to come home to me.”

“It’s not the same. You thought you couldn’t trust me. I mean you couldn’t trust me but, you,”

“Could trust you to have my best interests in mind. I should have known, it’s what you’d do as Mayura isn’t it? Lie to take that Miraculous and go behind my back because you were trying to give me what I wanted.”

“It looked rather worse than that.”

“Don’t try to make excuses for me darling. But I meant it. No more lies for us alright?”

“That might be a little hard for us of all people.” Lies are probably the only thing saving the two of them from mob violence right at this moment. As to what Gabriel has told Adrien of the truth- she has her doubts about that including the entirety of everything.

“No lies between us,” Gabriel shifts her head off his so he can turn and look her in the eyes, taking her face in his hands.

It must be a remarkably uncomfortable position for him but it’s hard to think caught in the full force of his gaze like this.

“Us?”

“I meant it. Maybe I would say it to hurt you but I promise I will never ever mean that. You’re not why I can’t have Emilie back. I can’t have Emilie back because she’s dead and there’s no way to save her. The Miraculous I thought could give me everything could only give me some of it and that’s not a bargain I’m willing to take. You’re not why I don’t have Emilie. You’ve why I still have Adrien. And I want you to be why I still have someone to love. Be mine Nathalie.”

“You must be hurting your back like that,” her mouth says while her brain completely shuts down at what her ears have just heard and her eyes have seen.

Gabriel’s brow furrows, “what?”

She falls back onto the bed, and Gabriel wholly unprepared follows her down, though he must manage to brace himself because though he’s enjoyably solid and heavy against her she’s not crushed in the slightest.

“I’d be remiss if I didn’t take care of you.” She explains, resting her arm on his back.

His jaw drops open, and then the widest smile she’s even seen on his face splits across it, “that’s a yes then.”

“Yes,” she finally manages to say properly.

“Good.”

He’s so so close and yet it seems like it takes an eternity for their mouths to meet.

It’s the same as when Monarch kissed Panthera and it’s totally different. He’s just as warm against her as he was then but there’s no masks separating them, no gloves on their hands, it’s skin against skin and her hands tangle in his hair inside of closing on his cowl.

Gabriel’s breathing heavily when they let the smallest bit of space creep in between them, “you know there’s room for change with that title I’m offering you know. Promotion even, if you think of it like that.”

“I guessed that.” She nuzzles back against him and kisses him again.

Untraditional as their courtship might have been Gabriel is. She’s known where the relationship he was offering was going in his head the moment he was offering it.

That’s why she had to be sure he was ready to offer it.

But right now, with the world condensed down to him and her, and his hands and his lips, it’s very easy to believe he is.

Nathalie’s woken by a tickling sensation to the back of her neck, as if butterfly wings are brushing against it. It takes her sleepy brain time to make sense of it and the weight on top of her before her looks down at the arm around her and the very familiar silver ring on it.

In the meantime Gabriel presses more kisses to her neck. She pushes up against his arm so she can roll over coming face to face with him.

“How are you awake before me?”

Nathalie always wakes first. Until her illness she’d been an early riser by nature and it was her job to make sure the Agrestes were ready for the day after all.

“I had the most horrifically vivid dream about Adrien coming as Chat Noir to come check I hadn’t scared you off again and peering in the window.”

“Sounds like a nightmare.”

“It would certainly be embarrassing. It’s bad enough he knows Panthera and Shadow Moth are us.”

“I don’t think anything we got photographed doing was bad enough that Adrien wouldn’t be alright with it now he knows it’s you and not a supervillain who’s stolen me away.”

“You’re very important to my son you know.” He nuzzles against her.

“I know. He told me.” She looks past Gabriel at the window, “you’re sure Adrien wouldn’t actually do that?”

“Unfortunately we both know he is given to sneaking out as a superhero. But no, I told him this was something I needed to do myself. I thought about what asking him to borrow his Miraculous to come visit you but,”

“You what?!”

She can only hope her shriek didn’t wake anyone in a surrounding room. It turns out the simultaneous fact of trying to imagine him daring to request that of his son, and the rather arresting image it creates should impossibly Adrien have agreed makes it hard to remember they’re not in the mansion but somewhere their neighbours can actually hear them.

“I did have to get in here somehow. Shadow Moth could have taken the window but as you know I don't have those Miraculous anymore.”

“Adrien would have thought you'd be going back on your word and next thing you’d be attacking Ladybug with his Miraculous.”

“I didn’t actually ask him,” Gabriel bristles but then he hugs her a little tighter, “I've lost all my son’s respect for me haven’t I?”

“Not forever. You can earn it back you know,”

“He’s my son, he’s supposed to,” Gabriel’s voice gives out in his frustration.

“And we’re disappointed him.”

“He’s forgiven you.”

“Hmm. He feels sorry for me, and he thought I might die. That’s not quite the same thing. If he does still respect me it’s only for not giving you his Miraculous or stealing Ladybug’s from hers.”

“So you did consider that?”

“Of course. Before I knew about the Wish. Adrien would have hated me for it but if I thought I could have got away with it. Then, yes I would have.”

“A good thing you didn’t then.”

Nathalie tries not to put too much weight in his words, she’s can’t assume he’s thankful she deceived him even if he’s logically, and apparently emotionally, willing to forgive her. His ongoing focus on honesty suggests otherwise.

Whether to distract him or just because it’s still such a novel sight she pushes back the strands of his hair falling onto his forehead.

“Knowing our luck paparazzi would have caught you climbing in the window or cataclysming it or whatever you were planning.”

He smirks, “Chat Noir’s father coming to steal his woman back from Shadow Moth?” She almost giggles at that image, but then perhaps in the hard-to-believe euphoria she’s in now it’s expected she’d be so like herself, “I’d like to see that. You fighting yourself.”

“Would you now?”

“Hmm,” she can’t quite meet his eyes, “hot as the image as you as, hmm, what would it have been M. Panther? Or M. Noir? Cat Father?”

“Those are all awful, though I’m glad you think it would carry it off.”

Warmth floods her core as she imagines Gabriel in a more masculine version of her own outfit as Panthera, probably with a cowl given him, and it’s hard to associate him with green but, “black’s always been your colour.”

“Do you think so?”

“I think most people would agree with me.”

“Maybe I should make some updates to my day to day wardrobe then. After all-“ he suddenly cut off and a shadow crossed his face.

It hit Nathalie a moment later; Gabriel could well be expecting to wear a lot of black in the near future as he officially said goodbye to Emilie.

Clearly attraction made her stupid. Maybe this is how Gabriel had felt when he’d been flirting with her as Panthera. She’d have to apologise for judging him for that if things kept up like this.

“Gabriel?”

“Shadow Noir,” he declares confusing her.

“What?”

“That's what I've have called myself with Adrien’s Miraculous.”

“I see,” Nathalie says slightly uneasily, “doesn’t that suggest some continuity with you as Shadow Moth though? And you wouldn’t the Butterfly or the Peacock. Unless you were really planning to scare the kids.”

“The continuity is in me.”

She tries not to raise an eyebrow. “If you say so sir.”

A slight crease forms between his eyebrows. “You know you’re going to have to stop calling me that.”

“Force of habit.”

“Hmm, you know I’m not blind to the fact you only use it instead of my name when you're annoyed with me, or making fun of me.”

“I hadn't noticed.”

“Sorry to steal a weapon from your arsenal love.”

Nathalie’s stomach does a stupid flippy thing at that finl word, “and yet you seem to be gaining them. That doesn't seem very fair.”

“Do you like me calling you my love?”

She kisses him. It’s easier than replying with words. Gabriel pulls her tight against him as she does turning what had been meant to be a quick peck into something far deeper.

As she gasps against his lips she manages, “Good answer?”

“Very good answer.”

Despite the fact that she was very very comfortable in her current position and honestly didn’t want to leave this bed anytime in the near future herself something, perhaps the lingering terrifying image of Chat Noir looking through the window, allowed her to force herself to be sensible and say, “We can’t do this all day you know.”

“Why not?”

His attempt to sound unbothered is undercut by the whining that creeps into her voice and at that she can't quite hold back her smile. It’s going to take her a while to adjust to being the one Gabriel wants as herself.

“For a start you did tell your son you were coming after me. I don't think it's a good idea for us to keep him waiting. He'll think he’s messed it up.”

Gabriel groans and rolls back onto his back. “He will, won’t he?”

“Hey. You didn’t. And we’ll be able to show him that, and we’ve time. We can fix things with him.”

“I hope we do have time.” He mutters.

Nathalie shuts her eyes in an attempt to stave off the headache building up. She’d almost thought she was out of all of this but if she’s going back with Gabriel then, “We probably should talk to Ladybug yes. I’ve been waiting for the axe to finally hit my neck for long enough now. It’d be nice to live without that.”

Gabriel’s face takes on that familiar mournful cast it has, “Was I the axe?”

“Anybody discovering it all could have been. I don’t think Ladybug would have taken too well to know that her new ally was Mayura. She was suspicious enough of me as it was.” Despite the cautious acceptance she’d received from the heroine in the end, even for her actions with Shadow Moth, she can’t help but feel like Ladybug would have leapt at the chance to expose her as the fraud she’s initially thought her and to swoop in and save Chat Noir.

At least the latter impulse is helpful to them even if Nathalie has no plans to let Adrien need saving anytime soon.

“I saw some of that.” Gabriel sounds amused again.

“I know.” And she can’t help it, “I did run into you after you know.”

She forces herself to get out of the bed hoping it’ll force Gabriel to do the same, but instead the infuriating man just watches her the whole way to the bathroom with a fondness that means she can’t even be properly angry at him.

Gabriel scowls at his hair as he attempts to brush it back into place in front of the mirror. “I don’t understand how you keep your bun in place with this hairspray.”

She shakes her head at his silliness, “hairpins mostly. And I’m not exactly going for height like you are.”

He rolls his eyes, “I’d have been able to skip this part until I got home with a Miraculous.”

Nathalie delays putting on her lipstick to point out, “apart from the whole thing of making Adrien worry that you hadn’t really given up, given what happened with your coat I’m not sure you should be trusted with any Miraculous if you don’t want to embarrass yourself more.”

Turning from the mirror to look, Gabriel raises his eyebrows, “Tempting as trapping you with any tail would have been I don’t think that’s particularly cat-like so I’m sure it would have been fine. Unless you think I was going to just pounce on you.”

She pretends to think about it, the memories of him holding her feeling almost physical against her body, “you almost did that as Shadow Moth and you had no excuse then.”

“And I seem to remember you liking it. Maybe that would have been a quicker way to resolve this than all of this talk.”

Nathalie turns and leans up to press a quick kiss to his lips, to remove that pout of his.

“Sometimes talking about our feelings is necessary Gabriel however much you hate it.”

“Is that hypocrisy I hear?”

It’s a fair point. And yet, “I don’t know. I like how yesterday’s conversation turned out. Didn’t you?”

“You’ve always been good at making convincing arguments.”

It would be nice to the take the compliment but there’s been a niggling pain behind her tooth since earlier in their conversation and softening the blow by resting her cheek against his shoulder and wrapping her arms around him she says, “you know you can talk to me about her you know?”

“What?”

“Earlier. When the subject of,” Emilie but her tongue feels heavy and awkward in her mouth and she can’t form those syllables when neither she nor Gabriel have spoken his wife’s name yet, and she fumbles and manages “funeral attire came up. You deflected it, and I don’t want that to be because of me. I don’t want things to be awkward. I helped you terrorise this city for her, you don’t have to pretend that she doesn’t exist. You don’t have to pretend you’re not grieving.”

She can feels the movement in his chest as he breathes in.

“What if I’m not?”

“What?”

“It feels like for so long I’ve been frozen in place, unable to move forward, lost in some pervasive darkness so deep I couldn’t feel anything at all, except for anger on occasion. And now…now it’s like the sun’s broken through the clouds Nathalie. I feel happy here with you. And I want to feel happy. That must sound terrible.”

“I don’t think so. I get it, this is a new start isn’t it? And we’re been trying to save her so long,”

Nathalie gets it in an almost instinctual level. It hadn’t been her reaction to discovering she couldn’t save Emilie, but then she’d still had to find a way to get Gabriel and Adrien out of it all mostly in one piece and without them hating each other.

But after that, even thinking Gabriel had rejected her definitively, having it all out in the open had been, “It’s almost a relief to know we can’t?”

“Yeah.”

“I will be. Later. Upset I mean. Adrien and I will have to say goodbye but for now I’ve sick of being unhappy.”

“You don’t have to be.” Her words carry the weight of a promise, but for once it’s a promise that doesn’t feel like thorns growing round her throat when she makes it. It’s not one she can make really, its Gabriel’s choice not hers, but she thinks this is one aim of his that she won’t regret helping him achieve.

Chapter 24: Plagg II

For how long he’d lived Plagg has known less humans than might be expected. Still he’s known enough that he doesn’t need Duusu or Nooroo’s skills to recognise the body language between his back-up holder and her disaster of a man.

After all a cat can definitely tell when another one has the cream.

And from how she comfortably allows the arm around her waist as she leaves the car Nathalie is quite satisfied indeed. Never mind the glow to her complexion. Though admittedly that last bit might just finally be finally seeing her when she’s healthy.

“Told ya kid.”

Adrien rushes to the window, “Is Father back? Does he have Nathalie?”

The boy gets his own answer as he sees them down below him and at the bright sunshine that spreads across his face Plagg can’t feel bad about anything he’d held back from anyone to get here.

Not, he muses, as he follows his holder down the stairs that he has any reason to do so. It’s hardly as if he’d had much choice in it with everything that binds him as a kwami and besides he did manage to bring Shadow Moth’s activities to an end. If maybe not in the way his boy and Tikki’s girl had exactly dreamed about. Or the way Tikki might have either.

But as much as he’d take great glee in tearing Gabriel Agreste apart atom from atom it would upset Adrien and Nathalie so really this is a better ending than the one in which the heroes unmasked him in front of the entire city.

And now he’s sorted all the adults all he has to do is get the kids sorted out too, and then he’ll be able to lie back and enjoy fine cheeses, paid for by Gabriel’s money, and no longer have to play emotional support like he’s Nooroo or someone more suited to that sort of thing.

No Plagg can be quite happy in that he’s made his people happy, and if that also happens to mean his favourite wielder in…well pretty much forever really, now has an increased chance of not finding his own destruction the way he’s seen before?

Well then. Plagg, saviour of the city of Paris, is winning on every count really.

And as for the city’s former villain?

Much as he doesn’t want to admit it Adrien’s father is not in fact the worst man Plagg’s met. Not that he’s ever telling him that. But if with a few hints about destroying computers and such Plagg can manage to nudge him to look after Adrien the way he should have instead of ruining his life and leading him to a possible early death then he thinks they can find a way to co-exist.

Especially as Adrien runs up to Nathalie to throw his arms around her, and the tension in his grip makes it clear just how afraid his boy had been of losing her.

“I’m so glad you’re back,” Adrien’s little whimper is barely audible even with Plagg’s better than human hearing.

“I couldn’t leave my kitten behind,” she murmurs as she hugs him back, “who knows what sort of trouble you might get into without me?”

The softness in face of the man Plagg’s two wielders both inexplicably love as he watches his son hug his woman is further evidence. Pity Nooroo and Duusu aren’t here to see that. They’d have liked it probably. Maybe Adrien can send them a photo or something.

He doesn’t really look like Shadow Moth at all as he nods at Plagg in wary recognition and, he likes to think, thanks.

Adrien finally seems to remember Nathalie needs to breathe and lets go of her, “So are you staying? This isn’t just you coming back to say goodbye properly?”

“No I’m staying,” she looks to her side, “so long as I’m wanted.”

“You know you’re wanted,” Gabriel says gruffly though Plagg could almost forgive me the awkwardness given the little smile tugging at Nathalie’s lips as he said it.

Even when plotting this happy ending, even when he’d started to care about it being happy for her and not just Adrien he hadn’t realised she care look like that. Look so unburdened.

A smirks pulls at the corner of Adrien’s lips, and he’s a little proud of his boy as he says, “so is Nathalie back as your assistant then?”

“Ah no.” Nathalie gives an apologetic shrug to her apparently former boss, “I’m not sure that would be a good idea.”

“It probably wouldn’t be, although I’m hardly known for my good ideas,” he agrees, turning his attention to his son, “no Adrien Nathalie’s going to be living with us as my partner.” Then he amends, “My romantic partner.”

The last amendment seems pretty unnecessary to Plagg. Humans are always coming up with new titles for things but he thinks it’s pretty clear exactly what’s going on between the two adults.

“Which is only happening because your father and I have known each other a long time and I’ve already been living with you two and is not something you should emulate on starting a realtionship.”

Adrien rubs at the back of his neck. “OK?”

“I’d hope Adrien’s a bit young for that to be necessary to say.” Adrien’s father says with a grimace.

Plagg personally thinks moving in with Ladybug would send his holder over the moon but as far as he can tell from all his boy’s friends it does seem like it’s not the done thing for a boy of his age so Plagg will just have to focus on giving Adrien the easiest home life he can with the least about of effort.

Namely generally bugging Nathalie about it when he gives her his cheese order for the week.

Oh it’s good to have a woman like that to sort things out for him.

Thank goodness he’d chosen someone whose family was capable of providing for his needs. More proof that Adrien was the perfect wielder for him that he should point out to Tikki’s girl.

“Hmm.” Nathalie’s doing that little smile again, “We’ve not exactly been great role models to Adrien when it comes to love. That’s probably something we should fix.”

Plagg waits for the Adrien to point out that they haven’t exactly been good role models in anything but apparently despite how his father’s been knocked off his pedestal he’s not quite ready to talk back like that.

Though admittedly he might have used a good bunch of that confidence up when he’d tried to tell his father about Nathalie’s feelings for Shadow Moth.

Oh the things a kwami has to do himself, “or in anything really.”

Nathalie’s ‘romantic partner’ exhales harshly through his nostrils and Plagg once again questions her taste.

“We gave up didn’t we?” He bites up, “perhaps at least Adrien can learn from us when to recognise futility. And perhaps that it’s better to just admit your feelings for someone.”

OK Plagg will admit that last bit wasn’t bad.

“You know I like Ladybug.” Adrien looks between everyone confusedly, “that’s not really the problem there.”

Nathalie at least looks a little abashed at that, which yeah, she probably should do even if she’s not the one of Adrien’s parents baring the most guilt here really.

“I don’t know,” she has that thoughtful frown on her face, “Ladybug might be hesitant to have anything too much to do with you given she’d not want your father and I to know who she is. But whoever Ladybug is beneath that mask might not have that issue.”

“Because you think she wouldn’t still worry about that?”

Nathalie concedes, “she probably would. But it’s not like we’d know if you were dating her or moving on with another girl. You have liked other girls you know.”

“I guess,” Adrien looks unsure.

Plagg tries not to roll his eyes at Mr Fail Father’s “He has?”

“I told you. Tomoe’s daughter.”

“Ah. Anyway I very much plan to remain in denial about the possibility of having Ladybug as a daughter-in-law. Preferably forever. You can tell her that by the way.”

Red takes over Adrien’s cheeks, “Father.”

“Like that’s not your plan,” Plagg reminds Adrien only slightly in revenge for the number of times he’s heard it. It’s a lot.

“Plagg,” his Adrien chimes in an almost identical tone.

“Anyway.” Nathalie straightens up and her shoulders move back in what he’s recognised is her getting back to business face, “we do need to talk to Ladybug.”

“Yes,” a shadow crossing his face makes it much easier to see as the man who’d terrorised this town, “We do. Adrien it’s important you get this set up quickly. Nathalie’s been feeling odd since Ladybug healed her, and,”

The cheese he’d eaten earlier seems to curdle further inside him. It had been bad enough when his own magic had failed Nathalie when she was holding his Miraculous, but it’s somehow impossibly worse to hear that Tikki’s might have.

No it’s not his fault this time but Plagg’s used to things going wrong when he uses his power. There’s a reason he doesn’t do it anymore. Tikki’s power is supposed to be different, to full all the holes in his lack, to create rather than destroy.

If Nathalie’s still ill after that then maybe healing is impossible for her.

Maybe this is just a ceasefire in the battle for a better life Adrien seems to have been born into, a temporary oasis in this desert, and not a real happy-ending.

Adrien’s gone pale as milk, and frankly looks as bad as Plagg feels.

“Nathalie’s not ok?”

“I’m fine.” Nathalie scowls at Gabriel.

“Are you though?” Plagg asks.

“What?”

“Just saying Lady, you don’t exactly have a good history being honest about this sort of thing.”

“I was honest enough with you wasn’t I? And you know why I had to keep what I did from Adrien and Gabriel.”

The latter takes her hand and Plagg watches his thumb rub over hers “you always did have a tendency to claim you were fine when you weren’t even before Adrien gave you that ring my dear Nathalie.”

She purses her lips, but her eyes aren’t angry at all, and then says, “that’s because I didn’t want to leave you dealing with all this alone Gabriel. I wasn’t being sentenced to my bed until I had to be. And,” she looked away from her lover and at Plagg and Adrien, “I’ll have you both know that I’m absolutely fine. This is nothing like before. I’m just a little restless that’s all. And as it happens I do plan to bring it up with Ladybug.”

Plagg’s about to ask her if she’s sure about that when Adrien deploys better pleading kitten eyes than he could ever manage, and with a pout says, “do you promise?”

“Yes.” Nathalie agrees. “I do. But that’s not why we need to talk to Ladybug.”

Once again Plagg and his holder react in sync, this time with a frown.

“Why do you need to talk to Ladybug?”

“Well I suppose maybe we could just talk to you if she’s told you, but Adrien, what does Ladybug want from us?”

“Want from you?”

“She healed Nathalie.” Gabriel says, “I exchanged both Miraculous for that. But given the circumstances of that being necessary,”

That, Plagg thought, was certainly one of saying that he’d scared her to almost death by threatening to lock her away forever, and then being ready to attack his own son such that she’d had to intervene before he’d do something permanent that he’d regret once his bloodlust went down.

“I believe the previous agreement Nathalie brokered on my behalf is probably null and void. Especially now that she’s aware Nathalie also misled her.”

“I…don’t think she blames Nathalie?” Adrien looks fidgety, “I mean we spoke a bit. More when Nathalie was still ill and Ladybug was checking on her but I uh, I think she gets why Nathalie did what she did and she understands it. I mean she gets why you did what you did but uh,”

“She understands it less.” Ladybug’s former nemesis’ answer is stiff.

“Yeah?” Adrien offers.

“Tikki’s chosen isn’t the type of hurt others. Now she’s plenty capable of doing it by accident,” how she’d treated Adrien on occasion still stung, “and she’s not without her own violent streak,” he did think it was a pity she’d never really got her chance to beat Shadow Moth up, “but she what stops her from being like you is that she couldn’t deal with the collateral damage to innocents you don’t care about.”

Nathalie gets something of a pass from the girl for not stealing both Miraculous Shadow Moth had held and putting a stop to it all firstly because Marinette understands being in love, and secondly because, quite apart from causing it all to stop in the end, she hadn’t actually been the one directly causing it in the first place. Shadow Moth would still have sent out his akumas if Adrien had given the ring to someone else.

“Temporary collateral damage,” Shadow Moth appears below the veneer of Gabriel Agreste in his shrug.

“Father,” Adrien hisses.

“Maybe don’t say that to Ladybug,” Nathalie says dryly.

“What? It’s not like…” he seems to belatedly register Adrien’s glare.

“We didn’t leave physical damage but we both know the worst scars aren’t always physical,” Nathalie says. Then she looks at Plagg, “you’re been speaking to Tikki then?”

“Someone had to. Kids a mess talking to his Lady,”

“Plagg,”

“And you were dying and then recovering and then you ran away. And that one hid in his office feeling sorry for himself.”

Said man glares at that. “Sometimes I think you only want cheap cheese.”

Plagg flies up to his face, “don’t you dare. Do you want me to set Tikki against her. Because her holder listens to her the way you didn’t to Nooroo.”

Gabirel’s jaw sets, “Fine. But just imagine a world without Tikki and then tell me if you’d be up to all this stuff you apparently think I should have been doing instead.”

A glower tags over his face and he can feel the power thrumming below his skin at the idea of having Tikki taken away from him, at the idea of being so out of balance, at the loss of his other half, at the idea of someone taking him from her.

He forgets for a moment where he is as darkness around him and-

Nathalie’s clear calm voice cuts through it, “he’s not threatening you Plagg.”

“Just asking for understanding,” the man who he has to remind himself that both Nathalie and Adrien love and that they would be very unhappy were something to happen to him.

“Never do that to me again,” he snarls at Gabriel Agreste.

“It’s ok Plagg,” Adrien calls him back and Plagg flies over to snuggle in his hair and settle himself down against the main reason apart from cheese that he knows it’s a good thing he doesn’t destroy this world.

“Just reminding you that things haven’t been easy for me.” Gabriel says.

“Doesn’t mean you did right by Adrien.”

“No,” the word is dragged out of him by force, but it nonetheless comes, “I’m aware of that.”

Adrien looks up, and that that look of hope on his face is exactly why Plagg dislikes his father, “you do?”

“My god Adrien do you think that if I’d known I couldn’t fix everything with magic I’d have become Hawk Moth for fun?”

“Well no but…”

Nathalie grimaces, “I’m not sure it’s entirely Hawk Moth he’s asking about Gabriel.”

It seems the perfect time to interject, “I disliked you even before I knew you were the Moth Man you know.”

“Please don’t call me that.”

“Bite me.”

“Plagg,” Adrien’s voice has the slightest shade of reprimand in it.

Adrien’s father looks down, “I do realise I’ve been distant since your mother died Adrien. I… would have made an attempt to be less so had I not thought I was going to be able to bring her back.”

Plagg wonders if he’s noticed how his language has changed over time as he’s been prodding at him in the Atelier. From “lost” and “sleeping” to “died” and from “save” to “bring her back” and “resurrect”

He’ll give him enough understanding given that he did eventually turn it around and given Plagg has managed to bring Gabriel to a breakdown once, rather to his satisfaction, he do him a favour and won’t bring it up now.

Especially in front of Adrien as he hardly wants to upset the boy further.

And now he thinks about it discussing her predecessor might upset Nathalie too.

So he’ll hold off for now.

Especially as Adrien seems to finally be showing claws of his own with a surprisingly sceptical, “You would?”

“I wouldn’t have turned into your mother, I know she was always the one of us who looked after you, but yes I’d have made an effort.”

“Are you going to make an effort now?”

“I plan to.”

“Assuming he can.” Nathalie cuts in.

The two Agrestes look at her, having apparently forgotten they’re not alone. Plagg isn’t offended. He’s used to that with his cats.

Adrien’s forehead creases, “what do you mean?”

“We got a little off topic,” her attempted lightness is a little strained, “but we do need to talk to Ladybug. Neither of us want to live with an axe above our necks.”

“My lady isn’t like that!”

The two adults and Plagg all wince a little at Adrien’s fervent defence of Ladybug.

He assumes not all their reasons are the same.

Much as he’d love the kids to get over their issues and get together personally he can’t help but wish Ladybug valued his chosen the way Adrien values Tikki’s.

She’ll get there. Probably.

He just wishes it didn’t hurt Adrien while she was doing so.

Eventually Nathalie speaks, “we’re not saying she is. But she doesn’t seem the type to be happy with just letting us go either. So since she knows our identities, and we could probably start to piece together more of hers,”

Adrien’s eyes go wide at that.

“I think it’s probably a good plan we talk to her and sort things out.” She finishes.

Chapter 25: Gabriel IX

Gabriel finds himself staring up at Ladybug. It’s an unusual position for him. He probably hasn’t sat below someone like this for what, a decade? Almost two?

Whichever, having this chit of a girl stand over him ready to define his destiny while he sits down awkwardly on his son’s couch makes his skin ready to break out in hives. Or it would if Nathalie wasn’t sat next to him curled into her side with her head rested on his shoulder.

She’s very…grounding is the best word he can offer for how Nathalie makes him feel. It’s easier to do what has to be done with her by his side. It always hard been. In his life as Hawk Moth and as Gabriel Agreste. And now when it comes to trying to create the relationship with his son he’s never had, and being ready to face the superheroine who holds all their fates in their lives.

Ladybug crosses her arms in front of her. “Adrien said you wanted to talk to me.”

“Yes,” it comes out stiffly but he doubts Adrien and Nathalie at least expect better from him, “about the future and about Nathalie’s future health?”

The girl gasps.

Internally Gabriel groans how did he lose to a child who wears her emotions on her face?

How did he never akumatise her?

Yes, he’s glad he didn’t win but it’s still offensive to his soul.

Ladybug’s eyes are wide,“Nathalie’s still ill?”

Nathalie sighs and sits up a little straighter, “I don’t know.”

Ladybug frowns and he sees her looking for signs of sickness in his lover. Gabriel himself has struggled to catch anything that would be obvious to see but he can’t deny that Ladybug is clever, and that she has access to knowledge about the Miraculous they don’t.

And he almost hopes she can see something because he has noticed other things. The restlessness Nathalie had alluded to. Which could just be a reaction to the strange purgatory they’re been in while they try to find a way out of this stalemate, and how she’s without a purpose to devote herself to without their quest and her job as his assistant.

But he doesn’t think that’s it. Not with how she’s sleeping less hours than she ever has, to the extent even a night owl like him struggles to match her, yet shows no sign of it either in her appearance or in her energy levels during the day. Nor with the fidgetiness she’s suddenly developed.

“What do you mean?” Ladybug sounds more interested than anything else now, that creative mind of hers gearing up to solve a puzzle. Gabriel can only hope she’s as good as solving this as she was at intuited the meanings of her Lucky Charms, “does it vary by day? Or is at that sort of level where it’s annoying but you put it off because you have other things to do?”

“You sound like you might be familiar with that,” Nathalie sounds amused, and faintly chiding as she looks over the heroine again as if wondering what secret she might be hiding.

Gabriel himself finds himself amused at how despite their circumstances, and despite how she’s lectured him on not taking back to Ladybug, Nathalie still finds herself taking this somewhat disapproving stance on the girl. Or rather that Ladybug is a girl and not a woman.

Not that…

Well let’s just said he’s rather glad he surrendered and won’t have to deal with the fact he has permanently beaten by the teenage fangirl of his son. Yes, Ladybug and Chat Noir had always won but they’d also never become any closer to unmasking him either.

Until his son had accidentally stumbled onto his identity all because he’d jumped to thinking the worse of Nathalie.

He’s lucky she took him back he knows. Lucky Adrien had intervened. If he’d kept her up in the lair he doubts very much things would have turned out like this.

No.

Since then he’s been making the right choices. He has to believe that. And it’s not so difficult when he’s with Adrien and Nathalie and everything seems to be going right.

It’s just that it doesn’t always go right.

His and Adrien’s relationship is like a set of puzzle pieces they’re trying to assemble and frankly Gabriel’s not sure they ever had some of those pieces. Emilie and then Nathalie had always handled the bulk of the childcare.

And Nathalie. Even Nathalie has those frowns on her face at times that she tries to hide from him.

But that’s why he has to work to make sure things do go right.

And that includes listening to Ladybug.

For whom it sounds like surprisingly enough not everything goes right for either.

“It’s pretty busy being Ladybug and handling my real life.”

“I can understand that,” Nathalie agrees though privately Gabriel doubts it could be harder for Ladybug than them running a full-on business at the same time. But then again maybe he’s being unfair. Ladybug wouldn’t have had a Nathalie. And if she worked less hours she’d have theoretically been watched for more of them.

Though if her school is like his son’s and with the amount of immature angst he tries to tune out until it’s strong enough to call to an akuma then frankly he doesn’t know what the teachers are doing.

Adrien looks like he wants to move to the girl, “but you’re ok?”

Her smile turns soft for his son, and Gabriel can’t help but reassured by the sign her affection for Adrien is still in face. It’s the main reason she bears them any goodwill after all whatever Plagg the annoyance might claim.

“I’m fine Chaton. Just. Having one less thing to juggle is going to be a relief.”

“Right,” Adrien doesn’t sound as relieved as he’d expect, “of course.”

He forces himself to try to take on some of that concern, it’s not worse than kissing up to Audrey after all, “I didn’t mean to lay another burden on you when I brought up Nathalie’s symptoms.”

“No,” a familiar determination gleams in Ladybug’s eyes, “if what I did to heal Nathalie didn’t work I want to know. I’d feel so much worse if I only found out when it got bad and I could have done something. And you know I don’t leave things unfinished either.”

Nathalie stiffens slightly next to him.

The other reason they’d wanted to talk to Ladybug of course.

“I don’t think it didn’t work,” Nathalie says, “I mean I’m not in coma right now. I just..could it be possible you gave me too much life energy or something. I know that sounds stupid but…”

“No,” Ladybug’s chewing her lip, “my powers do come from creation. It’s possible there could be some remnants of that. Maybe, may I?”

She comes a step closer.

“Sure,” Nathalie sits forward in her chair.

Ladybug frowns, as she bends slightly to better inspect Nathalie, too short to need to kneel the same way Gabriel would in that position.

She puts a hand to Nathalie’s forehead and her frown deepens.

Gabriel’s heart drops.

But then she says, “I don’t think it’s going to be problem.”

“It’s not?”

“Keep an eye on it and let me know immediately if anything changes, but I think it should sort itself out.”

Gabriel isn’t sure how she can see that with her bare eyes. Even transformed as she is. It’s not like she’d seen the Peacock’s strain on Panthera’s soul. Though he supposes it’s different when it’s you own magic.

“Anything I can do to speed that up?”

“Create,” Ladybug suggests with a shrug, “knit, or crochet, or paint, or draw, or I don’t know do pottery or something.”

Nathalie does something too elegant to report, “a pity it wasn’t Gabriel you healed. I’ve never been creative.”

Ladybug’s nose scrunches, “it doesn’t have to be something professional level. Don’t you doodle or anything? Or uh, I don’t know maybe even something like computer coding would bleed off the energy later.”

“I guess I’d better look for a new hobby then.” Nathalie says, and he can almost hear her mind churning, hearing it consider and then dispose of various different ideas.

Ladybug looks confused, “what do you do when you’re not working then?”

Gabriel reminds himself his son is here and bites his tongue.

“I read. Look after these two. Play boardgames. Mostly chess.”

“Oh.” The poor heroine is doing her best not to look appalled and not when pulling it off.

He knows without looking Nathalie’s smirking next to him. “Oh and I like to polish my self- defence skills. I’ve got back into going to the dojo I went to before I fell ill. Sports in general really.”

“Ah.” Now she doesn’t look sure how to respond.

“And languages of course. I’m thinking of picking up another. Though my Sanskrit has got dusty. Maybe I should focus on that first.”

“Stop teasing the girl Nathalie,” Gabriel decides to magnanimously save Ladybug since Adrien doesn’t seem to have picked up that he should be playing the dashing knight saving his lady right now.

“Wait, she doesn’t do all that then?”

“No she does,” Gabriel smiles.

“Yeah,” Adrien interjects, “did you know she’s dad’s bodyguard? How cool is that?”

“I did not know that.” Ladybug looks a little shell-shocked still.

“But we’ll find her a creative outlet too I’m sure.” Gabriel reassures her.

“Who knows, maybe it’ll make me more creative,” Nathalie says.

Gabriel’s throat goes tight. He looks at the teenage girl who’s the only one who might be able to answer him, and then at his son standing by watching. The son Nathalie’s become a parent to.

But then he’s not sure he can dare not ask. “Will it? This magic of yours, does it, that is to say, will it suddenly allow Nathalie to create things she wouldn’t have been able to without it?”

They’re been taking precautions of course but neither of them had considered the matter of literal creation magic potentially nullifying them.

Ladybug looks frankly concerned for his sanity but says, “that’s not how this works. It’s like my Lucky Charms. They can show me a way but I have to be the one to understand them. This isn’t just going to make Nathalie an artist.”

Nathalie seems to get his meaning, “well we’re all thankful for that.”

The relief in her voice is obvious. Neither of them want to make things even more complicated. Not when they don’t know what shape their future might take and when honestly Adrien is quick enough to be dealing with.

“Um.”

“Anyway,” his girlfriend says, “that not why we asked you here.”

“Right.” Ladybug says, “you said you wanted to talk about the future. I’m not going to give you back your Miraculous you know. Nooroo and Duusu are both happy with the other Kwami’s.”

“And we wouldn’t ask you to do that,” Gabriel finds it easy to say. Now he knows the Miraculous can’t give him anything he wants he sees little need for them.

“So what are you asking?”

Nathalie reaches for his hand, “you and I had a deal. But that was before you knew I was Mayura.”

“And before I attacked Nathalie and Adrien instead of stopping being Shadow Moth like I’d agreed,” Gabriel adds. Because little as he likes saying it that is what he’d done.

“And before Father actually did give both Miraculous back to you,” Adrien interjects, “he did give up. And Nathalie lied to protect me, and she’s why he gave up.”

Nathalie’s head immediately turns to Adrien, “he was giving up the Wish because of you Adrien. Not me.”

“And you’re the one who told him about it.”

Gabriel represses the urge to pinch at his nose. “I couldn’t risk you Adrien. And I couldn’t risk Nathalie dying when I could save her either. The two of you don’t need to argue about who is more important to me.”

“And I’m not going back on our deal.” Ladybug says, “I told you, what I wanted was just for everything to stop. And I’m certainly not going to complain about losing two villains for the price of one. I used to worry about what Mayura be plotting in the background.”

“Sorry.” Nathalie offers.

Ladybug shrugs, “I mean apparently it was to get Shadow Moth to give up, so?”

Gabriel does not point out that technically her aim had been to save this family and had the Wish not had a price Nathalie might well have gone ahead with a plot to take Ladybug’s earrings to combine with the ring she already had.

Though he supposes Nathalie did listen to Ladybug, and not try to fight her straight away which isn’t something he can say he would have managed to do in the circumstances. He know he can be- impulsive. And that he’d hated the girl in front of him until she’d saved the woman he loved.

“And,” Ladybug continues, “it wasn’t me you attacked after your initial surrender. I think it’s the two other people in the room you should be apologising to.”

Nathalie leans a little closer to him in a reminder that she at least has forgiven him. Though he supposes he’d asked for forgiveness from her in a way he hasn’t really from Adrien.

“I’m not happy about what you did,” Adrien says, “but it’s not worse than the rest of it. Someone you love who you thought was your best friend keeping secrets from you hurts. Even if they had a good reason.”

“Adrien,” Ladybug’s voice quavers on his name.

“I mean given my father turned out to be Hawk Moth I guess you were right to be so strict about our identities.”

“I really didn’t even consider that as one of my reasons. It’s just that,” she looks down and trails off.

“I don’t think this is a conversation you want to have in front of us Ladybug,” Nathalie says, “but I think it’s a conversation you two should have. Sitting here as someone who’s spoken to both of you, I know you’re very important to Adrien and he is to you however that ends up being. Don’t let things you haven’t said keep you apart.” The way it almost had the two of them he knows she means, “And don’t let us do that either.”

Stiffly Gabriel adds, “once we’ve removed certain secrets from the house I suppose it would be possible for Adrien to have a…limited amount of friends over provided they stay away from the Atelier and don’t do anything stupid. And as I said, I’m not going to try to take your Miraculous. Trust me, I’m delighted to go back to the realms of ignorance when it comes to petty teenage drama.”

Ladybug looks between them, “are you trying to invite the me behind the mask over to spend time with Adrien?”

“We’re just informing you that Adrien’s friends will be visiting on occasion.” It seems safer than letting them frolic on the streets with who knows what Miraculous, “whoever that happens to include. Which we won’t be attempting to check.”

“If this is your apology to Adrien then it’s a start.”

“Is it?”

Ladybug’s hands go to her hips, “I know you’ve been grieving Mr Agreste. And I know you’d scared of losing Adrien too. And maybe you think you’re helping him by trying to make him perfect. But you’re not. You’re just forcing him to wear a mask all the time, because Chat Noir? He’s all parts of Adrien you don’t let out. And if you don’t give your son more freedom and actually pay attention to him apart from when it comes to disciplining him and monetising him then you’d going to lose him after all.”

Anger burns in his chest. He wants to yell at the impudent girl. To throw her out of this house.

But Nathalie’s hand is on his knee holding him back. He has to play nice. For her. And for Adrien. And for himself.

And, a little voice whispers, he’d had no idea that Adrien was Chat Noir nor of how real the connection between Adrien and Nathalie rather than some parody of the bond with the mother Adrien missed. Because Adrien does miss Emilie, just the way Gabriel grieved her but that doesn’t mean they can’t love others.

Perhaps. And he hates this. Ladybug might have a point.

All he’s achieved since Emilie died was losing his link with his son chasing a dream whose cost would likely have been the death of his son.

He hates it when the people dripping with condescending impractical morality turn out to be right.

“Is she right?” He asks his son.

Adrien doesn’t meet his eyes. But he does mumble. “She is.”

It makes him wonder what Chat Noir had told Ladybug or Adrien had told whichever friend of his is Ladybug.

“I’m giving you a chance Mr Agreste.” Ladybug says, “what you do with it is up to you. But you’ve done a lot of damage ever since you chose to use that Miraculous. Maybe you should think about doing some good.”

“Are you suggesting we should try to, what makes things up to Paris?” Nathalie asks.

“Don’t you think you should? And to Adrien of course?”

“Obviously we want to fix things with Adrien,” Nathalie says.

“And maybe therapy?”

“Therapy?” Gabriel can’t help but be perturbed. Needing therapy feels like an admission of being a failure. Or an American.

“Don’t you want to be a better father and, um, partner?”

“I suppose I do.”

“Then go and do that.” Ladybug says.

And with Nathalie there with him Gabriel thinks he might just do that.
